# Implementation coverage

What Graphite does today, how each part is verified, and what is still missing. `OBJECTIVE.md` describes the complete product; this file tracks progress toward it. Update it with every change that adds, removes, or re-verifies behavior.

Last updated: 2026-10-21.

## Scanning documents: 2026-10-21

Graphite scans paper documents with the system scanner (VisionKit) where the device can, and saves the pages as one PDF. The design is in [Architecture](Architecture.md#scanning-documents); the checklist entry is Part 4 of [the library todo](Notebook-library-and-new-file-todo.md).

- **What exists:** "Scan Document" in a note's attach menu above the keyboard, in More › Insert and in the editor's Insert context menu, next to Take Photo: the scanner, then the PDF saved as "Scan 2026-10-21 14.05.pdf" in the vault's attachment folder and embedded at the cursor. "Scan Documents" in the New menu (library and the iPad and iPhone sidebar): a new PDF notebook of that name in the folder shown, opened in a tab. Both are offered only where `VNDocumentCameraViewController.isSupported`; the simulator and the Mac show no control. Cancelling the scanner writes nothing, and so does cancelling while the PDF is made (Cancel on the overlay over a note, a tap on the spinner in the New menu). A failed scan, or a write that fails, is reported through the same alert as other save errors. A scan whose text could not be recognized is saved and the alert says it cannot be searched.
- **The PDF:** one page per scanned picture at 595 points wide and the picture's own proportions, the picture as JPEG at quality 0.8, an invisible text layer from Vision's accurate recognition with language correction over each word. A synthetic 10-page scan of A4 pages at 200 dpi (1654 by 2339 pixels, shaded paper, a shape and text) came to less than 5 MB in the test's bound.
- **Invariants touched** ([invariants.md](solid/invariants.md)): D-01 and D-19 (files are created with `.absent` under a free name; a name taken meanwhile takes the next free one), D-21 (the attachment is always kept; the embed is not inserted, with an alert, when the note closed or is in conflict), NM-03 (the name is Gregorian with two-digit parts; no new `NM-` row was added, so the document's counts are unchanged), ED-20 (image encoding off the main thread). The PDF is built on an actor and the file is written by the vault's store, never on the main actor (FR-103).
- **Automated tests (all on macOS, `swift test --filter`, none audible or windowed):** `ScannedDocumentPDFBuilderTests` (11, Apple): page count and sizes for portrait, landscape and square pictures, a picture with transparency, no pages refused, a very tall picture kept within the 14,400-point page limit, words findable with `PDFDocument.findString` at the box where they were printed (centre and width within 6 points), the text layer painting nothing (the page renders to the same pixels with and without it), a page whose recognition failed written without text while the others keep theirs, cancellation ending the build with `CancellationError`, ten photographic pages under 5 MB with `/DCTDecode` in the file, Vision recognizing a rendered page of known lines so PDFKit finds "Invoice", "4821" and "francs" (skipped through `TextRecognitionCapability` on a machine without the models; it ran here), and the word splitter. `ScannedDocumentNameTests` (4, Core): the name, the time zone, zero padding, a name the file rules allow. `DocumentScanProcessingTests` (5, UI): a finished scan is saved once, no pages is reported and saves nothing, cancelling during recognition saves nothing and reports nothing, a scan can start again, every failure has words. `WorkspaceDocumentScanningTests` (7, UI, real vault folders): the PDF attachment is saved byte for byte and embedded, the vault's attachment folder is followed, two scans in a minute keep both files, pages without text are reported after the save, the notebook lands in the chosen folder and opens with all its pages, with no folder it goes to the vault's location for new files, an unwritable folder is reported and leaves nothing. `CreatableItemTests` (2 added, 4 changed; the whole run: 33 tests of the feature, 0 failures): Scan Documents is offered only when the device can scan, and needs no plugin. Related suites run again after the change: `InfoPropertyListDeclarationsTests`, `LibraryModelTests`, `NotebookLibraryPreferencesTests`, `UiEditorMacContextMenuTests` (28 tests, no failures).
- **Compile checks:** the `Graphite` scheme builds for the iOS simulator (`generic/platform=iOS Simulator`) and for macOS (`platform=macOS`) without errors and with no warning from the new files. The hosted iPad suites were not run (no full suites, by instruction).
- **Found while testing:** a text matrix that stretches the glyphs does not stretch the glyph positions CoreText lays out, so PDFKit selected only the unstretched width of a word (45.9 of 119 points in a probe); the word's font size comes from its box height and the letters are spaced with a kern to its width, which selects 119 points.
- **Not established (needs an iPhone or iPad):** the scanner itself (`VNDocumentCameraViewController` cannot run in the simulator), the camera permission prompt, how long recognition takes for a real 10-page scan and its memory (the build holds the pages as bitmaps while it runs), whether the progress overlay and the spinner button look right, VoiceOver on the new controls, a scan made with the keyboard shown in a split view, the placement of the highlighted text in Preview and Acrobat for a photographed page with tilted lines, and tilted text (the boxes are upright). Vault-wide search does not read the text of PDFs, so the text layer is not found by Graphite's search; it serves selection, copying and Read Aloud in the PDF view and search in other PDF readers. The Mac has no scanning.

## A small core: size budget, measurements at rest, Mac icons, plugins dormant while off: 2026-10-21

The owner's direction of 2026-10-21: Graphite is a small, light core, and extras are downloads, so size, memory and CPU are measured and guarded.

- **Size budget.** `scripts/app-size-budget.py` lists an archived app's parts (whole app, binary, asset catalog and its largest assets, resource bundles) and fails when one is more than 1 % over `scripts/app-size-budget.json`. `scripts/archive-for-testflight.sh` runs it after archiving and stops before the upload unless given `--allow-size-growth`. The iOS budget was recorded from the archive of `02ffa5c`: app 87.7 MB, binary 45.5 MB, asset catalog 29.5 MB (of which 29 MB are the five app icons, 5.8 MB each: 11 pre-rendered images per Liquid Glass icon). The resource bundles: SwiftUIMath 7.4 MB, MLX 2.4 MB, GraphiteUI 1.4 MB, GraphiteApple 1.3 MB.
- **The Mac leaves out the alternate icons.** Choosing the app icon is an iPad and iPhone setting, so the Mac build no longer compiles the four alternate icons and their Settings previews (`ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_ALTERNATE_APPICON_NAMES[sdk=macosx*]` empty, `EXCLUDED_SOURCE_FILE_NAMES[sdk=macosx*]`): its asset catalog went from 10.6 MB to 2.0 MB.
- **Community plugins cost nothing while off.** Opening a vault listed its plugins every time, which reads and regular-expression-scans each plugin's main.js (megabytes each), even with plugins off. Now nothing is read until plugins are turned on for the vault or Settings › Community Plugins is opened. `CommunityPluginHostDormantTests` pins it (no inventory, no load states, no web view after opening; turning plugins on lists and loads them); the plugin tests that need the list now ask for it as Settings does.
- **Measured at rest** (Mac Release build, a 28-file demo vault, one note with formulas and a drawing open, three runs each; 30 s of rest after 18 s of settling):

  | | Default features | All core plugins off | Default + Read Aloud on | No vault |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Launch to first window | 0.70–1.0 s | 0.70–0.73 s | 0.78–1.0 s | |
  | Memory footprint at rest | 137 MB | 126 MB | 138–140 MB | 58 MB |
  | Peak footprint during launch | 338 MB | 323 MB | | 60 MB |
  | CPU time in 30 s at rest | 0.00–0.01 s | 0.00–0.01 s | 0.01 s | |

  At rest Graphite uses no CPU, and switched-off features cost about 11 MB. Opening a vault and its note adds about 70 MB at rest and a transient peak near 330 MB in the first 1.5 s, mostly heap (`footprint`: 78 MB Malloc Small, 15 MB Malloc Large at rest; `heap`: SwiftUI property lists, Swift metadata, CoreSVG). A trace of `openVault` found no Graphite step that reads all notes, starts MLX or Metal, or makes a web view for a vault without a theme, and two experiments place the peak in the note editor: a vault of one one-line note with nothing open sits at 76 MB (peak 77 MB), and opening that note takes the peak to 273 MB and the footprint to 102 MB; turning spell checking off changes nothing (peak 336 against 337 MB).
- **The note-opening peak was AppKit's Metal renderer, now off on the Mac** (`MacViewDrawing`). The 170 MB were "Owned physical footprint (unmapped) (graphics)", 32 regions, gone about a second later. A 40-line AppKit app with one text view showed the same (17 MB, a 206 MB peak, 32 MB after) with TextKit 1 or 2, with `NSTextView.scrollableTextView()`, with the find bar, Writing Tools and the first responder each left out, every time a text view appeared, so no Graphite code causes it. The Metal System Trace showed no Metal allocation of the app's own; lldb found the stack: AppKit gives the drawing of a view that draws itself to Core Animation's Metal renderer (`CA::CG::Queue`, `AccelRenderer`) once its layer is over 589,824 pixels (768 by 768), and starting that renderer grows the graphics driver's texture pool (`AGX::Mempool::grow` from `setupDeferred`), which is released when the renderer goes idle. AppKit reads `NSViewCanUseGPUAcceleration` once, at the first view it draws, and takes it only as a Boolean (the text `NO` from the command line changed nothing). Graphite registers it as `false` before the first window (`GraphiteApp.init`). Measured on the Mac Release build, a vault of one one-line note, two runs each, before (the same build with the default set back to true) and after:

  | | Before | After |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Peak while opening the note | 271–272 MB | 93.6–94.5 MB |
  | Footprint after opening it | 101 MB | 93–94 MB |
  | Peak launching with the note open | 271.7 MB | 93.4 MB |

  In the small AppKit app, scrolling 300 paragraphs for 5 s at 120 steps a second took 1.28–1.38 s of processor time with the renderer off and 1.39–1.51 s with it on; a 400-sentence paragraph peaked at 64 MB instead of 257 MB. The editor draws its text, links, task boxes and code blocks as before (screenshot of the Release build). Opening a PDF keeps its own 170 MB peak (345 MB with or without the setting): PDFKit starts Metal itself, which this does not change. `MacViewDrawingTests` checks that the value is registered as a Boolean and that a value set with `defaults write` still wins. **Not measured:** the 338 MB launch peak of the 28-file demo vault above (the demo vault is no longer on this Mac), PDFs' own peak, and the iPad, where UIKit has no such renderer switch and the Simulator does not show graphics memory as a device does.
- **Opening a PDF on the Mac: PDFKit's own peak, left as it is.** A bare `PDFView` in a small AppKit app showing a short text PDF goes from 17 MB to a 330–360 MB peak and stays at 153–174 MB; Graphite opening the same PDF peaks at 345 MB and rests at 158 MB. lldb found two PDFKit features behind it, neither with a public switch. Page analysis: `-[PDFView visiblePagesChanged:]` sends each page that comes into view to `PDFPageAnalyzerV2`, which runs `VNRecognizeDocumentsRequest` (a neural text detector, Metal) on `PDFKit.PDFDocument.formFillingQueue`; `-[PDFView _commonInit]` turns it on unconditionally and only the private `setDocumentAnalysisEnabled:` turns it off. Without it the same view peaks at 243 MB and rests at 70 MB, but a scanned page loses its text: an image-only PDF had 4,496 characters on page 1 with analysis and none without, so selecting, highlighting and searching scanned pages depend on it. Tile rendering: `PDFTilePool` draws page tiles into GPU-accelerated IOSurface contexts (`CGIOSurfaceContextCreateWithOptions`, tile surface type 2), which starts Core Animation's Metal renderer, the same 170 MB burst as the note editor's; only the private `-[PDFTilePool setTileSurfaceType:]` chooses processor bitmaps. With analysis off and processor tiles the view peaks at 94–98 MB. Graphite uses neither private switch (owner's choice to be confirmed, 2026-10-21): they could fail App Review or break silently with a system update. **Not measured:** rendering speed of heavy PDFs with processor tiles, and the iPad.

## Add-ons: extras downloaded on first use, starting with the tour's pictures: 2026-10-21

Graphite stays a small core: extras that a feature can do without until they arrive are add-on packs, downloaded the first time they are needed instead of shipped in the app. A pack (`AddOnPack`, Core) lists each file's name, size and SHA-256 and the commit it comes from; `AddOnPackStore` (GraphiteApple) downloads every file into a staging folder, checks it, and moves the pack into place below Application Support, never into a vault, or changes nothing if a file fails; `AddOnLibrary` (GraphiteUI) tracks each pack's state, and Settings › General › Add-ons lists the packs with their size, Download and Remove. The size and SHA-256 check (`DownloadedFileCheck`) is now shared with Read Aloud's voices.

The welcome tour's screenshots are the first pack: 3.1 MB on iPad and iPhone and 1.7 MB on the Mac left the app (the iOS archive's asset catalog carried 3.2 MB of them). They are `AddOns/WelcomeTour` of the repository at commit `d10be4c`, downloaded over HTTPS from raw.githubusercontent.com the first time the tour opens; until they arrive the tour shows a placeholder with "Downloading the pictures…", or why they could not be downloaded with Try Again. Graphite sends nothing with the request.

- **Automated tests:** `AddOnPackStoreTests` (5, macOS package, a file:// folder as the server): a pack is installed only once every file matched; a file whose hash differs leaves nothing installed and no staging folder; a new revision downloads again and replaces the old files; removing deletes them; the packs live below Application Support. `WelcomeTourScreenshotPackTests` (2): every screenshot the platform's pages show is in its pack in light and dark, and the pack comes from a full commit hash over HTTPS. The Read Aloud voice tests still pass with the shared check. `scripts/check-add-on-packs.py` checks every listed file's size and SHA-256 against the pinned commit.
- **Checked by hand:** the iPad simulator, a fresh tour: the placeholder, then the pictures, all 28 files verified in Application Support/Graphite/Add-ons.
- **Not established:** a slow or lost connection in the middle of a download on a device, and the Mac downloading its pack (built, not run with an empty folder).

## A Guide for beginners: 2026-10-21

The Guide (`GuideView`, its topics in `GuideTopic` and their words in the `GuideCopy` files) teaches Graphite step by step, from making a vault and a first note to every feature: 32 topics in six sections (The Basics, Writing Notes, Finding and Organizing, Handwriting and PDFs, Studying, Making It Yours). Each topic has numbered steps that name the controls as the app labels them, Markdown to type where it helps, and tips; a search field finds topics by any of their words, and each topic links to the next. Where the Mac differs (no [[ suggestions or formatting shortcuts in its editor, canvases and PDFs viewed but not drawn on, a New Note button with a menu), its steps say so, and its Guide leaves out drawings, Pencil tools and notebooks. It opens from Settings › General › Guide, the welcome screen's Guide button and the welcome tour's last page, and on the Mac from Help › Graphite Guide (⌘?) in a window of its own.

- **Automated tests:** `GuideContentTests` (7, macOS package): every topic has a title, a summary and steps, titles are distinct, every section has topics and each topic is in one, the Guide starts with vaults and the first note, search matches every word ignoring case and reads the examples, the Mac keeps only the PDF topic of its section, and `**bold**` is styled with its marks removed. All pass.
- **Checked by hand:** the iPad simulator (Settings › General › Guide, topics with steps, an example and the next link), the iPhone simulator (the Guide opens on its list of topics with search), and the Mac (Help › Graphite Guide opens the window, with the Mac's words).
- **Not established:** each step followed by hand on a device. The labels were taken from the code on 2026-10-21; a renamed control makes its step wrong until the Guide is updated.

## A welcome tour for TestFlight testers: 2026-10-21

New testers open Graphite with no idea what it does, so the first launch on a device now shows a tour of eleven pages: a vault is a folder of ordinary files, Markdown notes the Obsidian way, embeds (notes, sections, PDF pages with a height, images, bases), a gallery base, handwriting in notes, annotating PDFs and notebooks, the two Pencil tool styles, the other features, Settings, and getting started. Each page names the buttons, menus and settings pages as the app labels them, and shows screenshots of Graphite in the light or the dark appearance, taken on iPad and, for the Mac's pages, on the Mac, so each platform shows itself (since the add-ons entry above, downloaded rather than in the app); a page with several pictures has a segmented control (Floating Palette, Fixed Bar, Settings). The Mac's tour leaves out the three Pencil pages and opens on what sets the Mac app apart: native (SwiftUI and AppKit, no browser engine drawing notes), open source, and fast and light (only claims the code or `A vault with tens of thousands of files at its root opens on the Mac` support). The tour shows again from Settings › General › Welcome Tour and from the welcome screen's Take the Tour. Invariant touched: ARCH-INV-17 (a hosted-tests launch never opens the tour, `LaunchMode.presentsWelcomeTour`).

- The Mac pictures were taken from a copy of the Mac app built under another bundle identifier (`com.graphite.study.screenshots`, so the installed Graphite's container was never touched), capturing only its window. The other screenshots were taken in the iPad simulator. Both used a demo vault built for them: trimmed copies of five of the owner's course lecture notes and two slide decks, a Fourier series note and an exam review note written for the demo, and a `Lectures.base` whose cards view uses a figure of each lecture as its cover. None of the owner's personal notes is in it.
- **Automated tests:** `WelcomeTourContentTests` (5, macOS package): every page has a title, a summary and points with distinct identifiers, several screenshots have distinct labels, the order starts with the welcome and ends with getting started, the Mac leaves out exactly the Pencil pages, and the source link is the repository. `LaunchModeTests.testOnlyANormalLaunchOpensTheWelcomeTourByItself`. Hosted `WelcomeTourScreenshotTests` (iPad simulator): every screenshot loads from the app bundle in light and dark, each with its own dark picture. All pass.
- **Checked by hand in the simulator:** the first launch shows the tour over the reopened vault (iPad Pro 11-inch, landscape, light and dark) and over the welcome screen with no vault (iPhone 17 Pro); every page fits the iPad in landscape without scrolling; Next, Back, the page dots and Skip work, and closing marks the tour seen.
- The hosted target did not compile on `main` (`CommunityPluginHostTests.swift:153` called the now throwing `CommunityPluginSecretStore.secrets()` without `try`); the call now uses `try?`.
- **Checked by hand on the Mac:** all eight pages of the Mac tour, in a sheet over the window, showing the Mac pictures; `assetutil` lists only the Mac pictures in the Mac app's `Assets.car`.
- **Not established:** iPad portrait and a split window, Dynamic Type at the largest sizes, and VoiceOver reading the pages. The repository has no license file, so "open source" means the source is public; a license makes it open source in the formal sense.

## The audit's Low defects fixed, each with a regression test: 2026-10-20

On the branch `fix-math-breaking-at-rounded-width`, commits `7fff98b` to `ceb2bec`: 36 of the 37 open Low defects of [the SOLID audit](solid/status.md) are fixed, one commit per area. Every row in `Docs/solid/status.md` names its commit and test.

- **Vault switching** (`67541c1`): a drawing open in its editor holds the vault (a `graphite://open` link is refused until it is inserted or cancelled), a failed last save keeps the vault and its typed text, and the listening queue, the pen's marks, the callout folds and a graph or map query of the vault being left no longer reach the next vault.
- **Links, files and search** (`0f51892`, `9e7e7ce`): a link to a missing `.flac`, `.ogg`, `.3gp`, `.webm` or `.m4v` file creates no note (`DocumentKind` alone lists the media extensions), and `content:/^$/` no longer lists every canvas. `BUG-ARCH-05` was already fixed by `93d82a6`.
- **Bases** (`5caa6dc`): turning off Default Zoom works on a map written with `zoom:`, Markdown links match aliases nowhere (as in backlinks), tapping a link cell opens the file the base resolves, and an edit refuses to write over views a root merge key supplies.
- **Canvas** (`a264185`): a card resized or grouped past 1,000,000 px stays on the board, and renaming a note named by a canvas Graphite cannot read reports that canvas as not updated (new index migration `unreadable-canvas-state-10`, which reads every canvas again once).
- **Editor and ink** (`723281b`): a note created on the Mac starts editing, and drawing on an image says the note changed, not that it dropped the image, when it only changed meanwhile.
- **PDFs and lectures** (`7fff98b`): a signed PDF embedded in a note says it is signed, the embed's page field reads any script's digits, and Open Settings on the Mac opens the refused device's privacy pane.
- **Markdown core and reading view** (`0388349`, `e7fa377`): a fence indented four columns outside a list opens no code block in the editor, reading view or Live Preview, private-use characters show as written in Live Preview, an unlinked mention stays listed when no link was written, and Live Preview labels Wikilinks as reading view does ("Note > Heading").
- **Plugin runtime** (`1e0b253`): a theme's fonts reach plugin pages and block copies, an older note read no longer replaces a plugin's write in the metadata cache, `app.getTheme()` follows the page, and an image map's area can neither leave nor replace the runtime page.
- **Shell** (`613c24d`, `b4d1e17`): removing a vault forgets its folds, Reveal current file shows the sidebar and scrolls to the file from the palette and tab menus, File Recovery shows why a list or copy cannot be read, the phone's tab switcher names plugin views, `graphite://read` reads a note open on the other side of the split, and a plugin's rename gets the move's real outcome however long the answered move runs.
- **Read Aloud** (`2ad2da3`): Stop holds while a fold change is prepared, folding goes on from the word reached, and a voice download keeps its failure reason.
- **Automated tests, all passing on macOS:** each new test was run before its fix (failing) and after it (passing). The full package suite passed locally (`swift test --parallel`, 3,397 tests) with `UiMapViewMacTests` skipped: that existing class opened the speaker through `systemsoundserverd` at its start in two runs, so it was left out under the no-sound rule. The Node runtime suite passes (136 tests: 118 pass, 18 skipped for unbuilt real plugins). `scripts/check-rules.py` fails only `default-over-own-enum`, as on `e3d97c9`.
- **Not established:** device checks of Reveal current file with the sidebar hidden (iPad, Mac), of a deep link reaching the app while the full-screen drawing editor is up, and of the Mac editor taking the keyboard for a new note in the app. The plugin rename still polls (`CC-PLUGIN-15`).
- **Still open:** `BUG-VOICE-12` (connecting headphones pauses High Quality reading). It needs a device check of what `AVAudioEngine` does on a new output, and on the Mac a way to tell a new output from a lost one.

## Formulas no longer lose their letters in reading view: 2026-10-20

In reading view, some inline formulas showed only their subscript: `$g_m$` as a lone `m`, `$r_o$` as `o`, `$V_A$` as `A`, in one callout of a note and not in the next (reported on the iPad, `Advanced Analog ic/2_SmallSignalAnalysis_I.md`). SwiftUIMath, the typesetter Textual draws formulas with, typesets each formula again at the width of the canvas that draws it. That frame is rounded to whole pixels and could come out a fraction of a point narrower than the formula, which then broke into `g` over `m`, with only one line showing. SwiftUIMath is now vendored in `Vendor/swiftui-math` (0.1.0, the latest release; upstream has no fix), and a formula that fits its width to within a point is never broken into lines (`Vendor/swiftui-math/GRAPHITE-PATCHES.md`, 1). The same typesetter draws Live Preview's formula images and display math, which share the fix.

- `AppProjectTextualPatchTests.testFormulaInAFrameNarrowerByAFractionOfAPointStaysOnOneLine` (macOS, rendered and read pixel by pixel): `g_m`, `r_o`, `V_A`, `V_{ov}` and `k_n`, each drawn in a frame 0.4 points narrower than itself, stay on one line, and a formula twice as wide as its width still breaks. It fails without the patch (`g_m` measured 13.5 points wide on two lines).
- The Mac never showed the fault: rendering the reported callouts, and the formulas at 24 fractional offsets, drew them whole before the fix as well, so the iPad is the place to confirm it.

## The four Medium defects of the SOLID audit fixed: 2026-10-20

On the branch `fix-four-medium-defects`, one commit per defect, each with its regression test written first and seen failing on the old code ([status.md](solid/status.md)).

- **BUG-MDCORE-05** (`9a96593`): a display formula's alignment environments are rewritten to 16 levels; deeper ones are left as written, and each environment's end is found in one scan. `ParserNestingLimitsTests`: 16 and 17 levels, 5,000 levels on a 512 KB stack (the old code ran about 7 minutes and died with signal 10), linear scaling.
- **BUG-PLUGIN-05** (`1a0f5d0`): when the keychain does not give back a vault's plugin secrets, a plugin's save is refused and the stored item is left as it was; the runtime starts without them and shows a notice. `CommunityPluginSecretStoreTests` writes an undecodable item into the real keychain. Not established: a failing keychain status itself (for example a denied Mac keychain prompt), which the store cannot be made to return in a test.
- **BUG-SHELL-10** (`a2b32ab`): a vault whose Templates or Daily notes settings file cannot be read no longer keeps the previous vault's settings; Settings shows Obsidian's defaults and the template and daily note actions are refused with the reason. `WorkspaceTemplatesVaultSwitchTests` (two vaults; the old code created `Journal/<today>.md` in the second).
- **BUG-VOICE-11** (`1b6f5ae`): Settings › Read Aloud › Pronunciations applies each edit to the vault's file as it is when saved, and writes only while the file is still that revision, so a term that arrived from another device is kept. `ReadAloudPronunciationVaultFileTests` and new cases in `ReadAloudPronunciationVaultFileWritingTests`.
- **Automated tests, all passing on macOS:** the full package suite (`swift test`, from a clean export of `1b6f5ae` with scratchpad build paths): 3,371 tests (GraphiteCore 1,620, GraphiteUI 1,181, GraphiteApple 419, GraphiteIndex 151), 13 skipped, 0 failures. No hosted iPad suite. During both full runs an audio-output assertion (`BuiltInSpeakerDevice`, with an `AudioTap` input) appeared for 1 to 4 seconds as `UiMapViewMacTests` and `UiNumberedEquationTests` ran, and once in the GraphiteApple bundle; not caused by these changes, and not investigated further under the no-sound rule.
- **Not established:** the four fixes on the iPad or iPhone, and the pronunciation page and the refused template and daily note actions by hand in the running app.

On the branch `goodnotes-notability-import`: step 9 of [the Phase 3 design](Goodnotes-Notability-import-phase-3-design.md), the import report as plain values. Nothing shows it yet (Phase 4).

- **Automated tests, all passing on macOS**, run as single classes (`xcrun xctest -XCTest`), no full or hosted suite: every GraphiteCore import class, 287 tests, with the owner's samples configured (none skipped), among them the new `NotebookImportReportTests` (5); the GraphiteApple import classes, 41 tests, whose writer tests now also check what each written page holds (strokes, placed pictures).
- **Samples:** a temporary check, not committed, built the report of every sample (50): each summary has at most 7 lines, 14 notebooks import with nothing to report, and the one Notability note without known pages is reported as such. ## Importing Goodnotes and Notability: recordings, and large files read without a copy: 2026-10-20

On the branch `goodnotes-notability-import`: step 7 of [the Phase 3 design](Goodnotes-Notability-import-phase-3-design.md), and the audit's CC-IMPORT-13. Nothing in the app calls the import yet.

- **Automated tests, all passing on macOS**, run as single classes (`xcrun xctest -XCTest`), no full or hosted suite:
  - GraphiteCore: every import class, 282 tests, with the owner's samples configured (none skipped). New: `ZipArchiveEntryCopyTests` (8: a stored entry read and copied from the archive's own bytes, a deflated one through one buffer, the checksum checked at the end, a stream past its size stopped there, the destination's error passed on, the limit before any piece) and `ImportedRecordingPlannerTests` (4).
  - GraphiteApple: `NotebookImportRecordingWriterTests` (5: bytes unchanged, a file that is not MPEG-4 refused, a source failing part way and an existing file leaving the destination as it was), with the other writer tests still passing (41 in all).
- **Samples:** a temporary check, not committed, wrote both made-for-purpose recordings through the readers and the writer: each `.m4a` is byte-identical to the notebook's file, and Core Audio's `afinfo` reads AAC, 22,050 Hz mono, 47.2 and 18.9 seconds (metadata only, nothing played). Every recording of every sample (4, the longest 486 seconds and 2 MB, deflated) copies with its checksum correct.

## Importing Goodnotes and Notability: pictures, shapes, Notability paper and bookmarks: 2026-10-20
