## Tables edited in place, scrolling only when wider, and the editor's Color menu: 2026-10-17

Reported by the user, comparing a lecture schedule table in Graphite and Obsidian: in Graphite a tap on a table showed its Markdown, where Obsidian keeps the table drawn and edits its cells; a table swiped sideways also moved slightly up and down; and the editor's right-click menu had no way to color text.

- Live Preview tables are edited in place (see "Editing a table in place" in `Architecture.md`): a tap opens an editor over the cell holding its Markdown; Return goes to the cell below, Tab and Shift-Tab across, Escape closes, a one-line cell's arrows leave it, and Return or Tab past the end adds a row. The table's menu (long press, secondary click) has Row and Column submenus: add, move, delete, and the column's alignment. Each change is one undoable edit of only the characters involved (`MarkdownTableEditing`, Core).
- A table that fits the column has no scroll view; a wider one scrolls sideways and no longer moves vertically (Textual patch 16).
- The iPad's editor no longer turns `---` into `—` across the note when an edit arrives from a rendered view, a task's checkbox or the toolbar while it does not have the keyboard: such edits now change its text storage directly (`replaceCharacters(in:with:in:)`). This is likely the cause of the em dash once seen in the frontmatter after a property edit.
- The editor's context menu (the Mac's right-click menu, the iPad's edit menu) has Obsidian's Add Link, Add External Link, Color (the vault's palette and Remove), Format, Paragraph (lists, task, headings, Body, Quote) and Insert submenus, through the toolbar's own commands; the Mac's Font submenu is gone. Color's Custom… is left out: the Mac's color panel reports every color passed while dragging and never a final choice.

The table model and the context menus were written by agents on the Messages API (Sonnet 5.5, high effort), and the change was reviewed by one (Opus 5.5); everything was read, integrated and checked here. The review found, and this change fixes: one table's editor ending released the hold of the table a tap had moved editing to; with two identical tables, a row added on Return could go into the other one; a table moved by text added above it lost later edits; on the Mac, clicking out of a cell not yet typed in brought the focus back; the Mac's field editor could undo the previous cell's typing in the next cell; a draft could be lost when the views were let go for Source mode; text an input method was composing was written into the note; and the iPad's undo actions targeted the editor without keeping it alive. A cell edit that makes a table wider than the column still closes the cell editor (the draft is written).

Checked:
- `MarkdownTableTests` (51, Core): parsing with exact UTF-16 ranges (outer pipes or none, indentation, `\r\n` and lone `\r`, escaped pipes, empty and ragged cells, every alignment, header only, not a table); every edit with exact expected text, including a cell past a ragged row's end, `|` and line breaks typed in a cell, replacing a cell with its own editable text leaving the source unchanged, rows added with and without a final line break and with Windows line endings, rows and columns moved at the edges, columns in tables without outer pipes, alignment keeping the delimiter's width, and accented, emoji and CJK text.
- `TextColorMarkupTests` (21, 9 of them new, Core): removing color at a cursor, over a selection, nested, unclosed, in code.
- `UiEditorMacTableEditingTests` (5, macOS, the AppKit editor in a window): edits from the table's view change only their cell, escape a typed pipe, add a row, keep the same view though the table's key changes, leave the note's cursor where it was, and undo back to the original text; an edit that changes nothing leaves the note and the undo history alone; of two identical tables, the edited one gets the row added right after a cell edit; a line added above the table while it is edited does not lose the next edit; one editor ending leaves another editor's hold.
- `UiEditorMacContextMenuTests` (3, macOS): the right-click menu's first items, its Color submenu with the default palette and Remove, no Font submenu, no Color submenu with the Colors feature off, choosing a color and removing one.
- In the iPad simulator ("Graphite Table Editing iPad", iPad Pro 11-inch (M5), iOS 27.0, a note with the user's schedule table, a table without outer pipes and a wide table), each step checked against the saved file: a tap opens the cell's editor; typing is written into that cell only and the row grows while the editor keeps the keyboard; Return moves down, and `" A⏎ B⏎ C"` typed in one burst lands in three rows; the long-press menu's Row submenu (Move Row Up disabled on the first row) adds a row below; four undos from the toolbar give back the original file byte for byte; selecting a word and choosing Color › red in the edit menu writes `~={#e93147}above=~`; with the sidebar and a split open, the schedule table moves into a scroll view and scrolls sideways, the small table stays without one, the app is idle, and a vertical drag over the table scrolls the note with the table still while held; inside the scrolling table a cell is edited as before, and a tap in the other table moves editing there (typed the instant after the tap, a key was dropped rather than written into the cell left behind; typed a moment later, it lands in the new cell, in the table without outer pipes as `betaQ | 2`).
- The hosted iPad editor tests (`GraphiteIntegrationTests`: `LivePreviewBlockPlacementTests`, `LivePreviewRevealTests`, `EditingContinuityTests`, `ReadingTasksAndEquationsTests`, same simulator): 42 of 42; `swift test` on macOS: 641 UI tests (5 opt-in benchmarks skipped), 1,043 Core, 140 Index and 249 Apple, no failure; Xcode builds of the app for the iOS simulator; both run again after the review's fixes.

Not verified: Tab, Shift-Tab, Escape and the arrows on a hardware keyboard (the simulator tool pastes a tab instead of pressing it), the Mac's cell editor and menus by hand in the app, the on-screen keyboard's Return, VoiceOver on the cell editor, and a physical iPad.

## Smoother scrolling, typing and tab switching: 2026-10-17

Live Preview does less work while scrolling and typing, and switching tabs updates less of the window:
- Typing in a list item no longer scans the whole note for blocks on every keystroke, and typing in a callout's body finds only that callout again (see Architecture, typing cost).
- Each save, about a second after typing stops, no longer redraws the callouts that hold only text; only bases, embeds and the callouts holding one read the index.
- Textual compiles its two math patterns once per thread instead of for every text it parses (`Vendor/textual/GRAPHITE-PATCHES.md`, 15), about a third of parsing a rendered block's Markdown.
- The window's root view and the sidebar are no longer updated for every change of the tab layout, such as each frame of a drag of the split's divider: the focused tab and the tab count are kept apart from the layout.
- Back and Forward keep showing the tab's file until the next one is ready, instead of an empty pane.
- The empty tab looks its recent files up on disk once per update instead of twice.

Measured with `PerformanceBenchmarkTests` (optimized builds, three runs each), main c605500 against these changes. The benchmark now also types in a list item and in a callout with the note focused; their "before" was measured on the Mac before the typing changes, with the new scenarios added.

| Scenario | Measure | Before | After | Change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mac, scrolling a long note down and up | main thread (ms) | 3,502 | 3,294 | −6% |
| Mac, scrolling a long note down and up | frames over 16 ms | 77 | 63 | −18% |
| Mac, typing in a list item | frame median (ms) | 10.1 | 7.6 | −25% |
| Mac, typing in a callout | frame median (ms) | 10.4 | 8.0 | −23% |
| iPad simulator, scrolling a long note down and up | main thread (ms) | 4,445 | 3,902 | −12% |
| iPad simulator, scrolling down | 95th percentile / worst frame (ms) | 37.7 / 126 | 32.6 / 48 | −14% / −62% |
| iPad simulator, typing in a paragraph | main thread (ms) | 363 | 324 | −11% |

Where scrolling's time goes (Time Profiler, Mac): almost all of it is SwiftUI building each rendered block the first time it comes into view (about 13 ms for a small table, 5 for a callout, 3 for a formula) and again, at about half that, when a kept block's view comes back, since a view taken out of the window is drawn again from nothing. Keeping those views hidden in the text view, or in a container beside it, made scrolling slower: a hosting view follows its position in the window and is laid out again on every step of a scroll. Laying a table out at its scroll container's width in its first pass saved about 4% and was left out. Building the blocks just below the screen while the note is idle is the remaining way to take this work out of scrolling; it is not done.

Checked: `swift test` on macOS (633 UI tests with 5 opt-in benchmarks skipped, 982 Core, 140 Index, 249 Apple), with new tests for typing in list items and callouts (`UiEditorEditTrackingTests`: the blocks equal a new scan's, and the edits that can change blocks scan again), for callouts that read the index (`UiEditorRenderedBlockReuseTests`), and for the focused note's views across a divider drag (`UiWorkspaceAreaHandoffTests`, which fails without the change); the iPad benchmark in the simulator.

Not verified: a physical iPad or iPhone; the hosted iPad test suite; Back and Forward in the running app (a package test with a window that is never shown could not tell the empty pane from the note, so it was not kept).

## Builds without warnings on the Mac, the iPhone and the iPad: 2026-10-17

The Mac, iPhone and iPad builds, and the package's test targets, compile without Graphite warnings. The C++ warnings in the MLX package come from outside Graphite and remain. The macOS 27 AVFoundation deprecations noted below are gone. A video part is written through its inputs' receivers (`inputReceiver(for:)`, `appendImmediately`, `start()`) from macOS 26 and iOS 26 on. `expectsMediaDataInRealTime` is still set, on purpose: without it, a receiver held video back while no sound arrived and took 36 of 180 frames, and with it all 180 (macOS 27, measured). From macOS 27 and iOS 27 the camera preview reaches its layer through a render synchronizer's receiver, and Read Aloud's player connects and starts with the calls that report errors. Older iOS versions keep the previous calls. The rest were Swift 6 isolation fixes (constants read by shapes, Reduce Motion, the pinch policy, the sidebar's row equality), unused results, and `UITraitCollection(traitsFrom:)` replaced by `modifyingTraits`.

Checked: Xcode builds of the Graphite scheme for macOS and for the iOS simulator (both architectures), the whole `swift test` on macOS (627 UI tests with 5 opt-in benchmarks skipped, 982 Core, 140 Index, 249 Apple) and a new `testASecondRecordingShowsItsCameraInTheSamePreview`. Without the release of the old synchronizer, that test ends with "The DisplayLayer cannot be added to a Synchronizer when it has already been added to a Synchronizer". A separate run on the Mac showed the preview layer displays frames marked "display immediately" while the synchronizer's clock is stopped. Another showed that the throwing `connectNode`/`playAudio` play and resume.

Not verified: the camera preview and a video recording on a device; Read Aloud's High Quality playback in the app; the iOS test suites (the iOS changes were only built).

## Bug fixes and speed-ups after the shared editor, measured on the Mac and the iPad simulator: 2026-10-17

Eight commits, 853021b to 89c1b0d, fix about 70 bugs and edge cases. Each fix is listed in its commit message. They cover typed text, saving and closing, Markdown reading, Bases' JavaScript arithmetic, Read Aloud, recording, plugins, canvas, themes, PDFs (places, pages, bookmarks, signatures, the lasso) and links and folds. They also make the Live Preview editor, the PDF pane and the map do less work. The last commit fixes a memory leak on the Mac: a closed note's rendered blocks stayed in memory, with the note's text and session, for as long as the app ran. A block's view left inside the text view stayed registered with AppKit's notification center. The editor now takes its block views out before it goes.

Measured with `PerformanceBenchmarkTests` (`GRAPHITE_BENCHMARKS=1`, optimized builds) on the development Mac and on the iPad simulator in Release:
- **Before:** the shared editor as merged, with the benchmarks of 7b3e434.
- **After:** 50fce43, before the leak fix.

The Mac figures are the means of two runs (three for the note scenarios); the iPad figures are from one run. Times are milliseconds of processor time unless marked otherwise; "main thread" is the main thread's own processor time.

Gains of 30% or more, Mac:

| Scenario | Measure | Before | After | Change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Embedded PDF while other files change | processor time | 120.6 | 36.5 | −70% |
| Embedded PDF, idle | frame median (ms) | 4.06 | 2.21 | −46% |
| Long PDF with pages sidebar, scrolling | main thread | 1,891 | 945 | −50% |
| Map updates | memory growth (MB) | 5.7 | 2.8 | −51% |
| Typing in a long note | main thread | 508 | 288 | −43% |
| Typing in a long note | processor time | 639 | 417 | −35% |
| Typing in a long note | frame median (ms) | 10.0 | 6.4 | −36% |
| Scrolling a long note back up | main thread | 2,239 | 1,496 | −33% |
| Scrolling a long note back up | frames over 16 ms | 73 | 31 | −58% |
| Scrolling a long note down and up | frames over 16 ms | 136 | 94 | −31% |

Gains of 30% or more, iPad simulator:

| Scenario | Measure | Before | After | Change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Embedded PDF while other files change | processor time | 174.8 | 70.3 | −60% |
| Opening a long PDF | processor time | 552 | 333 | −40% |
| Memory kept after closing a PDF | MB | 43.1 | 22.1 | −49% |
| Typing in a long note | main thread | 550 | 348 | −37% |
| Long PDF with pages sidebar, scrolling | frame median / 95th percentile (ms) | 11.9 / 12.9 | 7.8 / 8.6 | −34% / −33% |
| Scrolling a long note down | memory growth (MB) | 91.7 | 53.7 | −41% |
| Scrolling a long note back up | memory growth (MB) | 61.9 | 22.7 | −63% |
| Memory kept after closing a note | MB | 2.2 | −19.0 | all returned |

Got worse:
- **Mac, memory kept after closing a note:** 22 → 50 MB. This was measured while the benchmark still held the editor and before the leak fix, and it was not measured again after the fix.
- **Mac:** opening a note grows memory 2.8 → 5.5 MB. Scrolling a long note grows it 231 → 277 MB, because the kept-views pool keeps more views within its budget. Opening a PDF takes 14% more processor time.
- **iPad simulator:**
  - Opening a note takes 27% more processor time (822 → 1,046 ms across all threads; the main thread is up 3%).
  - Memory grows more while typing (24.7 → 38.6 MB), while scrolling a PDF (63 → 87 MB) and while the map updates (16 → 19 MB).
  - The frame median scrolling a long note down is 8.4 → 13.9 ms, though its 95th percentile went down 7%. This is from one run.

Checked: `swift test` on macOS, 627 UI tests with the new `UiEditorReleaseTests` (5 skipped), all passing. The leak was traced with `leaks --atExit` before the fix.

Not verified:
- A physical iPad: all iPad figures are from the simulator.
- The hosted iPad tests on main as merged.
- Whether the iPad has the same leak: its memory kept after closing a note went down, but the test is Mac only.
- PDFKit's own background text recognition while scrolling a PDF: it is outside Graphite's code.

## Code blocks drawn as Obsidian's boxes, and code themes: 2026-10-17

At the owner's request, with a screenshot of Obsidian, code blocks look as Obsidian draws them: in Live Preview, one rounded box across the column without a border, the fences hidden unless the cursor is in the block, the language at the top right, thin indentation guides, and Obsidian's syntax colors (types yellow, names cyan, keywords pink, values purple, operators red, strings green, comments and whole `#pragma` lines faint). Source mode keeps the fences inside the box; reading view draws the same box and label. The owner then asked for code themes, keeping the boxes: Settings › Editor › Code Theme offers Obsidian (the default, following the vault's theme), One Monokai (with One Light), Dracula (with Alucard), GitHub, Solarized and Xcode, each in a light and a dark version, with a preview. See Architecture.

The owner then preferred the earlier reading view's box, with a thin gray border, 4-point corners and tight padding (about 9 points, more above for the label): both editors and reading view now draw that box, keeping the label and the guides at the owner's choice. Checked again: the macOS UI suite, the hosted iPad classes (43) and the iPad screenshots, looked at.

Not done: guides in reading view; a theme's own box in only one appearance (a dark One Monokai box in light appearance); code quoted in callouts keeps the plain background.

Checked: `CodeSyntaxHighlightingTests.testTheHLSExampleIsColoredAsInObsidian` (the owner's example token by token) and the updated language tests (names now `identifier`, operators); `UiCodeHighlightingMacTests` (6 more, macOS AppKit editor: the box's decorations, label and guides with the fences hidden, the fences showing while the cursor is in the block and hiding when it leaves, Source mode, the box's gray across the column beside a short line in a rendered bitmap, which was also looked at, a code theme's colors and box, every theme readable on its own box in light and dark (the Increase Contrast colors checked as the guard makes them: a high-contrast `NSAppearance` made in a test resolves Graphite's appearance-dependent colors as the normal one)); `UiEditorRevealedMarkupTests` found that a selection spanning a block was taken as touching it by a whole restyle but not by a selection change, now one rule (the selection starts or ends in the block); hosted iPad `CodeHighlightingTests.testCodeBlockBoxesInEachViewAndTheme` (the owner's example in Live Preview and reading view, Obsidian and One Monokai, light and dark; screenshots looked at); `swift test` on macOS: 615 UI tests (one skipped), 959 Core, 140 Index and 247 Apple; hosted iPad, the three `CodeHighlightingTests` with the editor classes `EditingContinuityTests`, `LivePreviewRevealTests`, `LivePreviewBlockPlacementTests` and `ReadingTasksAndEquationsTests`: 43; all passing.

Not verified: by hand in the app (the simulator panel's device access was not granted); Increase Contrast on screen; a vault theme with its own syntax colors on screen.

## Code blocks formatted when the cursor leaves them: 2026-10-16

At the owner's request ("main(){ … }" should become properly indented code), a fenced code block whose code the user changed is formatted when the selection leaves it or editing ends with the cursor in it, on the iPad and the Mac, in Live Preview and Source mode. The owner chose formatting on leaving, of indentation and spacing, over a command or indenting as one types. It can be switched off (Settings › Editor › Format Code Blocks). A block only read or moved through is not rewritten, and Undo takes the formatting back. Languages re-indented: C, C++, Objective-C, C#, Java, Kotlin, JavaScript, TypeScript, Swift, Rust, Go, JSON, CSS, VHDL, Verilog/SystemVerilog, MATLAB, Lua, Ruby, shell and LaTeX; Python, YAML, SQL, assembly and HTML only lose trailing spaces and extra blank lines (and Python and SQL get spaces after commas); Markdown and code inside callouts are left alone. See Architecture for what changes.

Not handled: continuation lines of a statement get no extra indentation; a Verilog module's port list in parentheses on the `module` line is indented two levels; spaces around operators are not changed; a `case` label in C sits at the `switch`'s level (LLVM's style); a JavaScript regular expression literal is read as code, so `/(a){2}/` would get a space in `){`.

Checked: `CodeFormattingTests` (13, Core: the owner's `main(){` example, C with preprocessor lines, strings, comments and spacing, the code's own indentation width kept (two spaces, Go's tab), VHDL with entity, ports, architecture, process, case/when, if/elsif/else and an entity instantiation, Verilog with module, always, begin/end else and case, Python, shell with if, for and case, MATLAB, Lua, LaTeX, JSON with Windows line endings, formatting twice changes nothing, Markdown not formatted, a block's code range and whether a location is in it); `UiCodeHighlightingMacTests` (4 more, macOS AppKit editor: an edited block formatted on leaving and Undo restoring it, a block passed through left alone, nothing formatted while the cursor stays in the block and leaving it upward formats it, the setting off); hosted iPad `CodeHighlightingTests.testAnEditedBlockIsFormattedWhenTheCursorLeavesIt` (UIKit editor: typing, leaving, the saved note, Undo); `swift test` on macOS: 609 UI tests (one skipped), 958 Core, 140 Index and 247 Apple; hosted iPad, the two `CodeHighlightingTests` with `EditingContinuityTests`, `LivePreviewRevealTests`, `LivePreviewBlockPlacementTests` and `ReadingTasksAndEquationsTests`: 42; all passing.

Measured in an optimized build on the development Mac: a 2,000-line C block formats in about 6.4 ms, once, when the cursor leaves it.

Not verified: by hand in the app with a real keyboard, tap or trackpad; formatting time on a physical iPad; iPad dictation and the software keyboard's predictive text inside a block.

## Syntax highlighting in fenced code blocks: 2026-10-16

Fenced code is colored by the language its fence names, in Live Preview, Source mode and reading view, on the iPad and the Mac (`CodeLineHighlighter`, `CodeLanguage`, `CodeSyntaxColors`; see Architecture). Languages: C, C++ (and CUDA, Arduino), Objective-C, C#, Java, Kotlin, JavaScript, TypeScript, Python, Swift, Rust, Go, shell (bash, sh, zsh), JSON, YAML, SQL, VHDL, Verilog and SystemVerilog, assembly (x86, ARM, AArch64, RISC-V and MIPS registers), MATLAB and Octave, LaTeX, Markdown, HTML and XML, CSS, Ruby and Lua, with their common aliases (`c++`, `cc`, `h`, `hpp`, `vhd`, `sv`, `py`, `js`, `ts`, `sh`, `zsh`, `yml`, `rs`, `asm`, `tex`, `md`, …). A language Graphite does not know stays plain in the editor; in reading view Textual's Prism highlighter still colors the ones it bundles (Haskell, Scala, Elixir, …), in the same colors. Highlighting is lexical (keywords, types, functions, strings, numbers, constants, comments, directives, attributes, keys, variables): no parsing, so a name is colored by its spelling and place, not by what it refers to. Not handled: Rust raw strings with `#` that span lines, C++ raw strings with delimiters, shell here-documents, YAML block scalars, regular expression literals in JavaScript.

An edit restyles only its lines, as before. The state a code line starts in is kept on the line, so an edit that opens or closes a block comment or a string spanning lines recolors the following lines until a line's state agrees again; a blank line inside the block no longer ends the recoloring (it now has an empty `.codeBlock` span). Reading view tokenizes a block synchronously when it is laid out, cached by its text. Measured in an optimized build on the development Mac: a 2,000-line C++ block tokenizes in about 5 ms, and the state-only pass a keystroke at its end needs takes about 1.3 ms. Not measured on a physical iPad.

Colors followed Xcode's default light and dark themes at first (Obsidian's, and a choice of code themes, since 2026-10-17, above), kept at 4.5:1 against the code background's appearance and at 7:1 with Increase Contrast (`AccentLegibility`). A vault theme that changes Obsidian's syntax colors, its palette or its `--code-*` variables, has all of its syntax colors used: the theme probe reads Prism token classes in a code block (default layer version 5, so cached theme tokens are resolved again).

Checked: `CodeSyntaxHighlightingTests` (27, Core: each language's tokens, aliases, line states, Windows line endings, a 2,000-line block, the cache), `MarkdownStyleScannerTests.testCodeLinesKnowTheirFencedBlock`, `VaultThemeTokensTests.testSyntaxColorsAreTakenTogetherWhenTheThemeChangesAny`; `UiCodeHighlightingMacTests` (5, macOS: a ```` ```cpp ```` block's colors in the AppKit editor in Live Preview and Source mode, prose and an unknown language left plain, `/*` and `*/` recoloring 30 lines below the edit across a blank line, reading view drawing a C++ block in syntax colors and an unknown language without, reading segments joining to the code); the hosted iPad test `CodeHighlightingTests` (iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator "Graphite Code Highlighting iPad", iOS 27.0: the UIKit editor's colors in Live Preview and Source mode, reading view's syntax-colored pixels in light and dark, with screenshots of all three views in both appearances, looked at), run with the hosted editor classes `EditingContinuityTests`, `LivePreviewRevealTests`, `LivePreviewBlockPlacementTests` and `ReadingTasksAndEquationsTests`, 41 tests, all passing; `swift test` on macOS: 605 UI tests (one skipped), 945 Core, 140 Index and 247 Apple, all passing.

Not verified: by hand in the running app, on the iPad simulator (the simulator panel's device access was not granted during the session) or on the Mac; Increase Contrast and a vault theme's syntax colors on screen; a physical iPad's typing latency in a long code block.

## The Mac shows a block's source only while the note has focus; quotes, "Edit Source", folded callouts and properties on the iPad: 2026-10-16

On the Mac, the markup and a block's source showed at the cursor whether or not the note had keyboard focus, and a note opened from the file list keeps its cursor at its start: a note starting with a callout showed it as `> [!info]` source, with its bars, until a click in the note. The Mac's editor now reveals them only while its text view is the first responder, as the iPad does while the note is edited. `UiEditorMacRenderedBlocksTests.testABlockAtTheCursorShowsItsSourceOnlyWhileTheNoteHasKeyboardFocus`; the Mac editor tests that move the cursor now give the text view focus first. In the Mac app (a review copy on a scratch vault, captured in the background): `10b Unclosed fence.md` and `08 Properties.md` chosen in the file list draw their callouts.

A quoted line that wraps started its next lines at the margin, where the quote's bar is drawn over the first letter ("render", "layout"); it showed on both platforms whenever a callout or quote showed its source. The wrapped lines now start under the text, past the `> ` and a list item's marker (`MarkdownTextStyler`, a hanging indent). `UiStylingFixTests.testWrappedQuotedLinesStartUnderTheirText` fails without the change and passes with it; seen in the iPad simulator on `02 Lists and tasks.md`'s callout source.

The "</>" Edit Source button beside every rendered block is gone, so blocks are as wide as in reading view; "Edit Source" is in each block's menu (a long press on the iPad, a secondary click on the Mac) and among its VoiceOver actions, which also reaches a rule's source. Seen in the iPad simulator: a long press on a callout shows "Edit Source", which shows its source.

On the iPad, a tap on a folded callout's title did nothing, in Live Preview and in reading view: Textual covers a title's text with its own link tap, which took the tap from the fold button. A title without a link now leaves the tap to the fold button (a title with a link still follows the link; its symbol, chevron and the rest of the row fold it). In Live Preview, the text view's own gestures also took touches on a rendered block's view or a fold button before SwiftUI did; over them only its scrolling and zooming begin now, and a touch there no longer counts as touching the note. A drag that started on a property ended with the note editing, its cursor at its start and the properties shown as YAML with the keyboard up: when a block's view scrolled away, UIKit handed the first responder inside it to the text view (seen in an lldb backtrace: `UITextView becomeFirstResponder` from `removeFromSuperview` in `LivePreviewRenderedBlocks.detachViews`). A block's view now makes whatever inside it has the keyboard give it up before it leaves (`RenderedBlockHost.removeFromTextView`). Checked in the iPad simulator ("Graphite Shared Editor iPad", iPad Pro 11-inch (M5), iOS 27.0): the folded callouts of `Lecture_L01_Shannon.md` open on a tap on their title; the property checkbox of `08 Properties.md` toggles (it did not before) and the file changes only on that line; drags starting on `12 Everything at once.md`'s properties scroll the note with no keyboard, where the same drags showed the YAML before. `LivePreviewBlockPlacementTests.testADragStartingOnThePropertiesScrollsTheNote` (hosted) passes, but it passed before the change too: synthesized touches reach UIKit's gesture recognizers but not SwiftUI's controls, so the tap paths are checked by hand only.

Ran: the whole hosted suite (342 tests, one skipped, none failed) and `swift test` on macOS (602 UI tests, one skipped, 916 Core, 140 Index, 247 Apple; no failure). Not verified: a physical iPad and Apple Pencil; a callout title with a link (tap on its text follows the link, not tested); a property's text field being typed in while it scrolls away (it now ends editing); the Mac editor's focus change with a real click in the window (the review copy was driven in the background, which cannot give the text view focus). Seen once and not reproduced in three tries: after toggling the properties checkbox on the iPad, the note's opening `---` was saved as an em dash, which turned the frontmatter into text; `NoteProperties.replacingFrontmatter` keeps `---` for the same edit, and the cause is unknown.

## PDFs on the Mac, and the sidebar's bars and property values drawn as the Mac draws them: 2026-10-16

PDFs opened as a blank page on the Mac, "Page 1 of 1" in the title. The PDF view was there, with its document and zoom, but laid out 0 points tall: the pane's bar above the page has content only on the iPad, and on macOS 27 an inset with no content takes all the height (measured in a small SwiftUI program: under an empty inset, a view keeps 0 of 300 points; under an inset whose content is `if false`, 300). The inset is now applied on the iPad only. `UiPdfViewsTests.testThePageTakesThePanesHeightOnTheMac` (macOS package tests, the whole pane in a window) fails without the fix (0 points) and passes with it; in the Mac app, captured on screen, a test PDF of a title and fourteen lines shows its page.

On the Mac, the sidebar's picker had a white box behind it, reaching up under the toolbar's buttons, and the vault switcher a white band: both were drawn on the bars' material, which matches the iPad's bars and is opaque white on the Mac. They now sit on the sidebar's material, with the system's scroll edge effect (`sidebarBar`); the iPad keeps its material. Property values were bordered boxes on the Mac, with an empty box after each list and steppers beside dates: they are plain text and the date a field without steppers, as the iPad already drew them. Checked, captured on screen in the Mac app: a note with a text and a date property, and the owner's vault (24,712 files) with a note of three properties, two of them lists, and the file tree scrolled under the picker. In the iPad simulator (iPad Pro 13-inch (M4), iOS 18.0): the note's properties and the sidebar look as before. `swift test` on macOS: 600 UI tests (one skipped), 916 Core, 140 Index and 247 Apple tests, no failure; hosted iPad tests (`LivePreviewBlockPlacementTests`, `LivePreviewRevealTests`, `EditingContinuityTests`, `ReadingTasksAndEquationsTests`, `PencilWorkspaceTests`, `PDFLinkFollowingTests`, iPad Pro 11-inch (M5), iOS 27.0): 57 of 57, the keystroke test at 15.2 ms this time. Not checked: a list of search results or other panels under the Mac's picker, dark appearance, and editing a property value by hand on the Mac.

## One styling for the iPad's and the Mac's editors: 2026-10-16

Both editors had their own copy of the styling that follows edits and the selection: following character edits from the text storage, finding the rendered blocks, restyling the whole note or an edit's lines, the markup the selection reveals, and the heights rendered blocks report. They now share `MarkdownEditorStyling`, which works on either text view (`MarkdownStyledTextView`); each editor keeps when to style and what is its own (iPad: folds, keyboard, Pencil; Mac: the properties kept out of the cursor's reach). Behavior is meant to be unchanged; two details now follow one rule: a restyle sets the selection back only when the styling moved it (the iPad always set it), and a whole-note restyle takes the markup the selection reveals at that moment (the iPad's callers did so themselves).

The first version re-read the iPad's folds on each keystroke: the folds were found in one copy of the text and the blocks in another, so the next edit's "text before" never matched the folds' and they were found again. In the hosted keystroke test (`testLongNoteRestyleCostWithAndWithoutATheme`, 5,000 lines) a keystroke took 24.6 ms instead of about 17 ms; the folds now take the blocks' snapshot. That test's limit is 16.7 ms, and at the time of this change it fails on this branch and before it alike: this branch 16.62 to 16.95 ms in six runs, the commit before it 16.94 and 17.28 ms in two runs made the same hour (16.29 ms in an earlier run). Neither is the one-frame budget the test asks for on this simulator now.

Checked: the hosted iPad editor tests (the four classes above), 39 of 40, the one failure being that keystroke test; `swift test` on macOS: 599 UI tests (one skipped), 916 Core, 140 Index and 247 Apple tests, no failure. In the iPad simulator (iPad Pro 13-inch (M4), iOS 18.0), the note with properties, a table and a formula: text typed at the end of the paragraph above the table, with bold, moved the table and the formula down; undo took it back and the note's file was the same as before; the table's "Edit Source" showed its Markdown and a tap in the paragraph brought the table back; the main thread idle afterwards. (The simulator's text input pasted "é" as "√©", already so in the simulator's clipboard.) In the Mac app, drawn off screen: the same note and `L'italien.md` scrolled 600 points, as before.

## One rendered-block engine for the iPad and the Mac: 2026-10-16

The iPad's and the Mac's editors each had their own copy of the rendered blocks' bookkeeping: which blocks are concealed, the heights their lines reserve, the views placed on TextKit's viewport, the pool of views kept off screen and their refresh. Both now use one engine, `LivePreviewRenderedBlocks`, with `UIHostingController` or `NSHostingView` as the view; what stays each platform's is when a placement pass runs, folds (iPad), and when the properties show their YAML (see `Docs/Architecture.md`). Where the two copies differed, the engine keeps one behavior, which changes the iPad in these ways: the properties keep one view while they are edited (as on the Mac), a block's view takes the height its lines reserve in the text now and a height measured since by another editor or at a new width restyles the block, the properties start from their view's last height rather than an estimate, estimates are kept for the width they were made at, and kept heights are forgotten past four times the note's blocks plus 64 rather than twice. The Mac now keeps the reserved height when a report is within a point of it, as the iPad did.

Checked: the hosted iPad editor tests (`GraphiteIntegrationTests`: `LivePreviewBlockPlacementTests`, `LivePreviewRevealTests`, `EditingContinuityTests`, `ReadingTasksAndEquationsTests`, iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27.0), 40 of 40 before the change and 40 of 40 after it; `swift test` on macOS: 599 UI tests (one skipped), 916 Core, 140 Index and 247 Apple tests, no failure. In the iPad simulator (iPad Pro 13-inch (M4), iOS 18.0), a note with properties, a table and a formula: all three drawn in the column; the table's "Edit Source" shows its Markdown with the cursor at its start and the formula moved down below it, a tap in the paragraph above brings the table back, and the app's main thread is idle afterwards. In the Mac app (drawn off screen, the session locked): the same note, and `L'italien.md`'s conjugation tables scrolled 600 points, in place. Not checked by hand: editing a property's field on the iPad (the shared view kept across edits), a rotation, and a memory warning.

## Tables, math, callouts and the other rendered blocks in the Mac's Live Preview: 2026-10-16

The Mac's Live Preview showed tables, display math, callouts, embeds, bases and rules as Markdown; it now draws them as the iPad does, with the same views, each over its concealed source. A block shows its source while the cursor is in it, from the arrow keys or a click, and a click on its view puts the cursor at its start. Only the blocks TextKit has laid out have views; views of blocks scrolled away or being edited are kept and shown again without being built again.

Checked: `UiEditorMacRenderedBlocksTests` (macOS package tests, the AppKit editor in a window): a table away from the cursor is concealed and its view sits in the text column below the line before it, the table's last line reserving the view's measured height; the cursor in the table shows its source and removes the view at once, and leaving it brings the same view back; revealing a block's source puts the cursor at its start; of 40 math blocks only those near the top have views, scrolling to the last one gives it a view, and the blocks scrolled away give theirs up; typing after a table keeps its view, and a line typed above it moves the same view down; a callout, display math and a rule are rendered and fenced code is not. `UiEditorMacPropertiesTests` pass on the shared mechanism, and the properties' view stays in place, the same view, when a property edit changes the frontmatter. In the window (drawn off screen, the session locked): notes with a table, a formula and a callout; two notes copied from the owner's vault (`L'italien.md`, three conjugation tables; a terminal setup note, with tables and rules) at their top and scrolled 600 points, the tables in place; a red spelling mark drawn above each table (the checker marking the concealed source) is gone. A scroll wheel's turn over a table's view scrolls the note, as over text. `swift test` on macOS: 598 UI tests (one skipped), 916 Core, 247 Apple tests, no failure; the iPad simulator app builds.

Bullets, task boxes, quote bars, the " > " of `[[Note#Heading]]` and inline math are now drawn over their hidden markup on the Mac too, by the iPad's layout fragment made to draw through AppKit as well as UIKit, and a click on a drawn box checks or unchecks its task. `UiEditorMacEditorTests`: the markers are drawn or hidden away from the cursor and the Mac's paragraphs are drawn by that fragment (this test said the markers stayed visible before); a click on a box toggles it and can be undone, the cursor stays where it was, text is not a box, and the box of the cursor's line, which shows as `[ ]`, takes no click. In the window: a note with a list, a nested item, two tasks, a two-line quote, a link to a heading and a formula, in light and dark (a checked box was drawn without its mark until the symbol was tinted in one color); a click on a box checked it, written to the file. `swift test` on macOS after this step: 599 UI tests (one skipped) and 916 Core tests, no failure; the iPad simulator app builds. In the iPad simulator (iPad Pro 13-inch, with the launch fix below), the same note shows its bullets, nested item, boxes with the checked one's mark, quote bars, the heading separator and the formula drawn as before.

The iPad app froze at launch from the Mac Settings window's change on (`abef6fd`): its root view gave the sidebar a binding made anew at each update, which SwiftUI took for a change, so the sidebar updated again without end and the first frame was never drawn (main thread at 99 % of a core, white screen, in the iPad simulator). Found by building each commit since `1066296` in the simulator and launching it: `78f8d53` and `2183d7b` settle at launch, `abef6fd` does not; with the sidebar given the stored state's binding again, the app at this change settles and shows its welcome screen.

The Mac app now requires macOS 27, on Apple silicon only, as the owner's Macs run: the rendered blocks are placed each time TextKit reports a layout of the viewport, and the fallback for earlier systems (placing them as the view scrolled, and passes repeated until TextKit had laid the text out) is gone. Checked: `UiEditorMac` tests (29) and the whole `swift test` on macOS (599 UI tests, one skipped, and 916 Core tests), the Mac Release app built for macOS 27, and the iPad app launched in the simulator, which settles and shows its last vault. The new target makes AVFoundation report deprecations in the video recording files (`VideoRecording.swift`, `MoviePartWriter.swift`), whose code the iPad shares; it is unchanged.

Not verified: by hand in the app (a click on a table, arrows into and out of a block, scrolling a long note with the trackpad); the pointer's shape over a block's view; embeds of PDFs, audio and video, bases and plugins' code blocks on the Mac; a block whose view changes height while it is on screen, as an image loading; a vault theme's shapes (quote boxes, heading ornaments, tag pills), which the fragment now draws on the Mac too; VoiceOver.

## The Mac interface, made a Mac app's: 2026-10-16

The owner asked for an interface as pleasant and quick as Apple Notes, after NetNewsWire's. It changes in steps, each checked in Graphite's real window: a scratch app outside the repository hosts the Mac's scenes (`GraphiteMacScenes`) on a sample vault, makes its window the key window, runs a script of steps (menu commands, clicks, ⌘ shortcuts sent through the application) and captures each window.

- Settings is a window of its own, opened with ⌘, from the app menu, laid out like System Settings; the sidebar's Settings button is gone on the Mac. Checked in that window: the window opens at 900 by 588 points with every page's name whole, ⌘W closes it (it did nothing before the fix; a first fix in the menu bar's ⌘W closed the main window, which quits the app, and was replaced), ⌘W in the main window still closes the open tab, and "Manage Vaults…" closes Settings and shows the vault list in the main window. `swift test` for the root view, Settings, commands, focus mode and the file tree passes; the Mac (Release, arm64) and iPad simulator apps build.

- The graph view's sheet takes the page size, as the map's does: it had no size of its own and was drawn 470 by 152 points, its nodes squeezed into a line (captured before and after). The welcome screen and the vault list are grouped forms like System Settings': the welcome screen's column no longer has a background of its own running down the window, and creating or opening a vault are rows of the form rather than gray buttons of uneven widths; the vault list's sheet is 480 by 421 points instead of leaving half its height empty. Checked in the window: a click on "Create New Vault" on the welcome screen opens its form.
- The sidebar's panel picker and the right sidebar's no longer write their own names, "Sidebar" and "Panel", before their symbols on the Mac; each takes the whole width (captured before and after).
- The file tree is AppKit's outline view on the Mac (`MacFileTree`), and the search field moves to the toolbar, as in Notes and Mail, its results filling the sidebar. `UiFileTreeNativeMacTests` (macOS package tests): 25,000 root files list in 0.2 s; a note's muted extension is drawn in the secondary color (it was drawn in the name's color until the cells stopped using the table's `textField`; the test failed before the fix); folding and unfolding a folder in the tree is remembered; a dragged row is read by the editor as a link to the file; a drop on a file moves into its folder, outside the rows to the root, and an item is refused in its own folder or in itself (files moved on disk, with the index read); a row's menu has the sidebar's items. `UiFileTreeMacTests` passes on the outline view unchanged. In the window (drawn off screen once the session locked): folders fold, nested folders indent, the open note's row is selected once its folder is expanded, a search shows its results in the sidebar and clearing it brings the tree back, ⇧⌘F focuses the toolbar's field with the search operators listed. The owner's vault in the Release app (arm64), relaunched three times each, the session locked so the window was not drawn on screen: the main thread used 2.3 s of CPU until it calmed, about 6.5 s after launch, with SwiftUI's list, and 1.05 s with the outline view; in a sample of the launch, the tree's code took 36 of 2,028 main thread samples, the voices' list for Read Aloud 90 and the installed fonts' lookup 74.
- Launching no longer lists the system's voices and installed font families on the main thread. Read Aloud lists its voices the first time something needs them (`ReadAloudSessionTests.testTheVoicesAreListedOnlyOnceSomethingNeedsThem`: none before, once after, again when asked); the Mac asks for a vault's font family alone, ignoring case, in the system's spelling, as the whole list answers (`UiInstalledFontFamiliesMacTests`). Measured outside the app on this Mac: the first list of voices 99 ms, the first list of families 105 ms, one family 5 ms. In a sample of the Release app's launch on the owner's vault, neither appears on the main thread any more; its main thread CPU time went from 1.05 s to 1.00 s (three launches each), most of what is left being the window's layout, of which the toolbar's is the largest part.
- Explanations under the Settings window's form sections are set under the section's leading edge, in line with its header, as System Settings sets them (`formFooterAlignment`): the Mac's grouped forms set footers flush right, the place of a button, which left paragraphs ragged on the left (drawn before and after). Other sheets keep the system's placement, so the change stays within the Settings pages' files. The iPad's are unchanged.
- The sidebar's compose button makes a note with a click on the Mac, as Notes' does; the arrow beside it offers notebooks, bases, canvases, the daily note and folders. The iPad keeps the menu. The new note sheet takes its form's height on the Mac, 470 by 241 points instead of 381 (a notebook's keeps room for its paper options). Checked in the window: a click on the button opens the new note sheet.

Not verified: Settings opened from the app's own menu bar by hand, and a plugin asking for its settings tab while the Settings window is closed; the graph's sheet on a vault of thousands of notes; in the file tree, a drag with the pointer (the drop's rules are tested through the outline view's data source), a menu item chosen from a row's menu, Share in that menu, typing a name to jump to a row, and VoiceOver.

## The note's properties above its text on the Mac: 2026-10-16

The Mac's Live Preview showed a note's frontmatter as YAML; the owner asked for the properties as Obsidian shows them. They now appear in the properties view the iPad uses, at the top of the note, over their concealed YAML; editing one rewrites the frontmatter through the session, as on the iPad, and can be undone. The cursor never stays in the hidden YAML: a note opened at its start puts it on the first line of text, and a backspace there does not join the properties' closing line to the text. "Edit Source" shows the YAML, with the cursor on the first property, until the cursor leaves it. Without Live Preview or with the Properties view off, the YAML stays styled text. Finding the frontmatter after an edit reads only the start of the note, not every line.

Checked: `UiEditorMacPropertiesTests` (macOS package tests, the AppKit editor in a window): the YAML's lines are concealed and the text below is not; the properties view sits at the top of the text column at its width, a click there reaches the view, and the YAML's last line reserves the view's height; once laid out, the view's measured height replaces the estimate (five properties); a height measured earlier is reserved in the text as well as given to the view (this test failed on the first version, which gave the view a height the text did not reserve); a cursor or a selection moved into the YAML starts after it, Select All keeps it, and a backspace at the start of the text leaves the note unchanged; "Edit Source" shows the YAML and takes the cursor until it leaves, and the view comes back; a property changed through the view reaches the editor in place, under the same view, and undo restores the note; Source mode and the Properties view turned off leave the YAML as text, and turning the view off and on restyles it. The Release app (arm64) on the owner's vault, note `1LDK.md` (captured on screen): its ten properties, links and lists drawn in the column, the YAML hidden. On this change, `swift test` on macOS: 584 UI tests (one skipped), 916 Core, 140 Index and 246 Apple tests, no failure; the iPad simulator app builds.

Not verified: editing a property by hand in the Mac app (typing in a field, adding one, choosing a type); the pointer's shape over the view; VoiceOver; the light appearance on screen. Not handled: Up Arrow from the first line of text does not move into the properties, as it does in Obsidian; and in a note that is only frontmatter without a final line break, typing after it joins its closing line, which turns the YAML back into text.

## Map View, a core plugin: the vault's places and the open note's: 2026-10-16

Asked by the owner as something as present as the graph view, beyond Bases' map. A note is placed by the property the vault's Geocoding Properties plugin is set to write coordinates into, then the one the Map View plugin is set to read, then `coordinates` (Bases' map view, Obsidian's Maps plugin) and `location` (Map View's default), each as `[lat, lng]` or `"lat, lng"`; the map only reads them. The owner's vault writes `Coordonnées: lat,lng` through Geocoding Properties: 4,749 notes have the property, about 4,200 with a value. Settings › Core Plugins › Map View turns it off. View › Map View and the palette's "Map view: Open map view" show every placed note, with a path filter and Show All; "Map view: Open local map" and the right sidebar's map panel show the open note and the notes one link away. Nearby notes gather into one numbered marker; choosing it zooms in on them, choosing a note opens it. Notes are named without their extension, and the map frames them with room for their markers. On the Mac the whole-vault map is a page-sized sheet; on iPadOS it covers the screen, as the graph view does.

Checked: `PlacedNotePropertyNamesTests` (Core): the plugins' properties first, a turned-off property, repeated names, and unreadable or oversized settings. `VaultPlacesTests` (Index): both default properties in both forms, `coordinates` read first, and no place for an address, a mapping, decimal commas or a latitude past 90; the owner's `Coordonnées: lat,lng` with and without a space, and none for an empty value; a place moves with its property and goes with it. `UiMapViewMacTests` (macOS package tests, MapKit's map view hosted): the vault map has a marker for each of three placed notes, and selecting one opens its note; the local map has the open note and the note it links to, not a placed note it does not link to. A scratch app showing both screens on a vault of eight places (captured on screen): seven European notes gather into one marker beside Tokyo, and the local map frames Paris and its two linked notes. On this change, `swift test` on macOS: 574 UI tests (one skipped) and 139 Index tests, no failure; the iPad simulator app builds.

The owner's vault itself, indexed read-only by the scratch app: the whole-vault map has 4,428 placed notes, its markers 0.57 s after the screen appeared (Debug build), grouped by region (2,523 over France and western Europe, 438 in North America), lone notes named on the map (captured on screen).

Not verified: the map by hand on the Mac or the iPad (markers chosen with a pointer or a finger, the right sidebar's panel in the app); VoiceOver on markers. 

## A vault with tens of thousands of files at its root opens on the Mac: 2026-10-15

Opening a vault of 24,680 entries at its root (36,392 files) froze the Mac app: the main thread stayed at 100 % in `NSOutlineView insertItemsAtIndexes` for more than four minutes and the files never showed (`sample` of the Debug build). The sidebar's outline view looks up every row as it inserts rows (`_shouldHaveFlatIndentation`), and SwiftUI found each `VaultEntryRow` only by making all the rows before it: a row's number of views was known only once it was made, a folder's row also drawing its expanded contents.

`FileTreeRows` now lists one row per file or folder shown, an expanded folder's contents following it (the contents its row lists, and the folder a drag is over, are kept in `FileTreeContents`), and marks each row `.equatable()`, which makes it a single view. The wrapper hides every list trait set inside it, so the selection tag (with `selectionDisabled` for folders), insets, separator and a drop target's background are set outside it, and so is the row's `draggable` (its preview is `VaultItemDragPreview`). "Copy Vault Path" and "Copy Graphite URL" now copy on the Mac; they copied only with UIKit.

The first versions kept the tag, then the drag, inside the wrapper, and the owner found that clicking a file opened nothing (2026-10-16). It was found by clicking the real sidebar: a scratch app outside the repository hosts `VaultSidebar` (`@testable import GraphiteUI`) in a window that becomes the key window, as the test process's cannot, and sends it mouse clicks. With the tag inside `.equatable()`, an opened file's row was not selected; with `draggable` inside it, the outline view's click selected nothing (removing `draggable` alone fixed it, removing `dropDestination` alone did not, and handing the drag to the outline view with `itemProvider` did not either). With both outside: a click on a file selects and opens it, a click on a folder expands it, and an opened file's row is selected.

Checked: the Mac app (Debug, arm64) with that vault: the files show and the main thread is idle 7 s after launch, 2.5 s of its CPU time in all, the index's threads doing the rest. `UiFileTreeMacTests` (macOS package tests): the real sidebar with 2,000 files at the root and two nested folders; the outline view's rows follow expanding, folding and expanding again, nested contents included, in about 1.3 s (three runs), and the same test with the previous sidebar fails at about 20 s; an opened file's row is the outline view's selected row, which fails with the tag inside the wrapper. A click itself cannot be synthesized there: the test process's window never becomes the key window. The iPad simulator app builds.

Not verified: that a file dragged onto a folder moves there, on the Mac or the iPad (a drag session cannot be synthesized by these checks); the context menus by hand; the iPad's file tree by hand and the hosted iPad suite.

## A PDF page that springs back after every drag and pinch: 2026-10-13

The owner filmed alpha 6cff25c (IMG_6610; iPad Pro 11-inch, landscape, Graphite in split view beside another app, sidebar open, `2402.18736v2.pdf` page 2 with ink at about 250 %, writing): every drag and pinch moved the page a little and it sprang back; switching tabs and back revived it. No Read Aloud was playing.

Cause in split view, reproduced in the simulator with that build (the owner's PDF, a real landscape tile made with the window's own controls): the window was laid out about 1,065 points wide in a tile of about 600 (the window group's default size, which iPadOS 27 took as the window's minimum and maximum, fixed in 4e2bc38), so the PDF view, sized for the wider window, ran past the screen's edge. PDFKit's scroll view was as wide as that view, so the page's right side, off screen, could not be scrolled into view: a drag toward it had no room, rubber-banded and sprang back (the owner, watching the simulator: "the PDF is shifted, and if you try to go to the right side of the PDF it will be annoying"). The PDF's width also changed while the window was touched there (745, 1,065, 1,075, 1,115 points in the trace; some of it may be the window moving under the simulated finger), and at each change that build's refit put back the place and zoom it had kept, with no regard for a finger on the page (now `isMovedByPerson`, ed332ae). With this branch in the same tile: a 600-point scene, compact, the page fitting it; at about 290 % the page scrolled to its right edge (offset 1,200 of 1,800 − 600) and stayed.

A second cause, found while tracing, with Read Aloud following the word read on a PDF: following revealed the word at every update of the PDF's view, and moving the page reports the word's new place to the floating player, which updates the view: the page went back and forth between two offsets 20 points apart without end, the app at 98 % CPU, every pinch taken back at once and every drag taken back five seconds later, when following came back by itself. Fixed (see `Architecture.md`, Read Aloud): each word is revealed once, never while a finger or Apple Pencil is on the pages; a pinch pauses following as a scroll does; on a PDF following waits for Back to Reading. Paused, stopped or not reading, nothing follows.

`PDFPaneHealthMonitor` also counts a gesture whose result something put back within a second, so a page that springs back is recorded and its view built again.

Checked (hosted, iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, real finger touches through UIKit's event path, `SynthesizedTouches`, using the private calls KIF uses, test target only):
- `PDFGestureWinsTests.testADragAndAPinchOnAZoomedPageStayWhereTheyLeaveIt`: the whole workspace, page 2 at 250 %, writing: a drag, a pinch and a save each leave the page still for two seconds.
- `testWhileReadAloudPlaysADragAndAPinchStay`: reading page 2 aloud with the real voice: a pinch zooms and pauses following, a drag and a save, then six seconds untouched while reading moves on; Back to Reading brings the word into view, after which following never scrolls back up; paused, a drag stays. Fails with the previous follow code ("A pinch pauses following").
- `testInATiledWindowThePageZoomsAtEveryCornerAndEveryEdgeCanBeReached`: windows of 417 × 1194, 597 × 834 and 694 × 834 (split view halves and a two-thirds tile): the PDF inside the window; pinches at the four corners of the page shown, in and out, each kept for a second; drags to the page's right, left, bottom and top edges until each is on screen and stays; the first page's top and the last page's bottom clear at the document's ends.
- `testInATiledWindowStrokesAtEveryCornerAndMarginOfAZoomedPageReachTheFile`: in the 417- and 597-point windows at 250 %, at each corner and margin of page 2, 6 points from its edge, the touch reaches page 2's canvas, and a stroke there is in the saved file at that place (screenshots at 250 % and of the whole page). PencilKit draws nothing from a synthesized touch, so the stroke is handed to the canvas at the touched point.
- `testDragsThatSomethingPutsBackAreRecordedAndThePDFIsBuiltAgain`: three drags each put back 0.3 seconds later write `PDFStoppedTakingTouches-*.json` and build the view again. `PDFPaneHealthTests` (macOS): when a result counts as undone.
- In the simulator by finger (real touches): Read Aloud on the owner's PDF with each word revealed once (before the reveal scrolled the pages itself): a pinch and a drag kept with "Back to Reading" shown, Back to Reading brought the word into view, 8 % CPU while reading (98 % before). With the final code, in the right half of the landscape screen: a pinch at the page's top right corner and a two-finger drag to its right edge stayed, the owner's margin notes fully on screen; a finger stroke in the text drew. Finger strokes begun about 20 points from the window's right edge, and one begun 60 points in and drawn toward it, did not draw, and the window was resized: iPadOS takes drags at a window's edge before Graphite sees them. Whether it does so with Apple Pencil on the iPad is not known.

Not established: the owner's iPad (needs a build installed); Apple Pencil strokes at the page's edges (finger strokes stood in, through the same hit-testing); whether the iPad's split view gives the same widths as the simulator's; ink outside the page's box, which a PDF page cannot hold, so the margins tested are the page's own.

## Freezes: the owner's reports read, ink kept, a PDF that stops taking touches, evidence on the device: 2026-10-13

The owner's iPad (alpha Release builds) "often freezes". The reports copied from it, symbolicated by the system (frames of Graphite's own code are named in them), all threads read:
- 10-10 20:33 and 20:34 (0x8BADF00D, swiped away): a note's editor dismantled while it had the keyboard; the keyboard leaving laid the window out, SwiftUI made the note's next editor inside that dismantling, and handing the text view over changed the first responder inside a SwiftUI update (`NativeMarkdownEditor.Coordinator.suspend` → `viewWasMade` → `_UIHostingView._didChange(toFirstResponder:)` → `UpdateStack::update`). Fixed on 10-12 (db36718); the current code waits for the dismantled view to let go.
- 10-12 18:50 and 18:52 (one build; 18:52 a 10-second scene-update watchdog in the foreground): SwiftUI text layout, then `Character.hashValue` under `MarkdownPreview.body` while the keyboard's accessory changed. The reading view hashed the whole note at each update, and each word read aloud updated the pane and the whole reading view. Not reproduced as a freeze: in a hosted test a 0.8 MB note read aloud in the reading view kept the main thread busy 20 % of the time with the previous code; now 3 %, and under 1 ms per word instead of 19 (see `Architecture.md`, Responsiveness and freeze evidence).
- 10-12 23:31 (swiped away): `PKToolPicker setVisible:forFirstResponder:` from `PDFAnnotationCoordinator.updateToolPickerVisibility` in a PDF view update, inside PencilKit's map of tool pickers. Checked that a PDF view built again six times lets go of its coordinator and palette (`MainThreadResponsivenessTests.testPDFViewsBuiltAgainLetGoOfTheirPalettes`); no loop or leak found, cause not established.
- 10-12 20:22 to 20:52: crashes of MLX (`_mlx_error`, `raw_ptr`) in the neural voice benchmark screen, not freezes.
- 10-13 13:43 and 13:54: no report; the app ended when an lldb attach was tried. The owner then described and filmed the 13:54 kind: the PDF takes no touch at all (no scroll, no zoom) while the rest of Graphite works, switching tabs and back revives it, and strokes drawn just before are lost.

Ink: a page scrolled away and back while Graphite kept its canvas got a new canvas, restored from the page's last saved record, so strokes drawn since the last save vanished from the page and from the file at the next stroke on it. Fixed (the kept canvas goes into PDFKit's new overlay). Every canvas's unrecorded ink is now recorded at release, before every save and before the view is built again.

The PDF that takes no touches was not reproduced: the simulator cannot send Apple Pencil touches. Palm rejection, which every scroll, zoom and tap of a page waits for, was driven with stand-in touches: a Pencil touch cancelled, one whose end went elsewhere, and a finger while the Pencil was down all let fingers scroll again with the previous code; a Pencil touch object left going without change, or given to a later finger, kept every finger a resting hand with the previous code, and no longer does. Whether an iPad does either is not known. `PDFPaneHealthMonitor` now notices a PDF that stops answering, writes what it saw and builds the view again.

Checked (hosted, iPad simulator):
- `PDFInkSafetyTests`: strokes survive their page scrolling away and back (fails with the previous code: 0 of 2 strokes on the page's canvas, 1 of 3 editable in the file); strokes made just before switching tabs, and before Graphite leaves the screen, are in the file; ink a canvas never reported is recorded when the view is built again; a recognizer of the PDF left under way with no touch is reset within a few seconds and recorded (`PDFRepaired-*.json`, with the recognizers and the view a touch reaches).
- `PalmRejectionTouchTests`: the five touch sequences above; the last two fail with the previous `PencilActivity`.
- `PDFPaneHealthTests` (macOS package tests): when finger gestures that changed nothing count.
- `MainThreadResponsivenessTests`: a 3-second main-thread stall leaves `MainThreadStall-*.json` with the main thread asleep in the kernel and image UUIDs, then `MainThreadStallEnded-*.json`; a 0.8 MB note in the reading view costs under 1 ms of main thread per word changed (19 before, fails the 8 ms budget); the same note read aloud for real keeps the main thread busy 3 % of the time (20 % before, fails the 12 % budget); a PDF read aloud while written on in a 507-point window: no AttributeGraph cycle while it reads, the main thread never unanswered for 1 second; PDF views built again let go of their palettes.
- `~/.graphite-tools/fetch-diagnostics.sh` run against the iPad, read only: it reports that `Library/Diagnostics` does not exist yet, as no build with it has run; copying another folder of the container showed the files land directly in the destination.

Not established: 11 AttributeGraph cycle warnings printed as that test's window is first laid out, in full hosted runs only (and in `testReadAloudPenReadsFromATappedWordThenExactlyToASecondTap` on alpha), not seen when the app's own window opens the same PDF; the cause of the PDF that takes no touches on the iPad, and whether the monitor's rebuild revives it there; MetricKit payloads on the iPad (they come a day later); the watchdog's frames in a Release build; the 23:31 tool picker freeze; windowed scenes beyond what the window work tried.
## A PDF in a side window settles and stays still: 2026-10-13

The owner's recording on alpha 6cff25c (portrait, Graphite in the right half, about 400 points, beside ChatGPT) showed the PDF's zoom and place changing every half second while the title and Pencil bar stayed cut off on the right. The cut-off is the scene laid out wider than the window (next section). Checked whether the refit added in 6cff25c fights with anything:
- The previous refit, with the 1,200-point default size put back, in the simulator's windowing: a 600-point tile, a pinch, a scroll, and the window made narrower than its restriction allowed (laid out 1,065 wide and cut off): each PDF view width was logged once, with no width changing back and forth and no refit at a pinch or scroll. The instability in the recording was not reproduced, and whether it was the owner pinching on a page laid out wider than the window is not known.
- The refit now chooses the zoom only on a real change of the view's width, never at a layout pass of the same width, and choosing it again for the same width changes nothing. A pinch, scroll or fling under way when the width changes wins: the resize then keeps neither zoom nor place (`isMovedByPerson`). Widths and zooms chosen go to the debug log, category `PDFResize`.
- `PencilWorkspaceTests.testThePDFRefitsWhenTheWindowChangesSize` (hosted) now also counts PDFKit's zoom-change notifications and the PDF view's widths for two seconds after each resize (1194 → 590 → 820 → 375 → 1194 fitting, 590 and 820 zoomed): 0 zoom changes and one width each time, and the zoom chosen at most once per resize.
- `PencilWorkspaceTests.testThePDFFitsGraphitesOwnWindowAndStaysStill` (hosted, new): the workspace in a window at the scene's own size, with the same checks as for every window size above, then two seconds untouched (0 zoom changes, one width) and the zoom a pinch leaves (PDFKit's fitting off, 1.6 times fitting) kept for two seconds, not taken for a resize. Run in the test simulator (full screen, 834×1210, regular) and in the other simulator with Graphite tiled beside the home screen: landscape 411×834 and 578×834, both compact; widths logged `[411]` and `[578]`, 0 zoom changes.
- In that simulator, the window dragged from 1,065 down to 609 and to 411 points: the log shows each width once as the drag passes (the sidebar folding away at the compact width makes one step wider), then nothing; a pinch afterwards keeps its zoom.
- Not established: a pinch or scroll under way while the width changes (hosted tests cannot hold a gesture), portrait windows (the simulator's windows ignore a request to turn), and the owner's iPad, which needs a build installed.

`testReadingPositionSurvivesViewRecreationAndNewHeadingTakesPriority`, flaky in full hosted runs (alpha 6cff25c merged, 2026-10-13, and alpha-lasso, 2026-10-12; both failures were two timed-out waits, 12 seconds), is a Markdown reading view the PDF refit does not touch. The failure fits the test's own scroll, set from UIKit, never being recorded: the restored view then matched the unrecorded position at the top, so only the two waits failed. It passed 25 times alone, 6 times in its class and in a full run here, so the cause was not seen; the test now scrolls again until the view keeps the scroll, checks the scroll was recorded, and its waits report their own line.

## A window tiled beside another app is laid out at its own size: 2026-10-13

The fix above did not help on the owner's iPad (alpha 6cff25c, landscape, Graphite tiled on the right half beside ChatGPT): the page, title, tabs and Pencil bar were all laid out wider than the window and cut off at its right edge. Reproduced in the simulator by turning the scene to landscape (`requestGeometryUpdate`) and tiling the window on the right half with the window controls: the scene reported 1,065 by 834 points in a half-screen tile, and its `sizeRestrictions` were 1,200 by 852 points as both minimum and maximum, set by the window group's `.defaultSize(width: 1200, height: 820)`. Removing the default size on iPadOS (the Mac keeps it): the same tile is a 600 by 834 scene, compact, laid out to its edges (screenshot `shots/x6.png` in the build folder, against `x4.png` before). The hosted tests above set window frames directly and so never saw the scene's size.

Checked: `PencilWorkspaceTests.testGraphitesSceneCanBeAnySizeAndIsLaidOutAtIt` (hosted, the app's own scene turned to landscape and back to portrait: its size restrictions allow 375 points or less and the whole screen, and its windows and root view are laid out at the scene's size); with the previous scene it fails (minimum and maximum 1,200 by 852). Not established: the owner's iPad, which needs a build installed.
