Reported by the owner from the iPad (alpha 8a6ccb8, a window beside ChatGPT, and the same PDF full screen afterwards): the page did not fit the window and ran off one edge with an empty column at the other; the Pencil bar's tools were cut off in the tab row; the toolbar lost Back, Forward and More and clipped the title. Reproduced in a hosted test: a page zoomed by a pinch (PDFKit's own fitting off) in a 1,194-point window stayed 867 points wide in a 590-, 375- or 820-point window. See `Architecture.md`, the fixed tool bar paragraph, for the three changes: the zoom follows the width, the Pencil row has a compact form with a popover, and narrow regular windows fold a PDF's secondary toolbar actions into More.

Checked:
- `PencilWorkspaceTests.testThePDFWorkspaceFitsEveryWindowSize` (hosted): the whole workspace (`GraphiteRootView` with a workspace of its own, a note and a PDF in tabs, writing, fixed bar) in windows of 375×834 (compact), 507×834, 590×834 and 694×834 (compact and regular, as iPadOS gives either by screen and arrangement), 820×1180 and 1194×834 (regular): the page fits the view with no column wider than 40 points; the PDF is inside the window; in regular widths the Pencil bar is whole or compact, never scrolling, and inside the window; every button of the navigation and bottom bars is inside the window and unsqueezed, no button covers the title, which is at least 60 points wide, and the More menu's ellipsis shows. Screenshots at each size are attached.
- `PencilWorkspaceTests.testThePDFRefitsWhenTheWindowChangesSize` (hosted): one window resized 1194 → 590 → 820 → 375 → 1194 with PDFKit's fitting off: the page fits each width; zoomed to 1.5 times fitting, it keeps 1.5 at 590 and 820 with no empty column. With the previous `GraphitePDFView` it fails: the page stayed 867 points wide, and the zoom past fitting grew to 2.2 and 2.6.
- In the simulator (iPadOS 27 windowing, portrait; the simulator cannot rotate, and its windows are at least about 709 points wide): a 709-point window shows the title, Back, Forward, recording and More, the compact Pencil row, its popover (sizes, colors, Shapes, Ruler, Add Image) and More with Show Pages, Go to Page, Find in PDF and Add Page; a page zoomed in that window and then shown full screen keeps its zoom past fitting with no empty column.
- Not established, needing the iPad: landscape halves and thirds with the window's own controls in the toolbar, Slide Over, and windows narrower than the simulator allows; the size class iPadOS 27 gives each of them.

## Ink looks the same while reading and while writing: 2026-10-12

Reported by the owner from the iPad (alpha 8a6ccb8, a screen recording toggling writing every second): the ink changed each time, and lost quality while reading. Reproduced in the simulator: reading drew PDFKit's plain ink annotations (a center line of one width and flat color per stroke) instead of PencilKit's canvas, so the highlighter became a thin opaque line, the pen a uniform heavier line and the pencil a thin smooth gray line, at every zoom. The canvas now shows the ink in both modes, taking touches only while writing (see `Architecture.md`, the Read/Write paragraph). Not a color-space, z-order or rasterization-scale difference: the whole difference was which renderer drew the ink.

Checked: `PencilWorkspaceTests.testInkLooksTheSameWhileReadingAndWhileWriting` (hosted: highlighter, pen and pencil ink on a page, compared in writing and reading at the page's fit zoom and at three times it; under 1 percent of inked pixels may differ). With the previous code it fails: 40 percent of inked pixels changed at 138 percent zoom and 11 percent at 414 percent. In the simulator, screenshots of the same strokes in both modes: at 100 percent and at about 300 percent, 0 pixels differ with this change (before: about 4 percent of the region at 100 percent, highlighter thin and opaque, pencil grain gone). Reading still selects text with a long press over the ink. `testReadingModeRestoresPageTouchesAndKeepsInkWhenReturningToWriting` now expects the canvas to stay shown, not taking touches, while reading. Not tried on the iPad.

## Community plugins: Live Preview, restored views, gestures in copies, more menus, the Mac editor: 2026-10-12

The owner's list for no gaps left in plugin support, in order, on the branch `alpha-plugins-6`. `Community-plugins.md` describes each (How it works › In notes, menus, typing and tabs) and how it was checked (How it was verified › Live Preview blocks, restored views, copies' gestures, menus and the Mac editor).

- Live Preview: plugins' code blocks drawn by their plugin while the cursor is elsewhere (`LivePreviewBlock.Kind.pluginCodeBlock`), `getSectionInfo` looked up in the note each time; post-processors stay reading-view only, as in Obsidian.
- Views restored after a restart, tabs and sidebar panels (`SavedTabLayout.PluginView`, `view.restore`).
- Live views: two on screen swap with a tap, the other showing a picture of itself; each keeps its state; the focused side's takes the page as they appear.
- Copies: right-click and long press (menus), hovering, and drags reach the plugin where it listens (`data-graphite-listens`).
- Menus: `files-menu` through Pick Files for Plugins in the file list, `url-menu` on a web address in the editor.
- Mac editor: plugins' context menu items, `editor-change` and `editor-paste`.
- Excalidraw's embedded notes drawn from Graphite's rendering, now with Obsidian's highlights and callouts and no frontmatter; formulas in plugins' Bases views; TaskNotes' calendar checked.
- Apple Pencil: the plugins' page no longer zooms or bounces, so pinches and strokes reach a live view; Pencil reaches it as WebKit's pen pointer events.

Tests: the whole iPad integration suite, 299 tests, 0 failures. `swift test`: 1,817 tests (877 Core, 137 Index, 246 Apple, 557 UI), one skipped, no failures. Node: 84 runtime tests, all passing. `Community-plugin-API-coverage.md` regenerated, unchanged.

Not verified: Apple Pencil pressure, tilt and hover on a device; the Mac app by hand; drag and drop inside a copy by hand (jsdom only); a plugin with `files-menu` items.

## Read Aloud with downloadable voices, and Read Aloud as a core plugin: 2026-10-12

Branch `alpha-voices` (from alpha 1befb10). What it does is in `Architecture.md` (Read Aloud voices).

**Measured on the owner's iPad Pro 11-inch (3rd generation, M1, 8 GB, iOS 27), Release build, `-GraphiteVoiceBenchmark`, results copied with `devicectl device copy from`** (raw JSON, WAV and logs in `~/.graphite-voice-engine-build/results/`). Real-time factor (RTF) is rendering time divided by audio length; below 1 is faster than reading. iOS let the app use 5.36 GB (`os_proc_available_memory` at launch) without the increased-memory-limit entitlement; the entitlement could not be added because the command-line build has no Xcode account to update the provisioning profile ("Provisioning profile … doesn't include the Increased Memory Limit capability"), so it was not used.

| Voice | Download | Load, warm-up | RTF, cool | RTF, hot ("serious") | Peak footprint | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A Pocket TTS, alba | 236 MB | 0.1 s, 0.5 s | 0.40 | 0.63 over a 5.9-minute note (19 passages, 224 s) | 2.0–4.8 GB before the per-frame evaluation patch (not remeasured on the iPad since; 0.9 GB MLX peak on a Mac after it) | Offered on iPad |
| B Qwen3-TTS 0.6B, Ryan | 1.97 GB | 1.6 s, 4.9 s | 0.77–0.83 | 1.75 over the 7.1-minute note; 3 of 19 passages failed ("Impacting Interactivity", now retried once and decoded in one-second chunks) | 3.3 GB (after the decoder patch; before it, iOS ended the app at 5.45 GB) | Offered on iPad |
| C Qwen3-TTS 1.7B, Ryan | 3.08 GB | 2.3 s, 2.5 s | 1.03–1.08 | not measured; one hot run ended on an MLX graphics error, before errors were caught | 4.3 GB | Offered on iPad, slow |
| D Chatterbox Turbo (8-bit; the blind test used fp16, 3 GB) | 708 MB | 0.7 s, 7.4 s | not measured | 0.67–1.08 | 2.6 GB | Offered on iPad |
| E Dia 1.6B (fp16) + Descript codec | 3.53 GB | 2.1 s, never finished | — | — | over 5.36 GB: iOS ended the app (JetsamEvent) after more than two minutes on the warm-up sentence | Mac only (16 GB or more). On an M5 Max with its voice prompt: RTF 4–6, MLX peak 10.7 GB |

- First MLX use after the first install: the warm-up sentence took 222 s (Metal kernels being compiled); the next launches and later reinstalls took about 0.5 s. A person may see "Preparing… 0%" for a few minutes the very first time.
- Heat: after about an hour of measuring, the iPad stayed "serious" through every run; Pocket TTS slowed from 0.40 to 0.63 and Qwen3-TTS 0.6B from 0.8 to 1.75. After a four-minute rest it was "nominal" again and Pocket TTS rendered at 0.41.
- Background: iOS refuses graphics work in the background. With silent audio keeping the app alive, Pocket TTS on the graphics processor ended the app 7 s after it was sent to the background (a read of a buffer whose computation had failed). MLX on the main processor failed on the first passage on the iPad and ran at RTF 1.55 even on an M5 Max. So rendering stops while the app is inactive and resumes when it returns; reading prepared passages goes on in the background with the `audio` background mode, which is **not yet checked on the iPad** with the screen locked.
- End to end on the iPad: `graphite://read?vault=Vault&file=VLSI3/3_The_CMOS_Inverter` opened the owner's lecture note and Alba read it in High Quality; screenshots `~/.graphite-voice-engine-build/device-shots/pocket-01…07.png` show reading start within about 30 seconds and the highlight moving passage by passage, and the cache held the AAC passages. **The highlight was the passage, not the word:** on the iPad on-device recognition returned no words (the cached timing files had none), while the same AAC file, copied to a Mac, was recognized word by word (68 timed words). Why it fails on the iPad was not found before the iPad disconnected; `-GraphiteVoiceBenchmark alignment` now reports the reason. Qwen3-TTS was not yet run end to end on the iPad, and nobody has listened to the device's output.
- Dia's fixed voice (`DiaVoicePrompt`, from Dia's own blind-test clip E, the expressive passage): on the Mac, prompted output transcribed correctly for "Hello there." (unprompted Dia said only "The") and for the lecture passage; for the short math sentence it dropped the first half. Whether the voice stays the same across passages was not judged by ear.

- Babbling, found by the owner with Qwen3-TTS 0.6B on Graphite's pronunciation list (`~/.graphite-readaloud-build/listen/script.txt`, 52 respelled terms in groups of five): rendered directly it ran 164.8 s (Pocket TTS: 66.7 s), looped "R-D-F-R-D-F" for 30 s, invented "Amen" and made 45 s of noise. Through the new pipeline on the M5 Max Mac (`VoiceHarness terms`, results in `~/.graphite-voice-engine-build/terms/`): **Qwen3-TTS 0.6B 79.7 s** (5 groups clean at once, 3 on the second seed, 4 read by Pocket TTS after two rejections; rejected attempts included "Oh, Amber, I triple E, uh, uh…", "So? Hmm, what?" and "XO or…"), **Qwen3-TTS 1.7B 70.8 s** (8 at once, 3 on the second seed, 1 left to the system voice because the Pocket TTS fallback was also rejected as too long, a false positive on "T T L, P L L, A L U"), **Chatterbox Turbo 55.9 s** (all 12 at once), Pocket TTS alone 71.6 s (all at once). Whisper transcripts of the joined outputs read every group in order with no loop or invented words (Qwen3-TTS 0.6B said "VHDO" for VHDL and "VITA-D" for VDD; Chatterbox "BHDL" and "EMOS"). Not yet run on the iPad.

Simulator "Graphite Voices iPad" (iPad Pro 11-inch (M5), iOS 27.0): screenshots in `~/.graphite-voice-engine-build/`: `sim-read-aloud-settings-dark.png` (Reading Mode, Voices, the fill-in switch, System Voice and Speed, Storage), the Voices page with Download, downloading progress with Pause and Cancel, then Alba installed (Hugging Face download of 236 MB, checksums matched, `.graphite-installed` written), `sim-popover-fallback.png` (reading controls with "Prepared 5%", the fill-in switch, and the Simulator's "needs a real iPad" message while the system voice read), `sim-core-plugins.png`, `sim-plugin-off-dialog.png` (the deletion question anchored to Read Aloud's switch) and `sim-more-menu-plugin-off.png` (no Read Aloud menu when off). The Simulator cannot run MLX at all: the first MLX call ended the app, so the engine touches nothing MLX until a voice loads on a device.

Tests: `swift test` adds `ReadAloudRenderCheckTests` (the real 48-second Qwen3-TTS babbling clip `Tests/GraphiteCoreTests/Resources/QwenBabbling.m4a` rejected as too long and missing its text; clean speech accepted; loops as repeated words and as one repeated syllable; silences inside but not at the ends) and `ReadAloudVoiceTextTests` (written acronyms for autoregressive voices, word respellings kept, ranges and offsets mapped back), `NeuralVoiceManifestTests` (the shipped manifest, addresses, unsafe paths, checksums, duplicates, versions, platform and memory), `ReadAloudAudioCacheTests` (keys, least-recently-used eviction that keeps the document being read, the WAVE writer), `ReadAloudWordAlignmentTests` (exact, missed and misheard words, refusal, playback time, offsets) and `ReadAloudQualityModeTests`, plus the `graphite://read` link in `VaultURITests`. Hosted `HighQualityReadAloudTests` (in `ReadAloudLectureHighlightTests.swift`) run the real preparation, cache, AVAudioEngine player and session with a tone renderer and an evenly spaced aligner: render-ahead and word highlight moving forward, no re-render on a second reading and one re-render after an edit, pause and resume holding the place, waiting with "Preparing… 0%" and the system voice taking over once allowed, and both modes; with a fake voice that babbles at twelve times the expected length, two rejected attempts then Pocket TTS reading the passage's respellings, or, without Pocket TTS, the system voice. Not covered by tests: the background download session, the real MLX engine (device only), SpeechAnalyzer itself, interruptions during High Quality playback, VoiceOver order, the largest text sizes in the new pages.

## Community plugins' file views, Bases views, paste and drop, and splits: 2026-10-12

The four largest gaps of the community plugin sweep, on the branch `alpha-plugins-5`. `Community-plugins.md` describes each (How it works › In notes, menus, typing and tabs) and how it was checked (How it was verified › File views, Bases views, paste and drop, and splits).

- File views: files with an extension a plugin registered, and notes a plugin claims through `setViewState`, open in the plugin's view in a tab, shown live with the runtime's own web view (`CommunityPluginLiveViewContent`, `file.openInView`, `view.live`), so Excalidraw draws by finger and Fountain's editor types; file views save when Graphite leaves the screen (found by the check: a drawing's last 30 seconds were lost when the app ended).
- Bases: plugins' view types show in Graphite's bases (`CommunityPluginBasesViewContent`, `basesView.open`); TaskNotes' Kanban moves a task between status columns.
- Paste and drop: `editor-paste` and `editor-drop` reach plugins first; Link or Card's choice works and two Undos restore the note.
- Splits: `splitActiveLeaf` views open on the other side of Graphite's split (Mind Map).

Tests: Node `file-views.test.js` (4), 81 runtime tests, all passing. The whole iPad integration suite: 299 tests, 298 passing in the full run; `BasesViewsTests.testTheMapFetchesTiles…` timed out under load and passed alone. `swift test`: 1,812 tests (873 Core, 137 Index, 246 Apple, 556 UI), one skipped, no failures.

Not verified: on a device or with Apple Pencil; two live views at once; Excalidraw's embedded notes; TaskNotes' calendar views.

## A sweep of 48 community plugins, and the runtime fixes it led to: 2026-10-12

At the owner's request: install a sample of Obsidian's community plugins through Graphite's own installer and try each in the iPad simulator, so that nothing breaks, then fix in the runtime what is general. The sample, the results per plugin and how each was tried are in `Community-plugins.md` (How it was verified › A sweep of the directory): the 23 most downloaded that the Node checks did not cover and that run on mobile, every plugin by kepano, Contextual Typography, and 18 drawn at random. 16 work, 13 partly, 17 are blocked (by an API or part of the interface Graphite does not give plugins, or by an account the test did not have), Linter fails on its own first-run bug, and Highlights View has no release.

- Safety: each plugin alone and all 46 together never crashed, froze or left Graphite unusable, and the vault's 477 notes stayed byte-identical (checksums before and after). Turning plugins off and removing them restored Graphite.
- Runtime (`Sources/GraphiteUI/Resources/CommunityPluginRuntime`): Node.js and Electron modules are stand-ins until used; plugin code loads from a blob in WebKit so failures name the plugin; ES5 classes; a block that replaces its element, and windows without Obsidian's `.modal` class, are shown; callout and reading-view styles and adopted style sheets in copies; new tab leaves shown at once and laid out before they open, `resize` sent; Obsidian 1.13 setting pages and `Setting.setNavigable`, `setIcon`, `setAction`; `openFile` through `setViewState`; serialized writes to one file; paths outside the vault read as missing, "/" for the root, `ENOENT`; `window.open` and the clipboard through Graphite; notices' buttons; and undocumented members (`hotkeyManager`, `viewRegistry`, a container's `doc`, `vault.config`, `setting.pluginTabs`, `workspace.protocolHandlers`, `Menu.dom`, `coordsAtPos`, `Scope.keys`, `iterateCodeMirrors`, `getFullPath`, `setConfig` and `setTheme` reported).
- Swift: links whose action a plugin registered go to it (`VaultURI.Action.plugin`, `CommunityPluginHost.openPluginLink`); `clipboard.readText`/`writeText` and `notice.tap` answered by the host, with buttons on the notice toast; the vault bridge does not list symbolic links that lead out of the vault; plugin load results and failures in the unified log (`CommunityPlugins`); Settings lists a plugin's desktop-only modules once, as parts that need the desktop app.

Tests: `obsidian-conventions.test.js` (10 Node tests, one per group of fixes; the quick-saves test fails without its fix), `plugin-loading.test.js` updated (requiring a desktop module loads, using it fails), `VaultURITests` (plugin actions). Node: 73 tests, all passing. The whole iPad integration suite (`GraphiteIntegrationTests`, iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27.0): 269 tests, 268 passing; the one failure is `EvenHighlighterTests.testTheTipTurnsAcrossTheStrokeInEveryDirection` (a vertical band 26 points wide where a horizontal one is 24 tall), which alpha's project file had never compiled into the hosted target before `0b225f3`. `swift test` on macOS: 1,733 tests (844 Core, 137 Index, 216 Apple, 536 UI), one skipped, with only the known `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading` failing. `Community-plugin-API-coverage.md` regenerated, unchanged (422 of 442 documented methods: the additions are undocumented members).

Not verified: on a device or with Apple Pencil; sync, AI and account plugins with real accounts; Git commits and pushes, an Importer import, Excalidraw drawing; plugins' views after a restart. No real plugins were added to the Node checks (each must be pinned and built from source).

## Theme shapes and ornaments, and typing in long notes (theme Phase 3): 2026-10-12

Notes now also take a theme's shapes: heading underlines, backgrounds, text before the heading and upper case; quote boxes; callout corners, borders and tint strength; table header text and striped rows; list marker color; tag pill corners and borders (tags are pills by default); checkbox corners, border width and check mark; dashed and dotted rules; code block background, corners and border; highlight corners and text color; and the inline title's color, size, weight and font. The Mac editor keeps a readable column. The plugins' page links the same default variable layer the resolver measures against, with Graphite's look written as Obsidian's base color scale. Design: `Architecture.md` › Markdown views (theme Phase 3, typing cost in long notes) and Storage contracts (Phase 2 paragraph).

Groups each theme changes, measured by the resolver on the assessment's theme files (light / dark): Phase 1's 19 groups, and all 30 groups now drawn. AnuPpuccin counts one more Phase 1 group than before because unresolved links are now dimmed by the theme's `--link-unresolved-opacity` when it sets one.

| Theme | Phase 1 groups (of 19) | All groups (of 30) |
|---|---|---|
| Minimal (default scheme) | 15 / 16 | 21 / 22 |
| Minimal, Nord scheme | 15 / 16 | 21 / 22 |
| Things | 15 / 15 | 20 / 20 |
| AnuPpuccin | 13 / 13 | 19 / 19 |
| Blue Topaz | 17 / 18 | 24 / 25 |
| ITS Theme | 17 / 17 | 25 / 25 |
| Catppuccin | 18 / 18 | 27 / 27 |
| Flexoki | 13 / 13 | 18 / 18 |
| Obsidian Nord | 13 / 14 | 20 / 21 |

None of these themes puts text before its headings; Things underlines its second-level headings, Minimal draws round checkboxes, ITS gives tags and table headers its red. Selector-level styling outside the probed elements is still not drawn.

Typing cost, measured by the hosted test below in the simulator's debug build, a 5,000-line note with headings, tasks, bullets, quotes, tags and highlights, 40 keystrokes typed in its middle (time from the keystroke to restyled, laid-out text):

| | Without a theme | With a shape-drawing theme |
|---|---|---|
| Before (median / slowest) | 16.9 ms / 39.4 ms | 15.6 ms / 30.5 ms |
| After (median / slowest) | 4.3 ms / 10.0 ms | 4.1 ms / 12.4 ms |

The theme was never the cost: finding the note's folding regions on every keystroke was (about 15 ms); see `Architecture.md`. Restyling the whole note when the theme changes takes 66 ms without a theme and 92 ms with one, once per change.

Checks:
- Core tests: folding's list-item reading agrees with the list pattern on 34 lines; folding regions moved across every accepted edit of a sample note (each position, replacing up to 3 characters with 8 replacements, frontmatter and a code block included) equal the regions found again, and an edit in a heading is refused (`NoteFoldingTests`, 2 new).
- macOS tests: a theme's shapes and ornaments are resolved: tag corners and border, highlight corners, heading underline, upper case and `::before` text and color, a 50% checkbox radius, quote background, list marker, inline title color and size, code block corners, callout corners, dashed rules, striped rows and header text (`ThemeTokenResolverTests.testShapesAndOrnamentsAreResolved`); an edit reported as a whole line narrows to the typed character (`UiEditorEditTrackingTests`).
- Runtime test: the plugins' page links the default layer before its own styles, and its semantic colors come from the base scale (`tests/themes.test.js`).
- Hosted iPad tests (`EditingContinuityTests`): `testLivePreviewDrawsTheThemesShapes` (a test theme's tag pill, rounded highlight, heading underline, quote background and round checkbox are set on Live Preview's text); `testLongNoteRestyleCostWithAndWithoutATheme` (the table above; fails if the median keystroke with the theme exceeds 16.7 ms or 1.5 times the median without).
- Simulator ("Graphite Appearance iPad"): the lecture note with Minimal, Things, AnuPpuccin, Blue Topaz and ITS Theme, Live Preview and reading view, light and dark (`~/.graphite-themes-build/shots/phase3/*.png`). Fixed while checking: quote boxes and heading underlines stopped at the end of the text instead of the column; two tags side by side merged into one box; tags lost their pill shape where a theme leaves the radius alone; ITS's white tag and table header text on red were darkened against the white page and became unreadable (text on a theme's own surface is now checked against that surface); measuring two themes at once on the resolver's one web view waited forever (found by the hosted test; measurements now run one at a time with a 15-second limit).
- Hosted suite: 301 tests, 0 failures. `swift test`: 1,816 tests, 0 failures, 1 skipped. Runtime tests: 68, 0 failures.

Not verified: on a device; with VoiceOver; the Mac editor's column and plain tag and highlight backgrounds by hand (built and covered by the shared styler tests only); heading prefixes on a real theme (none of the nine uses one; covered by the resolver test); typing cost in a release build or on a device (the debug simulator numbers above are an upper bound).

## Notes in the vault's Obsidian theme (theme Phase 1): 2026-10-12

Notes now take the vault's Obsidian theme and CSS snippets in Live Preview, Source mode, the reading view, callouts, tables and embedded blocks: colors (page, text, muted, accent), headings (color, size, weight, font, italic), callout colors by type, tags, links by kind (internal, unresolved, external), checkboxes, code (background, color, font), the quote bar, highlights, rules, table borders and header shading, line height and the readable line width. Settings › Appearance › Use the Vault's Obsidian Theme (on by default) keeps Graphite's look when off. Design, guardrails and what is not themed: `Architecture.md` › Markdown views.

How much of each theme is drawn: of the 19 note-styling groups a theme can set per appearance (`ThemeModeTokens`), the groups each theme changes, measured by the resolver on the theme files of the assessment (light / dark); every group a theme changes is drawn natively, except checkbox and tag corner radii and table header text color (drawn since Phase 3, above):

| Theme | Groups changed (light / dark) |
|---|---|
| Minimal (default scheme) | 15 / 16 |
| Minimal, Nord scheme (Minimal Theme Settings' classes) | 15 / 16 |
| Things | 15 / 15 |
| AnuPpuccin | 12 / 12 |
| Catppuccin | 18 / 18 |
| Blue Topaz | 17 / 18 |
| Flexoki | 13 / 13 |
| Obsidian Nord | 13 / 14 |
| Atom | 13 / 13 |

Selector-level styling outside the probed elements (Phase 3's shim), heading ornaments, backgrounds and `::before` glyphs are not drawn.

Checks:
- Core tests (`VaultThemeTokensTests`, 5): computed color formats, values left at the defaults read as nil, sizes as multiples of the text size, unresolved links dimmed, the contrast guard (4.5:1, 7:1 under Increase Contrast, translucent text judged as drawn, readable colors kept), the Read Aloud look-alike check, callout aliases.
- macOS tests (`ThemeTokenResolverTests`, 3): WebKit resolves literals, `var()` chains and fallbacks, `calc()`, `color-mix()`, a theme's own selectors, body classes and inline variables from plugins, per appearance; results are cached by their inputs.
- Hosted iPad test (`EditingContinuityTests.testEditorAndReadingViewTakeTheVaultThemesHeadingAndCalloutColors`): a test theme's heading color (light and dark) and size reach Source mode and the reading view, its warning callout color reaches callouts with a readable title, and the setting off restores Graphite's look.
- Simulator ("Graphite Appearance iPad"): a lecture note with headings, callouts, tags, links, a task list, a table, a quote, inline code, a highlight, a code block, display math and a drawing, before (theme off) and with Minimal in Nord (through Minimal Theme Settings), Things and AnuPpuccin, Live Preview and reading view, light and dark, and Increase Contrast on AnuPpuccin light (links darkened further). Screenshots `~/.graphite-themes-build/shots/p1-*.png`. Fixed while checking: a theme that set a color in one appearance only drew nothing in the other (every color now falls back to Graphite's own); reading view headings lost the theme's size when it named a heading font.
- Hosted suite: 299 tests, 0 failures. `swift test`: 1,812 tests, 0 failures. Runtime tests: 67, 0 failures.

Not verified: on a device; with VoiceOver; Pencil writing over a themed note; the Mac app by hand; Style Settings changes re-resolving notes by hand (the trigger is the plugins' page change, covered by code path only); the cost on a very long note (measured in Phase 3, above).

## Obsidian themes and snippets in community plugins' pages and copies (theme Phase 2): 2026-10-12

Settings › Appearance › Themes and Snippets chooses the vault's Obsidian theme, installs one from Obsidian's theme directory, and turns CSS snippets on and off, written to `.obsidian/appearance.json` (`cssTheme`, `enabledCssSnippets`). The theme and snippets go into the community plugins' page after the plugins' styles, with `app.customCss`, and into the copies of plugin blocks and views together with the page's body classes and inline style, so Style Settings and Minimal Theme Settings work for plugin UIs, Minimal's color schemes included, following light and dark live. Notes keep Graphite's own styling (Phase 1, not started). Design and limits: `Architecture.md` › Storage contracts; what was checked by hand: `Community-plugins.md` › Themes and snippets in the plugins' page.

Checks: Core tests for writing `cssTheme` and `enabledCssSnippets` as Obsidian lays them out, listing themes and snippets, refusing names outside their folders, and the theme directory (`ObsidianAppearanceTests`, 14 in all); a unit test for the accent's hue, saturation and lightness; runtime tests in jsdom (`tests/themes.test.js`, 3; a real Style Settings test in `tests/real-plugins.test.js`; the harness gained `ownerNode` for style sheets, which jsdom lacks and WebKit has): 67 runtime tests pass with the real plugins built. Hosted suite: 298 tests, 0 failures. `swift test`: 1,804 tests, 0 failures. Screenshots: `~/.graphite-themes-build/shots/p2-*.png`.

Not verified: on a device; themes other than Minimal by hand; snippets by hand; memory of many copies each carrying a large theme (the 3 MB cap keeps a theme with fonts in its file out of copies, measured only in the runtime test).

## Fonts merged with the Read Aloud rework: 2026-10-12

The fonts work was merged with alpha at 9695a6c and then 1befb10 (Read Aloud's system voice, reading highlight in both views, Words / Sentences / Off, acronyms; the new laser). Both sides were kept where they met: reading view text and callout titles keep Read Aloud's source tokens and take the vault's text font; the editor keeps the quick font size pinch and reading's follow suspension; Settings has the Font section and the Read Aloud page; on a Mac, Control-scrolling changes the size without stopping the view from following what is read. The highlight is drawn from the text's own layout and its ranges are source ranges, so neither depends on the font. In the simulator, while Read Aloud spoke `Fonts Check.md`, the font and size were changed from outside (Avenir Next 17 to Baskerville 22) and by pinching (to 11 in reading view, and up again in Live Preview): the blue highlight stayed on the word being spoken in both views each time (`~/.graphite-themes-build/shots/m2-reading-trio.png`, `m2-p2`, `m2-lp1`, `m2-lp2`). Hosted suite: 298 tests, 0 failures, 1 skipped. `swift test`: 1,800 tests, 0 failures.

## Fonts and font size, shared with Obsidian's appearance.json (alpha only): 2026-10-12

Settings › Appearance › Font has Obsidian's five settings (Interface Font, Text Font, Monospace Font, Font Size with a reset button, Quick Font Size Adjustment), stored in the vault's `.obsidian/appearance.json`; the vault's Obsidian accent is used until one is chosen in Graphite. This is Phase 0 of the theme assessment plus fonts; `cssTheme` and snippets are read into the model only and nothing draws them. `Architecture.md` (Storage contracts, Markdown views) has the design and the precedence rules.

How it was checked:
- Unit tests (`ObsidianAppearanceTests`, 11): the owner's real `Vault/.obsidian/appearance.json` read key by key; missing, empty and mistyped values; the size range; quoted and multi-family lists; first installed family and generic names; byte-for-byte preservation of unknown keys and of unchanged quoted lists; an Obsidian change made meanwhile kept; atomic write with no file created by a no-op save. Keys and Obsidian's own reading and writing of font lists (`replace(/"/g,"").split(",")`, `join(",")`) were checked against the Obsidian 1.14.4 bundle on this Mac.
- Hosted iPad tests (`EditingContinuityTests`, 5 new): Live Preview body, heading, inline code and code block use the vault's fonts at the vault's size grown by Dynamic Type, with a missing first family skipped; a font changed while editing restyles in place with the cursor, the first responder and the rendered math block kept, and only the changed key is written; reading view sets text in Georgia and inline code in Courier New (measured by rendered width); the pinch recognizer never takes Pencil touches, leaves PencilKit canvases, PDF views and views with their own pinch alone, and follows the setting; pinch sizes step and clamp; vault accent precedence.
- Simulator ("Graphite Appearance iPad", iPad Pro 11-inch (M5), iOS 27): a test-vault note in both views, light and dark and at an accessibility text size; `appearance.json` rewritten from outside while the app ran was followed within seconds (fonts, size, accent); fonts picked from the searchable list and a size dragged were written to the file with every other key kept; pinching changed the size in Live Preview (20 to 28) and in reading view (28 to 15). Screenshots: `~/.graphite-themes-build/shots/`.
- The whole hosted suite: 276 tests, 0 failures, 1 skipped. `swift test`: 1,746 tests; the only failure is the known `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading`.

Not verified: anything on a device or with a real Apple Pencil (the Pencil exclusion is checked through the recognizer's allowed touch types and its delegate policy, not with Pencil hardware); trackpad pinches on iPad; ⌘+, ⌘– and ⌘0 pressed on a hardware keyboard (the commands are built, not pressed); the Mac editor's Control-scroll and pinch and the Mac reading view's pinch (built only); fonts installed through Settings › General › Fonts, which the simulator has none of; how Obsidian shows a size Graphite wrote on a device where Obsidian already keeps its own size. "Iowan Old Style" is not installed on the iOS 27 simulator, so lists naming it fall to the next family there.

## The even highlighter's tests, run on the iPad simulator for the first time: 2026-10-12

`EvenHighlighterTests` shared project IDs with `PDFLinkFollowingTests`, so the iPad suite never ran it, and the earlier sections' claims about its 7 tests were not true until 0b225f3 gave it IDs of its own. Run since, `testTheTipTurnsAcrossTheStrokeInEveryDirection` failed: it compared `renderBounds`, a bound PencilKit makes 2 points looser around a tip turned down the page (26 points) than across it (24). What PencilKit draws is the same both ways: in images at two pixels to the point, a horizontal and a vertical highlight 24 points wide both draw a band 21.5 points across and 207 long. The test now measures the drawn pixels, and also checks the band is about as wide as the tool. All 7 `EvenHighlighterTests` and 27 `PencilToolsTests` pass on the iPad simulator.

## Community plugins in notes, menus, typing and tabs: 2026-10-12

The plugin work of 2026-10-10 (`Obsidian community plugins: 2026-10-10`, below) merged into the alpha branch, then the owner's next four priorities, in their order. `Community-plugins.md` describes each (How it works › In notes, menus, typing and tabs) and how it was checked; `Architecture.md` the copies, timeouts and remaining risks.

- Merge: conflicts in `Coverage.md`, the project file (both branches' test files kept) and `SettingsView.swift` (both sides kept, labels in title case). Settings now reads "Turn On Community Plugins", "Community Plugins", "Installed Plugins"; a plugin's notice fades instead of sliding under Reduce Motion (`GraphiteMotion`).
- Reading view: plugins' code blocks (`NotePreviewBlock.pluginCodeBlock`, `CommunityPluginCodeBlockView`) and Markdown post-processors (`RenderedBlock.pluginSection`, `CommunityPluginSectionView`, notes divided into Obsidian's sections by `NotePreviewDocument.dividedIntoSections` while post-processors exist), shown as script-free copies of what the plugin drew in its page, kept current, answering taps, typing and keys. Live Preview still shows such blocks as code.
- Menus: plugins' items at the end of the note editor's edit menu (`UIDeferredMenuElement`), and Plugin Actions… in the file list's file and folder menus, a note's More menu and a tab's menu (`CommunityPluginMenus.swift`).
- Typing: `editor-change` and `EditorSuggest` in the note editor, suggestions in Graphite's own list (`CompletionContext.pluginSuggestion`, `CommunityPluginTyping.swift`); `editor.cm.state.field` answers Obsidian's editor state fields, now exported, so Outliner's item moves work and Tasks suggests.
- Views: plugin views in the sidebar's picker or in tabs (`CommunityPluginViews.swift`, `TabDocument.pluginViewIdentifier`), following the system text size; the plugin panel keeps modals and settings.
- Runtime additions: `community-plugin-runtime-reading.js`, links plugins draw opened through Graphite (a link could replace the plugins' page before), `getClickableTokenAt`, `vault.getAvailablePath`, `app.foldManager` (a stand-in), `i18next` (English strings). `Community-plugin-API-coverage.md` regenerated: all 56 exported functions and constants.

Checked: the package builds for macOS and the app for the iPad simulator. Tests on the final commit are listed under Tests below. In the iPad simulator, with Dataview, Tasks, Templater, Tag Wrangler, Iconize, Natural Language Dates, Calendar, Recent Files and Style Settings built from their sources at the commits in `compatibility-plugins.json` and copied into a copy of `Tests/test_vault`: Dataview's table and inline queries and Tasks' query in a note, in light and dark appearance, with ticking, filtering, copying, editing a task and following a link; Templater's folder item making a note from a template and Tag Wrangler renaming a tag in five notes from the editor's menu; Natural Language Dates' and Tasks' suggestions while typing, Undo included; Calendar's sidebar panel making and opening a daily note, also at the extra-extra-large text size; Recent Files' panel; Style Settings' view in a tab. Screenshots were taken of each.

Tests: on the final commit, the iPad integration tests (`GraphiteIntegrationTests`, iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator) ran 220, all passing, six of them new in `CommunityPluginHostTests` (11 there in all; the one with Dataview and Tasks reads the built plugins from the Mac's checkout and is skipped when they are not built). `swift test` on macOS: Core 844, Index 137, Apple 206, UI 517 with one skipped and one failure, `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading`, which fails on main too. Node: 63 runtime tests, all passing, with the sixteen real plugins built from their lockfiles. New Core tests: plugin code blocks in `NotePreviewDocument`, and its division into sections.

Not verified: on a physical iPad or iPhone, on the iPhone simulator, and in the Mac app (the Mac editor offers no plugin menu items or typing events). VoiceOver inside copies of blocks and views, a hardware keyboard's arrow keys in plugin suggestions, the runtime's memory with many copies shown, and plugin views after a restart (not restored) were not checked.

## Alpha with main, the PDF compaction, the palm round and the lasso round, checked together: 2026-10-12

Alpha `c693653` with, in this order: main `829779d` (one rule for the selection frame's handles, below), the PDF compaction cherry-picked from `main-pdf-size` (alpha only), the second palm and polish round (`alpha-palm-2`, then `alpha-palm-3`), a fix for turned frames found while checking, and the lasso's latest round (`alpha-lasso-3`). In the bar, the laser hides the ink colors (lasso) and the size dots show their widths (palm); both are kept. The lasso and the laser turn PencilKit's drawing off, and the even highlighter's band follows only a canvas that draws with its invisible stand-in, so neither gets the stand-in or the band.

Verified on the final commit: the whole hosted suite on an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27.0, 250 of 250; `swift test` on macOS, 1,713 tests (825 Core, 137 Index, 215 Apple, 536 UI), one skipped, with only the known `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaits…` failing. By finger in the iPad simulator app (fixed bar, finger drawing on), on a copy of a 44-page lecture PDF with a photo on page 1: the photo dragged up under the tab bar, its frame cut at the PDF view's top, its top handles just below the bar and the rotate handle below the frame; each of the four corners dragged and resizing the photo; the rotate handle below turning it. After the turn, the first build drew the frame and rotate handle over the tab bar; fixed (`709777f`), the turned frame was cut at the bar and its top-right handle, just below it, resized the photo. Three moves with autosave after each: 7,219,278, 7,219,278, 7,219,276 and 7,219,276 bytes (the file was 7.2 MB before the moves, with the photo and earlier edits in it). A stroke drawn into page 1's top-left corner, lassoed: its frame's top-left handle stood just inside the corner and resized the stroke. The pen's size dot grew at 8.8 mm and shrank at 0.3 mm with the slider. With Highlighter Ignores Tilt, a finger highlight across a line had the same height, color and translucency in screenshots taken while it was drawn and after the lift. With the laser over the highlighter's stand-in, a drag showed only the laser's dot, no band, and left nothing. With the final build, the laser's bar showed Dot and Line without colors, and the pen's bar its colors and sized dots. Screenshots in `~/.graphite-alpha-next-build/shots`. Not checked on an iPad or with Apple Pencil: none of the above; the PDF compaction's save times on an iPad; pasting pictures by hand (hosted tests only).

## The laser as neon lines that accumulate and fade on idle: 2026-10-12

Branch `alpha-lasso-4`, from alpha `1a9ed7b`, after the owner shared another app's laser ("i prefer this laser behavior"). Line mode now draws neon lines (white core, red glow about three times as wide, core width from Apple Pencil pressure, constant with a finger) that stay after each lift and fade together 1 s after the last lift over 0.35 s; touching down cancels the countdown and a new line during the fade restores every line at once. The glow pulses gently (70 to 100%, 1.6 s) unless Reduce Motion is on; Increase Contrast adds a red rim to the core. Still in a view over the window only: no document change, history step or file change, and palm rejection's resting-hand check still applies. The same on PDF pages and drawings (one overlay per window). This supersedes the per-line fade of the section below.

Checked: `InkSelectionTests.testTheLaserPointsWithoutChangingTheDocumentAndTheBarAndPaletteChooseIt` (two lines show together; pressure widens the core; nothing fades for 0.6 s after the lift, then the lines fade together; a new line mid-fade brings opacity back to 1 and keeps all three lines; nothing fades while a line is drawn; everything gone afterwards; the pulse runs, and stops with the lines; ink, history and file unchanged). By finger in the iPad simulator with a frame recording (`~/.graphite-lasso-build/shots/In-app-neon-laser-recording.mp4`, frames in `In-app-neon-laser-recording-frames.png`): two strokes on a white page both showed, then vanished about 1.4 s after the last lift. Whole hosted suite 270 of 271 with one skipped, none failing; `swift test` 1,735 tests with only the known `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaits…` failing. Not established: pressure with a real Apple Pencil (the simulator's touches have no force), the look on a dark slide in the running app (the white core is drawn for it; no dark slide in the test vault), Increase Contrast and Reduce Motion by hand, a device.

## The laser's line and glyph, and pasting pictures on pages: 2026-10-12

Branch `alpha-lasso-3`, from the owner trying the lasso build (alpha `c693653`) on the iPad.

- **The laser's line** now stays whole at full strength while the Pencil or finger is down and fades as a whole over 0.75 seconds once it lifts; a line begun during a fade is a new one, and the old one goes on fading. Before, each part faded 0.9 seconds after it was drawn, so a slow line shortened from its start while still being drawn. The dot and the hover dot are unchanged. Checked by `InkSelectionTests.testTheLaserPointsWithoutChangingTheDocumentAndTheBarAndPaletteChooseIt` (a line held 1.2 s keeps every point at full opacity; after the lift it fades with every point; a second line starts at full strength while the first fades away) and by finger in the simulator (`shots/In-app-laser-line-held-then-fading.png`: the line whole during a two-second hold, then fading together).
- **The laser's glyph.** Review (apple-design): `laser.burst` is a spiky starburst that reads as a flash, not as pointing, and is far denser than the bar's other tools; `icons.md › Best practices`: "Maintain visual consistency across all interface icons… a consistent size, level of detail, stroke thickness (or weight), and perspective" and "Create a recognizable, highly simplified design… familiar visual metaphors". It is now `LaserPointerGlyph`: a slim pointer at the neighbors' stroke weight (1.5 points, 2 in use), leaning as the inks' tips do, with a 5-point red dot at its tip, its only color: 4.7:1 on white and 6.2:1 on black, 6.0:1 and 7.5:1 with Increase Contrast (non-text minimum 3:1, computed in `testTheLaserHasAGlyphOfTheBarsFamilyAndOffersNoColors`). The comparison sheet (`~/.graphite-lasso-build/shots/laser-icon-options.png`, the bar's real glyphs, light and dark, not in use and in use) shows the custom glyph, `cursorarrow.rays` and `hand.point.up.left`: the custom one is the only one at the family's weight and the only one that says "a beam from a pointer"; `cursorarrow.rays` reads as a click and is as busy as the starburst; the hand is heavier and reads as touch. The custom one ships; the owner can choose another from the sheet. VoiceOver says "Laser Pointer". With the laser in use, the bar shows only Dot | Line after it: the ink colors, which do not apply, are hidden (`PencilToolRow.offersColors`), seen in light and dark (`shots/In-app-bar-laser-only-dot-line*.png`).
- **Pasting screenshots on PDFs.** What the owner's build did, found by reading its code (not tried in it): after Take Screenshot › Copy, a tap on a page with the lasso offered nothing (Paste was offered only for ink copied with the lasso), ⌘V had no handler on a page or a drawing, and only the More menu's Add Image › Paste Image placed an image, at 60% of the page's width whatever its size, which made a small screenshot large and soft. Now Paste, from the lasso's tap or a marked area, and ⌘V place an image from the pasteboard as a picture (a movable, rotatable stamp, one undo step, selected) where asked, at its own size, sharp: a screenshot at the size of its area. The same on a drawing. Ink copied with the lasso still pastes as ink. Checked by `InkSelectionTests` (a PNG pasted at the tap at half its pixels, undone in one step; a screenshot pasted by ⌘V at its area's size; a JPEG alone, as Photos copies, pasted the same way; on a drawing at the tap and by the host's ⌘V) and by finger in the simulator: Take Screenshot › Copy, then a tap on the next page and Paste placed the area as a picture with its frame and rotate handle (`shots/In-app-screenshot-pasted-on-PDF-as-picture.png`, `In-app-copied-screenshot-pasted-on-PDF.png`). Also fixed there: a tap on one page left a selection showing on another.
- Not established: ⌘V with a hardware keyboard by hand (the simulator tool sends no ⌘ keys; hosted tests call the paste action); a photo copied in Photos by hand: the simulator's pasteboard is synced with the Mac's, and an item synced from it (the hosted tests' copies) could not be read, so Paste found nothing to paste there; this is the simulator's sync, not seen with Graphite's own copies. The drawing editor's paste by hand. All on a device.

Verified on the branch's last code: the whole hosted suite on an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27.0, 234 of 234 (an earlier run lost one paste test to the simulator's pasteboard sync, which is why the paste tests now use a private pasteboard through `LassoPasteboard.pasteboard`); `swift test` on macOS, 1,705 tests (825 Core, 137 Index, 208 Apple, 535 UI), with only the known `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaits…` failing, the large-base measurement skipped as always, and `UiWorkspaceModelFixTests.testEditsMadeWhileChangedPDFReloadsAreKept` skipping itself when the reload finishes first.

## PDFs no longer grow with every edit (alpha only): 2026-10-12

Cherry-picked to alpha from `main-pdf-size` (`a38bce6`) to be tried on the iPad before it goes to main; main still writes the unreachable copies. It fixes the size problem found with the picture work (below).

Found on 2026-10-12: each save after moving, turning or cropping a picture on a PDF page kept the replaced stamp annotation in the file; after four edits 4.4 MB of a 5.5 MB PDF holding one photo were unreachable copies. Erased ink and removed markup grew files the same way (twenty strokes erased four times added 0.96 MB). PDFKit writes all objects of the file it read unless the page tree changes. Saves now go through `PDFObjectCompaction`, which keeps only the objects reachable from the trailer (see Save and conflict boundary in `Architecture.md`). The same pass stores a picture's appearance as its JPEG instead of the inflated pixels and opaque soft mask PDFKit wrote, and drops the second base64 copy of the picture PDFKit put in its markup data: a 187 KB JPEG took 693 KB, it now takes 438 KB (the JPEG and its base64 text for re-editing).

Checked by `PDFObjectCompactionTests` (9, on macOS with `swift test` and on the iPad simulator in `GraphiteIntegrationTests`): five moves and a turn of a photo keep the file within 4 KB of its size with the photo and store the JPEG once as the image; erased ink and removed markup leave the file within 1 KB; a hand-written PDF with an outline, page labels, a link, a filled form field, named destinations, viewer settings, document information and another application's annotation with a key of its own keeps all of them, and every catalog key, through a picture added and moved three times; files PDFKit did not write in its form are left alone; the JPEG image renders within 3 of 255 per channel of PDFKit's pixels in sRGB and Display P3. Without the compaction the size tests fail (the ink one by 0.96 MB). Turned pictures: compaction first recognised only `GraphitePictureV1`, so a picture turned by hand (`GraphitePictureV2`) kept its appearance as 2.0 MB of Flate pixels and its second base64 copy (1.28 MB) in the file; a 7.25 MB `Handles.pdf` (one photo turned about 22 degrees) now becomes 5.06 MB. `PDFObjectCompactionTests` has two more tests for it (9 in all): a photo turned 30 degrees, a quarter turn and 30 degrees on a page shown turned, moved five times, stays within 4 KB of its first save and within 2.5 times its JPEG's size of an empty file, with the JPEG as the image, no soft mask, one base64 copy, and Graphite reads back its bytes, angle, quarter turns and frame; and the JPEG image of turned pictures (30 degrees, a quarter turn, -60 degrees and a quarter turn on turned pages) renders within 3 of 255 per channel of PDFKit's pixels (the photo drawn at three quarters of its pixels). Both fail without the fix (the size one by 750 KB). Checked in the iPad simulator app: `Handles.pdf` opened, a stroke drawn by finger on page 1 so it was saved: 7,248,693 bytes before, 5,055,790 after, the photo's image 479,640 bytes of `DCTDecode`, no unreachable objects; after closing and reopening the app the photo is shown turned and in the same place. Known, not changed: a JPEG drawn by Core Graphics at half of its pixel size or less is decoded at reduced size, so the saved PDF's appearance of a photo shown that small differs from PDFKit's pixels by up to 130 of 255 at individual pixels of fine texture (about 30 percent of the channels in the photo measured), for upright and turned pictures alike (identical at 0.6 of its pixels or more, and by eye); Graphite's own display draws the picture from its bytes and is not affected, other readers may show such a small photo slightly differently. On this change: the whole hosted suite on an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27.0, 252 of 252; `swift test` on macOS with GraphiteAppleTests 217 of 217 and GraphiteUITests 536 with one skipped and only the known `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaits…` failing. Not checked: Preview and other readers, a device. `PicturesAndPaperTests.testMovingAPhotoAndSavingAfterEachMoveKeepsThePDFAboutItsSize` does the same through `PDFSession` on the iPad simulator. In the iPad simulator app: a photo placed on page 1 of a 44-page, 3.7 MB lecture PDF, moved twice and turned, with autosave after each step: 5,022,273, 5,022,521, 5,022,541 and 5,019,445 bytes, no unreachable objects, the image stored as `DCTDecode`. Save time on a Mac with a 102 MB, 592-page lecture: 0.27 s before, 0.39 s after in a debug build (the compaction itself 0.05 s optimized); 30 MB, 695 pages: 0.11 s before, 0.22 s after. Not checked on the iPad, so neither its save times; Preview and other readers were not opened on the saved files (Core Graphics and PDFKit read them in the tests).

## Main merged into alpha: one rule for where a selection frame's handles stand: 2026-10-12

Main `829779d` (the embedded PDF's toolbar after Back, and every corner of a picture near the top of a PDF) merged into alpha `c693653`. Three changes to the selection frame met: main's frame kept inside the PDF view's visible part, alpha's rotate handle below a picture it cannot be reached above, and the lasso's handles (a quarter of the frame at most inward, one knob on a tiny frame, a corner handle past a page's edge stood just inside it). They are now one rule in `SelectionFrameView.layoutSubviews`, against one reachable region (the superview's bounds less its safe area, in the frame's coordinates): each corner handle is first placed for its corner with the lasso's quarter rule, then kept inside the region by main's `handleCenter` (moved along the frame's sides, never past the opposite side, knob centered once moved); the rotate handle stands below when its center above is out of the same region. The lasso's own clamp (`reachableCenter`, in the superview's coordinates) and the rotate handle's `canReachHandle` did the same job as `handleCenter` and the region, and were removed; `handleCenter` is the one kept. `staysInSuperviewSafeArea` now only decides the clipping mask and refusing touches outside the region (on for PDF pictures, off for ink, which may hang past its page). A frame turned by other than a quarter turn checks its handles in the superview's coordinates and is masked with the region as turned: trying it by finger in the simulator showed that the first version of the merge, which used the rectangle around the turned region, drew a turned picture's frame and rotate handle over the tab bar (`testATurnedFrameAtTheTopKeepsItsHandlesAndItsDrawingInReach` covers it).

Checked: `PicturesAndPaperTests` and `InkSelectionTests` hosted on an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27, 34 of 34, including main's `testEveryCornerHandleOfAPictureAtTheTopOfTheViewCanBeTouched`, alpha's `testTheRotateHandleStaysWhereATouchReachesItAndVoiceOverReadsTheAngle` and the lasso's `testACornerHandlePastThePagesEdgeStandsInsideItAndTakesTouches`. The whole suite and the checks by finger are in "Alpha with main, the PDF compaction, the palm round and the lasso round, checked together" above.

## An embedded PDF's toolbar answers right after returning to its note: 2026-10-12

Found on 2026-10-12 in the simulator: a tap on an embedded PDF's Annotate or Open in PDF View within about a second of returning to the note was ignored. Reproduced in the iPad simulator with a note whose properties are long (19 lines) above a PDF embed: after Open in PDF View and Back, the note is a new editor, styled before its text view had a width, so the properties block kept its estimated height (622 points instead of the 364 it measures). When the properties view reported 364, the editor compared it with the height an earlier editor had measured, found them equal, and did not restyle, so the empty space stayed and the PDF embed sat 258 points lower than before. A tap where its toolbar had been went to the text (the log showed the touch reaching the text view's own content), which started editing and showed the frontmatter's source. The editor now compares each report with the height the text keeps for the block (`heightsKeptInText`, see `Architecture.md`). `EditingContinuityTests.testRenderedBlocksOfANoteOpenedAgainAreWhereTheyWereBefore` opens such a note, another note in the same tab, and the first again: before the change the embed was at 570 instead of 383 points, after it at 383. In the iPad simulator app: after Open in PDF View and Back, the embed is where it was, and Annotate tapped half a second later starts annotating (screenshots before and after). Not checked on the iPad.

Also found, not changed: switching tabs builds a note's rendered blocks again, and SwiftUI takes about 100 to 150 ms to show them and take touches; a tap in that time is lost. Keeping the views of a hidden note was tried and works (a hosted test showed the same views back), but it keeps up to the pool's 24 MB per hidden note, so it was left out. While an embedded PDF opens again (it is released 1.5 s after leaving the screen), Annotate is disabled; a large PDF on an iPad may take noticeably long to open.

## Every corner of a picture near the top of a PDF can be dragged: 2026-10-12

Found on 2026-10-12: near the top edge of the PDF view, a selected picture's corner handle could be under the tab bar, where touches went to the bar, so it could not be dragged; the frame's line was also drawn over the bar. The frame now stays inside what the PDF view shows, and a handle that would leave it moves inward along the frame's sides (`SelectionFrameView.staysInSuperviewSafeArea`, see `Architecture.md`). `PicturesAndPaperTests.testEveryCornerHandleOfAPictureAtTheTopOfTheViewCanBeTouched` places a picture half above page 1's top and checks, in a hosted PDF pane under a navigation bar, that every handle is inside the visible part of the PDF view and that a touch at its middle reaches it; it failed before the change (two handles under the bar, touches reaching the bar's content view and its Undo button) and passes after. `testSelectionFrameMovesWithinLimitsAndResizesInProportion` covers the placement rule. In the iPad simulator app: before, with a picture scrolled up under the tab bar, the frame's line ran over the tab bar and the top bar; after, the frame is cut at the PDF view's top, its top handles sit just below the tab bar, and dragging the top-left one by finger resized the picture. Not checked on the iPad or with a Pencil, in landscape, or in an embedded PDF in a note (the same controller places the frame there). Frames around lassoed ink and pictures in drawings are not clipped; on alpha their handles follow the same placement rule (see the merge of main into alpha above).
