## The lasso upgraded, area screenshots and a laser pointer: 2026-10-12

Asked for by the owner: the lasso's loop could barely be seen on a white page in dark appearance and lassoing handwriting to resize it was unreliable; the lasso could not take a picture of an area of a PDF; and the planned rectangular lasso, one selection of ink and pictures, and a laser pointer (`Premium-experience-plan.md`, "Requested on 2026-10-12"). Built on the `alpha-lasso` branch: `InkSelection.swift`, `SelectionFrameView.swift`, and the new `LassoTools.swift`, `PDFPageLasso.swift` and `LaserPointer.swift`; see `Architecture.md`.

- The loop: the accent as it reads on white over a white halo, 1.5 points and 6-point dashes on screen at any zoom (6.6:1 against white with the default accent). Checked by `InkSelectionTests` at three zooms of a PDF page in dark appearance, with the contrast computed, and seen in the simulator.
- Reliability, each found by reading the code or in the running app and fixed: a one-point stroke (the dot of an "i") could not be lassoed; the loop began where the pan was recognized, about 15 points from the touch-down; a tap on other ink while a selection showed only cleared it; on a small frame the corner handles covered the whole frame, so it could not be moved, and four knobs touched each other; after zooming a PDF page or the drawing editor the frame stayed in the old coordinates with handles grown or shrunk. Resizing was already corrected for PencilKit's padding and showed no jump; Undo was already one step.
- Rectangle lasso, and one selection of ink and pictures on PDF pages and drawings that moves, resizes and deletes as one undo step (Cut and Duplicate only for ink alone; Copy of a mixed selection is a picture).
- Take Screenshot: the area drawn afresh at 4 pixels per point whatever the zoom, a turned page turned as shown, a highlighter drawn once; the share sheet with Copy, Save Image and Add to “Note” for the note beside a PDF, the note a PDF is embedded in, or the note a drawing goes into.
- Laser pointer (Dot, Line) on the fixed bar and in the floating palette's menu, never changing the document.

Verified:

- `InkSelectionTests` (14 hosted, 9 new): the loop's drawing at three zooms in dark appearance; a dot taken by a loop, a rectangle taking what it encloses, a slip taking nothing, a tap moving the selection, the small frame moved by its middle with one knob; the frame following a 2.2× zoom with handles the same size on screen; a marked area and its menu; a screenshot's pixels (picture, ink over it, a highlighter at single strength, white paper) and pixel size the same at two zooms, and a turned page; ink and a picture moved, resized and deleted together with one undo each on a PDF page; the same on a drawing, with its screenshot, Add to Note's PNG and name, and the share sheet presented; the laser's overlay, its fading line, and the ink, history and file unchanged; the palette's menu; the pictures' pixel limits. `PicturesAndPaperTests.testFixedToolBarGivesCanvasesItsToolAndThePaletteTakesOverAgain` adds the bar's laser; `PencilToolsTests` the palette's new menu sections. `InkLassoTests` (macOS, 4 new): the rectangle's loop, a picture's enclosed share as turned, the remembered lasso type and laser style, file names from picture names.
- The whole hosted suite on an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27.0: before the laser commit, `PencilToolsTests.testPaletteHasEveryToolOnceAndTheColorsAndShapesButton` failed on the palette's new sections and was fixed; on the laser commit, 217 tests with 215 passing, `BasesViewsTests.testTheMapFetchesTiles…` and `PencilWorkspaceTests.testReadingPositionSurvives…` having timed out under load and passed when run alone; on the final commit (`f1ad1d7`), 217 tests with 216 passing, the known `ReadingTasksAndEquationsTests.testCheckboxIsAToggle…` failing in the full run and passing alone. `swift test` on macOS: 1,688 tests (825 Core, 137 Index, 205 Apple, 521 UI), one skipped, with only the known `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading` failing.
- By finger in the iPad simulator (finger drawing on), fixed bar: drawing a stroke and a dot on a PDF page, lassoing only the dot, its menu with Take Screenshot on the first page, the share sheet titled "Slides, page 1" with Copy and Save Image (the photo library permission was declined, so nothing was saved), moving the dot's small frame by its middle, a rectangle around the stroke resized by its corner without a jump and undone in one step, the laser's Line drawn and fading. On the PDF embedded in a note: a rectangle with nothing selectable marked as an area, Take Screenshot, Add to “Lasso note.md”, which wrote `Slides, page 1.png` (1388 × 456 pixels for a 347-point area) and appended its embed to the note. With the floating palette: its menu with Select Ink, Lasso Type, Laser Pointer and Laser Type, and a rectangle beginning at the touch-down. Found and fixed there: the rectangle's late start, the laser's line never showing (a long press with no minimum duration ended when the finger moved), the four knobs touching on a small frame, and Add to Note inserting at the top of an untouched note.
- Screenshots: hosted views (attachments of the test results) and the running app, in `~/.graphite-lasso-build/shots`.

Not established: anything with Apple Pencil or on a device (hover for the laser's dot, Pencil-only drawing with a resting palm, how the loop and the frame feel in hand); the lasso in the drawing editor by hand (hosted tests only); a selection of ink and pictures by hand; Save Image completing (permission declined in the simulator); Add to Note from a PDF pane with a note beside it by hand (the path is shared with the embedded PDF's and Quote in Note's); the laser on an external display or with AirPlay; Increase Contrast and Reduce Motion by hand (the loop's wider line under Increase Contrast is in code; nothing the lasso or laser adds animates beyond the share sheet, presented without animation under Reduce Motion); VoiceOver by hand (labels and custom actions are set and partly tested).

Known limits, not fixed: a selection's frame can lag a frame behind PencilKit's redraw at the end of a drag (not seen in the simulator). The corner handle past the page's edge and the resting palm's laser dot were fixed afterwards (section above).

## Hold to make a shape works on every stroke while a hand rests: 2026-10-12

`StrokeHoldRecognizer` set itself failed after each stroke. A failed recognizer hears no more touches until every touch has lifted, so with a hand resting on the page the strokes after the first were not followed, and holding at their end made no shape. It now stays possible, as the Pencil follower and `DrawnStrokeFollower` do. Checked by `PencilToolsTests.testHoldToShapeFollowsEveryStrokeWhileAHandRests` (hosted: the states the recognizer asks for over three strokes and a cancelled one never include failed; UIKit ignores a state set outside its own touch handling, so the test records them), which fails with the previous code; `PencilToolsTests`, `EvenHighlighterTests` and `PicturesAndPaperTests` (44) pass, and `swift test` (1,701, the one known failure). Not tried with a real hand and Pencil.

## Checks of the second palm and polish round together: 2026-10-12

On the last of the commits in the four sections below (the size dots, the even highlighter and the cover, the favorite colors), in a clone of alpha 85f7552 outside iCloud: the whole hosted suite on an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27.0, 224 of 224; `swift test` on macOS, 1,701 tests (825 Core, 137 Index, 208 Apple, 531 UI) with one skipped and the one known failure, `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading`. Nothing was tried on the iPad or with Apple Pencil.

## The even highlighter looks the same while drawn and after, and no frame loses the stroke: 2026-10-12

Reported by the owner from the iPad with a real Pencil (alpha cfce131): with Ignore Pencil Tilt on and a narrow highlighter, the stroke was a broad tilted band while drawn and became the thin even stroke at the lift. Also found in this round: replacing a stroke just drawn (the even highlighter, the shape tool) left the canvas without it for about 30 ms. See `Architecture.md`, the even highlighter paragraphs, for how both now work: PencilKit draws with an invisible stand-in while Graphite draws the band the stroke will become, and the canvas as it was covers PencilKit's redrawing.

Checked:
- `EvenHighlighterTests` (7, hosted): the even tip as before, now at one opacity; the tip turns across the stroke in every direction, a dot and a tremble included, and the band is as wide down the page as across it; the stand-in draws nothing, has the highlighter's width, and its stroke takes the highlighter's ink; the band path reaches as far as the even stroke, and its opacity grows with its width.
- `PencilToolsTests` (hosted): `testAnEvenHighlightLooksTheSameBeforeAndAfterTheLift` draws, in the drawing editor, the band of a stroke being drawn, then hands the canvas a tilted, turning stroke of the invisible stand-in, as PencilKit would at the lift: the stroke kept has the highlighter's ink, the canvas is covered until PencilKit has drawn it, and the band and the stroke are within 12 percent in size with the same middle color; only PencilKit's softened edges differ. `testAShapeReplacingAStrokeIsCoveredUntilPencilKitHasDrawnIt`: the cover is up from the moment the shape replaces the stroke and gone, with the canvas's mask, once PencilKit has drawn it.
- Simulator frame recordings (iPad Pro 11-inch (M5), finger strokes, 120 Hz capture), before and after: the highlighter's stroke missing for 30 ms at the lift before, the shape tool's for 30 ms before, and neither after; the band while drawn and the even stroke after the same size and color across a line, over text at full opacity, translucent, and on a bent diagonal, apart from PencilKit's softened edges.
- Not established, needing the iPad and a Pencil: a tilted Pencil's stroke (the simulator cannot tilt; the hosted test synthesizes one, and the band never depends on the tilt); the band's latency beside PencilKit's own live stroke; Apple Pencil hover previews; dark appearance on the drawing editor's paper. The floating palette gives the canvas its tool first and Graphite replaces it on the next turn of the main queue, as for the PDF zoom; a stroke begun in that moment draws PencilKit's tilted live stroke and is made even at the lift.

## The size dots on the Pencil bar show their widths: 2026-10-12

Reported by the owner from the iPad (screen recording on alpha cfce131): with Monoline on a PDF, moving its width from 1.4 mm to 0.2 mm and back left the three size dots exactly as they were; the dots were three fixed sizes, whatever the widths. Each dot is now as large as its width (`PencilSizeDot`, see `Architecture.md`), on the fixed bar and in the squeeze palette. Checked: `PencilToolboxTests.testTheSizeDotsFollowTheirWidthsWithinAVisibleRange` (macOS: the owner's monoline case differs by more than 6 points, every ink's three starting sizes by more than 1.5, every width within its range stays between 3.5 and 22 points, the broadest eraser and odd values clamped); `PencilToolsTests.testTheBarsSizeDotFollowsItsWidth` (hosted: the chosen dot's pixels grow and shrink as its width is changed); in the simulator, the pen's dot growing to 8.8 mm and shrinking to 0.3 mm while its popover's slider moved. The buttons keep their size (28 by 44 points). Not looked at on the iPad, nor the squeeze palette (it needs a Pencil).

## The favorite colors on the Pencil bar follow Settings at once: 2026-10-12

The fixed bar's favorite colors were handed in by `PDFPane`, `EmbeddedPDFViewer` and `DrawingEditor` as `GraphitePreferences.storedColorPalette()` when the view was built, so a palette changed in Settings › Colors showed on the bar only when the document was opened again, as the highlighter's colors did before. `PencilToolRow` and the squeeze palette now follow the stored palette themselves, and the owners no longer pass it. Checked by `PencilToolsTests.testThePDFBarsFavoriteColorsFollowSettingsAtOnce` (hosted): a PDF's fixed bar with the pen in use shows the default red swatch, then, once Settings saves another palette, its color and no red; the same test fails with the previous code (the second wait times out). Not looked at on the iPad.

## Drawings in Live Preview no longer flicker: 2026-10-12

Reported by the owner with a screen recording from the iPad: while typing under the drawings at the end of a note, the drawings went black for about 75 ms, now and then; moving the cursor onto a drawing's line and off it showed a spinner and an empty space for a frame or two; scrolling showed spinners. Three causes, each reproduced in the iPad simulator with a copy of the recorded note and its six drawings, with frame recordings (`simctl io recordVideo`) and breakpoints that log:

- **Every save rebuilt every rendered block.** The system reports a note's own coordinated save back to the vault's file presenter (`VaultMonitor.presentedSubitemDidChange` for the note, seen in the debugger, in the simulator's local vault and not only in iCloud), the outside-change refresh then raises `drawingVersion`, and the editor answered that with `removeAllWidgets()`. A new embed view shows nothing until it has found its file through the index, so a second or so after each pause in typing every drawing on screen went blank (2 frames in the simulator, about 75 ms on the iPad). The views are now given the new environment in place (`LivePreviewWidgetSignature.drawingVersion`), keep what they show while they look their files up again, and a table or callout keeps its prepared text until the new one is ready. An embedded PDF, which was rebuilt the same way, now checks its file when the version changes and shows a version saved elsewhere unless it has annotations not saved yet (`EmbeddedPDFViewer.drawingVersion`; built and run in the hosted suites, but not checked by hand with a PDF annotated in another tab).
- **A new view of an embed started empty.** The pool of off-screen views keeps two drawings, so a drawing scrolled back into view, or next to a block the cursor enters, was usually made again and showed a progress indicator first. It now draws, in its first frame, the picture it last showed (`LivePreviewEmbedImageLocations` remembers where each embed's image was found; `ReadingImageCache` keeps the picture).
- **A drawing the cursor leaves was placed a turn late.** Restyling its line replaces its fragment, which TextKit had not laid out when the blocks were placed, so placement waited for the next turn of the run loop while the text below had already moved down. The viewport is now laid out in the same pass, and once more if that layout scrolled the view (a tap on a misspelled word below the drawing selects it and scrolls).

Checked in the iPad simulator ("Graphite Flicker iPad", dark appearance) by frame recordings, before and after: typing with three autosaves, before 2 blank frames at each save, after none (no frame below 80% of the drawings' area); the cursor entering and leaving a drawing by `</>` and taps, three times by a misspelled word: before 28 to 38 ms of empty space each time, after none; plain taps were clean after the change. Automated: `UiLivePreviewEmbedContinuityTests` (5, macOS), and in `LivePreviewBlockPlacementTests` `testAChangedFileKeepsTheDrawingOnScreen`, which failed with the old teardown (in a scratch copy) and passes, and `testADrawingTheCursorLeavesIsPlacedInTheSameLayoutPass`, which passes with and without the change (it does not reach the late path; the recordings are the evidence for it). Ran: the package UI tests (527, 1 skipped measurement) and the hosted `LivePreviewBlockPlacementTests`, `LivePreviewRevealTests` and `EditingContinuityTests` (27); `ReadingTasksAndEquationsTests.testEquationNumberStandsAtTheRightOfTheColumnAndIsNotCutOffWhenNarrow` fails on this simulator (iPad Pro 11-inch (M5)) with and without these changes, on main 3865bc4 alone too; it is in reading view, which they do not touch. Not checked on the iPad, in reading view (which also reloads its images when `drawingVersion` changes), or on canvases.

## Pictures on PDF pages, finished: 2026-10-12

Found in the review of the changes of 2026-10-11 (below): a picture placed on a PDF page and made smaller was smooth while it was edited but showed moiré again once the PDF was opened again, the owner's original report half unfixed.

- Cause: after a reopen PDFKit draws the stamp's saved appearance, and draws its image with the interpolation of the context it draws into. Rendered by `PDFPage.draw` into a context with no interpolation, the saved appearance of a 720-pixel picture shown 120 points wide differed from the same image drawn by Core Graphics with high interpolation by 49 gray levels (root mean square), and by 5 when the context had high interpolation too (macOS); PDFView on iOS draws it with low interpolation even with `interpolationQuality` set to high, while the picture drawn by `PDFPictureAnnotation` after an edit sets high interpolation itself. Fix: every shown PDF document makes the stamps it reads `PDFPictureAnnotation`s (`PDFPictureDrawingDelegate`), which draw Graphite's picture from its keys; the file is not changed.
- Seen in the iPad simulator (iPad Pro 11-inch (M5), iOS 27): a PDF with a zone-plate picture of 720 × 540 pixels shown about 166 points wide. Before the change, opened again, it differed from the same picture after a move and undo (drawn live) by 5.6 gray levels RMS (largest 39, 40% of pixels equal), with plain moiré rings; after the change, opened again at the same zoom, it is pixel for pixel the live-drawn picture (RMS 0, 100% equal). Opening and closing it left the file's bytes unchanged (same MD5).
- The written appearance (`/Interpolate true`, the picture's own pixels) was checked in other readers at 144 dpi against a Lanczos reduction: Quick Look 5.1 and Ghostscript 6.2 gray levels RMS, both smooth; poppler 27.7 with moiré, the same as for the same JPEG drawn straight into page content, so poppler's reduction is the cause and the writing side was left as it is. Found, not changed: PDFKit writes the appearance's image as Flate pixels with a soft mask, not the JPEG's bytes, so a photograph's appearance is several times the size of its JPEG.
- Tests: `PDFPictureTests.testAPictureOfAReopenedPDFIsDrawnAsSmoothlyAsWhileItIsEdited` (pages turned 0° and 90°, pictures turned 0 and 30°: opened again and drawn without interpolation it is within 0.5 gray levels of the live-drawn picture, while the same file without the delegate is more than 3 away; Graphite reads back the same picture) and `testPicturesOfInsertedPagesAreDrawnTooAndOtherAnnotationsAreReadAsBefore` (pages put back by undo; links and notes keep PDFKit's own classes; another application's stamp draws exactly as without the delegate), on macOS and in the hosted iOS suite; `PicturesAndPaperTests.testPictureReadFromAFileIsSelectedByATapAndStaysUnderTheInk` checks a reopened session's picture is Graphite's annotation.
- Not checked: on the iPad itself, and on the Mac app's PDF view, which uses the same code.
- Older builds: a picture turned by hand on a PDF page is now stored under `GraphitePictureV2`; upright pictures stay under `GraphitePictureV1`, and turning one back upright returns it there. `PDFPictureTests.testATurnedPictureIsStoredUnderItsOwnKeyAndOlderFilesAreStillRead` checks the keys, that a turned picture under the old key (as written on 2026-10-11) still reads turned, and that the new key survives a save that inserts a page. Main's own code (62e023b, the same picture code as the owner's main) was run in a scratch checkout on a PDF written by this branch: it lists only the upright picture, shows the turned one turned (red above blue where it stands, white where an upright one would be), and cannot select or move it. After main moved its upright picture and saved, this branch read the turned picture back with its frame and angle; after main inserted a page and saved, the turned picture was still a visible stamp but had lost Graphite's keys, so it can no longer be arranged (main drops keys it does not know on a page-tree rewrite, as it already did for the angle under the old key).
- Names: the quarter turn clockwise was the button "Turn Image" and the step "Turn Image", while rotating by the handle was the step "Rotate Image". Following Preview's Rotate Right and the PDF pages' own Rotate Right, the button and its step are now "Rotate Image Right" (`rotatePictureRight`, `rotateSelectedPictureRight`), and the handle's step stays "Rotate Image" (`rotatePicture(_:to:)`). `PDFPictureEditingTests` (macOS) and `PicturesAndPaperTests` (hosted, 16) pass with the new names.
- The rotate handle at the top edge, not tried in the review: in the iPad simulator, a picture moved to the top of a PDF's only page had its handle drawn over the tab bar, where a drag did nothing (`top-edge-before.png`). The handle now stands below the frame wherever it could not be reached above (the view's bounds less its safe area, also as a scroll view scrolls); seen below the picture there and turning it by a drag. The help line under the page now says "the arrow handle" rather than "the handle above it". Not fixed: near the top edge a corner handle can still fall over the tab bar, out of reach; the picture can be moved down to reach it.
- VoiceOver: the frame read only its label, hint and two actions, so the angle could be changed but not known. It now has the angle as its value and announces it after Rotate Clockwise or Rotate Counterclockwise. `PicturesAndPaperTests.testTheRotateHandleStaysWhereATouchReachesItAndVoiceOverReadsTheAngle` (hosted) checks the handle's side above, at the top edge, upside down, and after scrolling, and the value and actions (15° each, one rotation reported per action, none while cropping). VoiceOver itself was not run, and the drawing editor's handle at the top of a drawing was checked only through that test's scroll view, not in the editor.
- SVG drawings with a turned picture in an older build: main's own reader was run on SVGs written by this branch. With an upright picture it shows the drawing; with a turned one it refuses it ("Graphite previews only SVG drawings it created"), because the drawing record is version 5, which it cannot read, and it previews only SVGs whose record it reads. A backward-compatible encoding was not found (a version 4 record would show the picture upright there and let that build save it upright; a picture redrawn into an upright bitmap would degrade the file for every reader); this is documented in `Architecture.md` as a known limit.
- Found, not changed: every save after a picture is moved, rotated or cropped on a PDF page keeps the replaced stamp in the file as objects nothing refers to. After four such edits in the simulator, 4.4 MB of a 5.5 MB PDF holding one 200 KB photograph were unreferenced copies of its earlier stamps (its bytes twice and its appearance image). PDFKit writes the objects of the file it read even when an annotation was removed; ink that is erased is likely affected the same way. Main does the same. Fixed on alpha by the compaction (see "PDFs no longer grow with every edit" above), not yet on main.
- Whole suites on the last commit of this work: the hosted iPad suite, 212 of 212 (iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27); `swift test` on macOS, 1,687 tests (825 Core, 137 Index, 208 Apple, 517 UI), one skipped and one failure, the known `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading`.

## The even highlighter draws the chosen height, without a jump at the lift: 2026-10-12

A review asked whether the even highlighter's change at the lift looked like a jump. In the simulator it did, and not only from tilt: a finger's highlighter stroke on a PDF page, with the highlighter ignoring tilt, was 60 points tall while drawn and 34 points after the lift, because the even points were the whole tool width at an azimuth that lays PencilKit's chisel tip along the stroke. They are now two thirds of the width, turned across the rows, as PencilKit records a finger's or an upright Pencil's points; the band is as tall as the tool is wide (see `Architecture.md`).

Checked: `EvenHighlighterTests` (4, hosted) measures what PencilKit draws (`renderBounds`): a highlight across a line is as tall as the tool is wide, the same height and length as an upright stroke, and the same from a tilted stroke; `PencilToolsTests.testHighlighterThatIgnoresTiltDrawsOneWidthAndOtherInksStayAsDrawn` checks the band's height in the drawing editor. In the simulator, a frame recording of a finger stroke shows the band the same height before and after the lift. It also shows the stroke missing for about 20 ms at the lift, while PencilKit draws the replaced drawing; the shape tool replaces the drawing the same way, and this was not looked into further. A tilted Pencil's stroke still changes at the lift; not seen, needing the iPad.

## What Settings says about highlights on PDF pages: 2026-10-12

Settings › Colors › Highlighter said the highlighter's light colors keep the text under a highlight easy to read. On a PDF page they do not: seen in the simulator, a highlighter stroke at full opacity covers the text under it in solid yellow (also on main). Blending highlighter ink with multiply, as real highlighters and Goodnotes look, was not done: PencilKit draws every stroke of a page in one canvas, and multiplying the whole canvas would make white or light ink vanish on dark slides and darken colored ink over figures (see `Architecture.md`, the even highlighter paragraph, for what a real fix needs). The footer now says what happens and what keeps text readable: a lower opacity in the highlighter's options (seen in the simulator: at about 40 percent the text under a highlight reads clearly) or selecting the text and choosing Highlight.

## A reading view's first scroll is not pulled back: 2026-10-12

`PencilWorkspaceTests.testReadingPositionSurvivesViewRecreationAndNewHeadingTakesPriority` timed out once in a full alpha run (both of its waits for the scrolled offset) and passed alone. Alpha's per-vault places are not involved: the test hosts a `MarkdownPreview` with its own `ReadingPosition` and no workspace, and workspaces in tests have no vault identifier, so they keep no places. The run's log shows no dropped scroll-geometry update after the test's scroll, so the scroll was seen and set aside: the reading view, with nothing saved, recorded its offsets into the shared checkpoint straight away, and once any of them was not zero (as while a view settles under the window's bars) it was taken for a saved position, restored, and the restoration pulled the test's scroll back. Such offsets are now kept apart until the view knows it has nothing to restore (see `Architecture.md`, reading position). The failure could not be made to happen again, before or after the change: the test passed 40 times alone, the whole `PencilWorkspaceTests` class 25 times (10 with 18 busy processes), and the whole hosted suite on this branch. So the cause above is the explanation that fits the log, not one seen happening.

## Places kept in memory: 2026-10-12

Where each document was left was decoded from the device's preferences, and encoded back, on every read and every change: every tab switch and every document opened read the whole list of up to 400 places. `DocumentPlaceStore` now reads a vault's places once and writes only when they change, shared by every workspace of the app. Removing a vault forgets its places in memory too. Checked by `DocumentPlaceStoreTests` (3, macOS: read once and kept, written only on change, damaged places read as none and replaced, a forgotten vault's places gone) and the whole hosted suite. Not looked at separately in the simulator; a PDF opening again where it was left after a relaunch was seen with the final build (see the section on all of these checks above).

## The Pencil tool bar's code in four files: 2026-10-12

`PencilToolbox.swift` had grown to about 1,040 lines. It now keeps the tool bar's state and the inks (`PencilToolbarStyle`, `PencilInk`, `InkPreset`, `PencilToolbox` and their PencilKit tools); `PencilToolbar.swift` has the bar, its row and the tab bar's tools; `PencilToolButtons.swift` the buttons the bar and the squeeze palette share, the bar's metrics and the tool glyphs; `PencilToolOptions.swift` the size editor and the ink options. The code moved unchanged (checked line by line); the package picks the files up by folder, so the Xcode project needed no change. Checked by building for iPad and macOS and by the hosted `PencilToolsTests`.

## The highlighter's colors follow Settings at once: 2026-10-12

The fixed bar and the squeeze palette read the highlighter's colors once, when they were made, so a color changed in Settings › Colors showed only after the bar was made again. They now follow the stored list. Checked by `PencilToolsTests.testTheBarsHighlighterColorsFollowSettingsAtOnce` (hosted): a bar on screen with the highlighter in use shows the default blue swatch, then, once Settings saves another list, that list's color and no blue; with the previous code the same test fails. Not looked at on the iPad.

## Palm rejection hardened, with a switch in Settings: 2026-10-12

A review of alpha's palm rejection (written without a compiler, never tried with a Pencil) found that a broad touch was rejected with no Pencil about, and with finger drawing took back the person's own stroke; that a missed Pencil touch could let finger drawing's take-back cancel a Pencil stroke; that recognizers and touches were tracked by object address, which a freed object hands on; that a lost touch or hover ending could keep fingers waiting 20 seconds; and that the Pencil follower failed after the first stroke of a sequence, so with a hand resting it no longer heard later Pencil touches. All are changed as described in `Architecture.md` (the palm rejection paragraph): no breadth rule; liveness asked of UIKit, with one second as the bound for what cannot be asked; weak tables and per-recognizer numbers; take-back only of a stroke every touch of which is the hand's; and a Settings switch, Palm Rejection, on by default and read as each touch lands. Writing the hosted test also found that the drawing editor's pinch was never made to wait (UIKit creates it only once the canvas can zoom), so a resting hand could zoom a drawing; it now waits.

Checked:
- `PalmRejectionTests` (10, macOS): active while touching, hovering and 0.6 s after; fingers free within a second of a lift or a hover's end; a lost touch or hover ends at its last report; one second without news ends either; a new touch replaces a lost one; a pointer's hover is not the Pencil's; a hand only with the switch on, while writing and with the Pencil active, whatever its breadth; the preference's default and live reading; take-back only of a stroke all of whose touches are the hand's, never with the Pencil down or a Pencil touch among them.
- Hosted (`PencilToolsTests`, 3 new): the PDF view's and the drawing editor's scrolling and pinch wait for palm rejection and PencilKit's drawing does not; the recognizer sees no Pencil touches and delays nothing; a page counts as written on only in writing mode, the drawing editor except while pictures are arranged; 200 recognizers made and freed in turn are each made to wait (none mistaken for a freed one); a hover whose view left the window, or whose recognizer was freed, stops making fingers wait at once, while one still going on keeps them waiting.
- In the iPad simulator, switch on, no Pencil: on a PDF page in writing mode, one-finger scrolling, a long press selecting text with its menu, a tap dismissing it, and pinching in and out; in the drawing editor, finger drawing (strokes stay), one-finger scrolling, pinch zoom, PencilKit's long-press menu (Select All, Insert Space) and a tap dismissing it. The switch shows on in Settings › Pencil Drawings. With a temporary build that took the Pencil to be always active (not committed), a finger stroke on a PDF page with finger drawing was taken back and the page did not scroll; its log showed PencilKit's drawing recognizer reporting the hand's touch at exactly the hand's location, which is what take-back relies on.
- Not established, needing the iPad and a Pencil: real palms, whether 0.6 s feels right, hover from a real Pencil, and that recognizers waiting on one that never sees a Pencil's touch are not held up.

## The selection frame on a page in dark appearance: 2026-10-12

Reported by the owner on the iPad: in dark appearance, the frame around lassoed handwriting could not be seen on a white PDF page. `SelectionFrameView` drew its line in `label` and its knobs in `systemBackground`, which turn white and black in dark appearance, while a PDF page and a drawing's paper keep their own colors. The frame now draws the accent resolved for light appearance (`PlatformColor.graphiteAccent`, at least 4.5:1 on white; the default ink blue is 6.6:1), over a white halo one point wider on each side, and white knobs ringed in the accent with a soft shadow; with Increase Contrast the line is 2.5 points instead of 1.5. `GraphitePreferences.storedAccentHex()` reads the chosen accent for views made outside SwiftUI.

Checked in the iPad simulator in dark appearance, with handwriting lassoed on a white page of a PDF (the frame and its knobs show), and by `InkSelectionTests` (5) and `PicturesAndPaperTests` (15). Not checked on the iPad, over dark ink or photos, with Increase Contrast, or around a picture.

## The note editor's first layout, and handing it to a new view: 2026-10-12

Found on commit e3b973c in the iPad simulator: the app crashed as soon as a note opened in Live Preview or Source mode (at launch when a note was restored, and in every hosted test that shows one), `EXC_BAD_ACCESS` at 0x10 in `MarkdownTextView.updateReadAloudHighlights()`. The text view was made by `UITextView(usingTextLayoutManager: true)`, a UIKit factory that creates the object through an initializer Swift does not see, so `MarkdownTextView`'s stored properties never got their initial values and were all zero. That had gone unnoticed while every one of them was valid as zero (only `usesReadableLineLength` started false, and each update set it); Read Aloud's highlight array was the first that was not. `MarkdownTextView()` now calls `UITextView(frame:textContainer: nil)`, which makes a TextKit 2 view. `EditingContinuityTests.testNotesTextViewStartsWithItsInitialValuesOnTextKit2` failed before the change (the readable line length read false) and passes after it. The Mac editor's `MarkdownMacTextView(usingTextLayoutManager:)` uses AppKit's factory of the same kind; its properties are all optional, so it does not crash, but it has not been changed or checked.

Reported by the owner on the iPad (Release build of 2026-10-10): tapping Annotate on a PDF embedded in a note froze Graphite until it was ended, twice. Both of the iPad's reports show SwiftUI dismantling the note's view while a view inside its text view held the keyboard; giving the keyboard up laid the window out at once, SwiftUI made the note's next view inside that dismantling, and moving the text view into it while UIKit was still removing it left SwiftUI updating without end. The editor now ends editing inside the text view first, and a view made while a dismantled one lets go of the text view gets it once that is done (`NativeMarkdownEditor.Coordinator.viewWasDismantled`). `testAViewMadeWhileTheNoteLeavesItsViewGetsTheTextView` takes those steps one by one: without the change the text view was moved mid-removal and the note was kept as hidden although on screen; with it the test passes. The freeze itself was not reproduced: the owner's note and PDF, the same build and settings, the keyboard up and the PDF open in another tab, by finger in the iPad simulator, all annotated normally. What made SwiftUI dismantle the note's view on the iPad is not known (a Pencil tap, or a width class change, which rebuilds the whole document area through `DocumentTitle`'s two branches, are candidates). Not yet checked on the iPad.

Checked: the hosted suite on the iPad simulator, 201 of 201 (with the three new tests), on e3b973c with only this change.

## Built and checked: the changes of 2026-10-11, on 2026-10-12

The three sections below were written without a Swift compiler. On 2026-10-12 they were built and run for the first time, in a clone outside iCloud, against main (`e3b973c`) as the baseline. Every run below also carries main's fix for the note editor's start-up crash (`MarkdownTextView()` instead of `MarkdownTextView(usingTextLayoutManager:)`), without which every hosted test that opens a note crashed on main too.

- Build: the iPad app and its tests built without changes. The macOS package did not: `PencilSizeChoice` and `PencilWidthText` were declared for UIKit only while `PencilToolboxTests` uses them on macOS; they now sit before the UIKit part of `PencilToolbox.swift`. `EvenHighlighterTests` was in no test target and so ran nowhere; it is now in `GraphiteIntegrationTests`.
- `swift test` on macOS: 1,684 tests (825 Core, 137 Index, 205 Apple, 517 UI), with one skipped and one failure, `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading`, which fails on main in the same way. The new `DocumentPlacesTests`, `DrawingGeometryTests`, `PDFPictureTests`, `VectorPictureTests`, `PalmRejectionTests` and `PencilToolboxTests` cases pass.
- Hosted tests on an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27.0: the whole `GraphiteIntegrationTests` suite, 205 tests including the new `EvenHighlighterTests` (3), `PicturesAndPaperTests` and `PencilToolsTests` cases, passes; main passes its 198. One run showed `ReadingTasksAndEquationsTests.testCheckboxIsAToggleThatTicksItsTaskAndKeepsTheReadingPosition` failing in the full suite only; it passed alone and in the next full run.
- Seen in the simulator, beside main on a second simulator: a 4000 × 3000 picture made small on a drawing is smooth where main shows it jagged, and the drag preview of a picture on a PDF page is smooth where main's is jagged. A picture on a PDF page after the drag looks the same as on main, and so does a PDF opened again: there, PDFKit draws the stamp's saved appearance itself, with visible moiré on fine patterns, and `PDFView.interpolationQuality` changes nothing (pixel-identical screenshots). A picture turned by its handle on a PDF page and on a drawing, then resized, cropped, turned a quarter, undone and redone; the PDF opened again turned, and poppler, Ghostscript and Quick Look show it turned; the PNG drawing's record is version 5 with the angle, and the SVG has `rotate()`, which Quick Look shows and which opens again for editing. The fixed bar: the size popover (the pen's medium size set to 6.7 mm), the highlighter's own colors, and Ignore Pencil Tilt in its options; a finger stroke with the highlighter still draws. A PDF opens again where it was left after the app is ended, and so do a note in the editor and in the reading view, after the fix below.
- Fixed from what was seen: a note opened after a relaunch about a dozen paragraphs below where it had been left, because places were taken in the save task that runs after Graphite leaves the screen; they are now taken the moment Graphite stops being active (`GraphiteRootView`). A quick drag on the turning handle of a picture on a PDF page ended arranging instead of turning it, because the PDF view's tap took the touch as a tap beside the picture; touches on the selection frame and its handles are now left to the frame, as in the drawing editor.
- Seen with an older build (main): a PDF picture turned by this build shows turned, but moving it there makes it upright and stretched to the box around it, and loses the angle; a version 5 PNG drawing shows as a plain picture, as intended; an SVG drawing with a turned picture shows "cannot be shown".
- Still not established: anything that needs Apple Pencil or a hand (palm rejection, tilt, hover, the even highlighter's look with a real tilted Pencil), and the iPhone.

## Palm rejection, and documents that open where they were left: 2026-10-11

Reported from use: a hand resting on the page while writing with Apple Pencil still moved or selected things, and a note or PDF left somewhere in it opened at its top after coming back to the app. The second happened when iOS had ended Graphite in the background: nothing about a document's place was saved, so it reopened at its start (within one run, tabs already came back to their place, a PDF only to the top of its page). See `Architecture.md`, the paragraph after the shape hold and the one after tabs.

- Palm rejection: while a page is written on, and the Pencil touches, hovers, or has lifted within 0.6 seconds, or for any palm-sized touch, a hand does not scroll or zoom the page, tap, select text or open PencilKit's menu, on PDF pages and in the drawing editor; with finger drawing, its stroke is taken back. PencilKit's drawing never waits for this. Not covered: notes (Scribble), a picture's selection frame, and the note around an embedded PDF.
- Places: each vault keeps, on the device, where every note (the line at the top and the cursor) and PDF (the page and how far down it) was left, taken as Graphite leaves the screen or vault, as tabs close, and as a tab opens another file; at most 400, following renames and deletions. Notes and PDFs open there, unless a link asks for a page. A PDF now also comes back to its place on the page after another tab or a reload. A note adopting another app's changes keeps its scroll position.

Tests: `PalmRejectionTests` (5, UI target): the Pencil is active on the screen, while hovering, and 0.6 seconds after; a lost lift lets go after 20 seconds and the next touch replaces it; a hover whose end was lost does not keep fingers waiting after later strokes; a pointer's hover is not the Pencil's; which touches are a hand, only while writing. `DocumentPlacesTests` (4, Core): places per document, the start not kept, the bound, renames and deletions, kept between launches. None has been run: written without a Swift compiler, syntax-parsed and reviewed only. A second reading of the first version found no compile errors but five faults, fixed before anything was built: a PDF's place was checked against the page PDFKit counts as current (across the middle of the view) rather than the page at its top, so it was mostly dropped and moved a page down; a link to a page could land at the old place on it; a reading view scrolled back to its top was not remembered; one lost Pencil ending could keep fingers waiting 20 seconds after every later stroke; and a test compared identifiers of objects already freed. It also led to taking rejection out of reading, covering PDFKit's recognizers on pages shown later, and leaving a link or search match to win over the remembered place in the reading view. Not established, and needing the iPad: whether 34 points tells a palm from a finger on real hands, whether 0.6 seconds feels right, whether PDFKit's own recognizers are all reached, and the places on relaunch in the app itself.

## Sizes that can be changed, and colors of the highlighter's own: 2026-10-11

Asked for after the change below, with Goodnotes' "Highlighter thickness" and "Stroke Settings" popovers as the example: the three sizes of the pen, the pencil and the eraser are easy to reach, and should be as easy to change; and a highlighter's colors should differ from a pen's, as they are used differently. See `Architecture.md`, the paragraph on the fixed tool bar.

- Each tool of the fixed bar keeps three widths of its own, and the pixel eraser three; a second tap on the size in use opens a popover with a slider and the width in millimeters. The ink options' Width slider changes the size in use. Saved tool bars from before keep working, with the ink's and the eraser's usual sizes.
- While a highlighter is in use, the bar, the squeeze palette and the floating palette's colors menu offer light highlighter colors in place of black and the note colors; Settings › Colors has a Highlighter section to change them.
- Not done: Goodnotes' dashed and dotted strokes, which PencilKit has no ink for; and changing sizes from the floating palette, whose widths are the system's.

Tests: `PencilToolboxTests` (4 added, 4 changed): each tool's sizes change, are kept within what the ink draws, are its own and are kept between launches; the eraser's likewise; a state without sizes, or with sizes another build would not offer, reads with the usual ones; widths read in millimeters; the highlighter's colors are light, none is a note color, and they are kept. None of these has been run: like the change below, this was written without a Swift compiler, checked by a syntax parser and by reading, and not seen in the simulator or on the iPad.

## Pictures that stay sharp when shrunk and turn freely, and a highlighter that ignores tilt: 2026-10-11

Three things reported from use: a picture on a drawing or a PDF page looked pixelated when made smaller; pictures could not be rotated, only turned a quarter at a time with an icon-only button; and the highlighter's width followed the Pencil's tilt. See `Architecture.md` (the drawing record, pictures in vector drawings, pictures on PDF pages, and the paragraphs after the shape hold).

- Shrunk pictures: picture views on drawings and the selection frame's drag preview use the trilinear minification filter, and PDF views high image interpolation. The likely cause is how Core Animation's default linear filter and PDF image interpolation sample a picture shown much smaller than its pixels; it has not been confirmed on a device that this was what was seen.
- Turning freely: a handle above a selected picture's frame turns it by any angle, holding at quarter turns; moving, resizing, cropping, the quarter-turn button, reordering and undo keep the angle. Stored as an angle: version 5 of the drawing record, SVG's `rotate()` on the `<image>`, and on a PDF page a stamp whose rectangle is the box around the turned picture, with Graphite keys for the angle and size.
- Highlighter: "Highlighter Ignores Tilt" in Settings › Pencil Drawings and in the highlighter's options on the fixed bar (off by default). A marker stroke takes one even width when the Pencil lifts; while it is drawn it still shows PencilKit's tilted tip.

Tests added, none of them run yet:

- `DrawingGeometryTests` (5): angles kept within a turn and held at quarter turns nearby; a turned picture's box, hit test and offset; the part of a turned picture a crop keeps; a record with a turned picture is version 5 and keeps every angle, also with the most pictures a drawing holds, while upright pictures stay version 3; angles that do not match the pictures or the version are refused.
- `PDFPictureTests` (2): a picture turned a quarter by hand is shown turned in the open and the written document, reads back with its upright frame and angle as a standard stamp whose rectangle is the box around it, and keeps its angle when moved; an upright picture writes no angle, and a turned stamp another application resized keeps its proportions.
- `VectorPictureTests` (2): a turned picture shows turned in Core Graphics, PDFKit, Graphite's preview and Apple's SVG renderer, reads back its angle from PDF and SVG, and the SVG reader accepts only the rotation Graphite writes.
- `EvenHighlighterTests` (3): every point of a tilted highlighter stroke takes one upright tip of the tool's width on the same line; the width is in the drawing's points under a scaled stroke; other inks and unusable widths are left alone.
- `PicturesAndPaperTests` (1 hosted): on a drawing, turning by hand changes only the angle as one undoable step; moving and the quarter turn keep it; a crop keeps the part where it was shown.
- `PencilToolsTests` (1 hosted): in the drawing editor with the preference on, a highlighter stroke arrives as one width, the tool's, in one step, and a pen stroke stays as drawn.

A second, independent reading of the diff for compile errors, Swift 6 isolation, angle signs and the tests' numbers found none to fix. Its other findings were taken up: the highlighter check no longer compares the whole drawing at every stroke, the even stroke keeps the stroke's random seed, the turned-picture test now tells clockwise from counterclockwise, references to a turned PDF picture use its annotation's rectangle, a PDF picture's angle is kept within one turn whenever it is set, and on a small picture the turning handle stands clear of the top corners' handles.

Not established: this change was written without Xcode or a Swift compiler. It was checked only by a Swift syntax parser (tree-sitter) and by reading. It has not been built, the tests above and the existing suites have not been run against it, and nothing of it has been seen in the simulator or tried with Apple Pencil. That includes whether the pictures now look smooth when shrunk, how the rotation handle and its snapping feel, the handle's place on a frame at the top of the screen, and whether PencilKit draws the even highlighter's square upright tip as a band of even height in every direction. The even width appears only at the lift, which may look like a jump.

## The Pencil palette under sheets: 2026-10-10

Seen in the iPad simulator, on commit e4c22aa and on the design-review working tree alike: with a PDF of the test vault open in Write mode and the floating palette showing, Settings opened from the sidebar's gear showed the palette on top of the Settings sheet. The palette shows for an invisible first responder in the PDF view (`PencilToolPickerHostView`) while the PDF is written on, Apple draws it above every screen of the app, and presenting a sheet leaves that first responder in place, so nothing hid it. The same code path showed an embedded PDF's palette, and the drawing editor's, over any screen presented over them.

Fix: the PDF view (in a pane or embedded) and the drawing editor hide their palette while a screen is presented over their document and show it again, with the same tool, when it goes (`PresentedScreenObserver`, `UIView.isCoveredByPresentedScreen`; see `Architecture.md`, after the paragraph on the palette's first responder). A covered PDF keeps first responder but no longer takes it back from a sheet's text field; it takes it back when the screen goes, and so does the drawing editor. The note editor's Pencil double-tap uses the same covering check as before, now shared. Written in a cloud session without a compiler and handed over; it compiled here without a change.

Checked:

- Hosted tests: `PencilWorkspaceTests.testPaletteGivesWayToASheetOverThePDFAndComesBackWithTheSameTool` (a form sheet hides the palette while the PDF keeps first responder; dismissing it brings the palette back with the marker chosen before; a second sheet's text field keeps first responder through a preference update reaching the PDF's view under it, and when that sheet goes the PDF has first responder and the palette again) and `PencilToolsTests.testPresentedScreenObserverReportsAScreenComingOverAViewAndGoing` pass, with the rest of `PencilWorkspaceTests`, `PencilToolsTests` and `PDFInkHistoryTests` (33 tests), on an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) and an iPhone 17 Pro simulator, iOS 27; the whole hosted suite on the iPad simulator, 198 tests, passes.
- By hand in the iPad simulator with the test vault's `Digital_IC_Design/9_Static logic.pdf` in Write mode with the floating palette: Settings from the sidebar's gear no longer shows the palette over it, and the palette is back when Settings closes; the same with the file picker of the More menu's Add Image › Choose File…. The palette stays while its own color picker, its system menu and Graphite's accessory menu (Select Ink, Draw Shapes, Favorite Colors) are open; it steps aside while the PDF's More menu is open and returns when it closes. In the drawing editor, started from a note's Draw button, the editor shows its own palette, the photo library from Add Image › Photo Library… shows none over it, and the editor's palette is back when the library closes; cancelling the empty drawing left the note byte for byte as it was.

Not verified: the vault manager, the Versions sheet of a file with conflict versions, the iPhone's tab switcher, an embedded PDF being annotated under Settings, and anything with a physical Apple Pencil or on a device.

## Read-aloud implementation: 2026-10-11

Latest range increment: scanned-page review offers inclusive Start Here/End Here on indexed recognized words and a Whole Page reset. Corrections retain the chosen word identities and original rectangles, including replacements with phrases; a chosen passage waits for Play and stops on the current page. PDF Read Section Pages follows nested outline entries and includes full boundary pages with a disclosure; exact heading character boundaries remain open.

Checks for this increment: 24 selected macOS tests passed (`ReadAloudControllerTests`, `PDFReadAloudScanTests`), covering repeated-word selection, correction offsets, stale review identifiers, stop-before-next-page behavior, source bytes, nested outlines, shared-page headings, bookmarks and invalid/backward destinations. The iPad simulator app built and all 12 selected hosted PDF extraction/recognition/highlight tests passed. The real PDF pane test selects the second repeated word after zoom, verifies its rectangle and completion, and leaves the PDF unchanged. The captured image was visually inspected: the second “Repeat” is highlighted over the scanned text. Logs: `/tmp/graphite-reading-range-tests.log`, `/tmp/graphite-reading-range-ipad.log`; result: `/tmp/graphite-read-aloud-derived/Logs/Test/Test-Graphite-2026.10.11_16-49-50-+0200.xcresult`; inspected image: `/tmp/graphite-reading-range-attachments/FEB1917C-AD6E-4C48-A0D8-87D3D62FBFA8.png`. Outline policy and reader preparation are tested; direct menu gesture delivery, VoiceOver and physical-device behavior for the new controls remain unverified.

Latest voice review: the owner rejected the Apple Enhanced sample on 2026-10-11. This supersedes the earlier tentative acceptance. The sample used Voice 4 (`com.apple.siri.natural.Nora`, en-US, Enhanced), default native rate/pitch, about 30 seconds of technical narration; a Standard Samantha comparison was also provided. Neither the short audition nor Apple's tier labels establish acceptable naturalness. The owner then said "we can make the voice nicer later", deferring voice improvements while functional work continues. The current local engine is temporary; human-sounding narration has not passed acceptance. No document has been sent to a cloud speech service.

Scanned-text increment: image-only pages now run bounded, local Vision recognition and wait for transcript review/Play, including between native-text pages. Page Layout uses document recognition on system 26+; Line Order uses the accurate text recognizer also available on the older supported systems. Every recognized word retains its own page rectangle and derived text range. Review Recognized Words makes temporary one-line corrections, rebuilding offsets while keeping the original region; stale correction requests cannot replace the current page. Mathematical meaning is not recovered by this text recognizer. Four new Apple tests pass for exact synthetic English/decimal text, separate repeated words, known visible page coordinates, baseline text recognition, all four page rotations with a crop box, bounded corrections and invalid correction rejection. Two new native Mac tests cover mixed native/scan playback waiting for review, correction-to-original-region mapping, stale callbacks, unchanged source bytes/dirty state, real overlay geometry/pixels through zoom, touch passthrough and clearing without annotations. The delayed-display test caught PDFKit adding transient Live Text after rendering; recognized anchors now validate page revision/identity and geometry instead of requiring native text to stay empty.

Final checks for scanned reading: 145 selected read-aloud/editor regression tests passed on macOS. The iOS Simulator app built, and all 12 selected hosted tests passed on the iPad: seven existing native-layout tests, four scan recognition tests and a new real PDF-pane highlight test. The pane test verifies that both scanned text and the yellow word highlight are visible in captured pixels, native geometry stays aligned through zoom, touches pass through, Stop clears the overlay, and source bytes/annotations/dirty state remain unchanged. Its iPad screenshot was exported and visually inspected: the first “Repeat” is highlighted while the second occurrence stays clear. The synthetic source PDF was also independently rendered with Poppler and inspected. Mac cached-view captures establish overlay pixels/geometry but omit PDFKit's tiled page contents; Mac on-screen compositing remains unverified. Physical Pencil/gesture delivery, representative course scans, handwriting, complex table reading, mixed embedded-text/image regions, equation recognition, old-system runtime behavior and sustained accuracy/latency remain open. The baseline text recognizer path was exercised on the current systems, not an older operating system installation.

PDF layout increment: Page Layout/File Order is selectable and remembered. On macOS/iOS 26+, Apple's on-device document detector supplies paragraph order while narration keeps every original native PDF token and exact character coordinates. Recognition spelling is never spoken in place of selectable original text. Ambiguous/missing mappings, detected tables and older systems fall back to complete file order with a notice. Native range endpoints translate into prepared order before validation; order changes rebuild the current requested page/range, wait for Play and reject canceled speech events. Seven Apple tests passed for synthetic mixed columns/spanning headings, single-column text, exact occurrence/range retention, original offsets reversed relative to layout order, table fallback, rotated/cropped coordinates or explained fallback, file-order identity, unreadable pages and cancellation/recovery. A native controller integration test verifies repeated-word source highlighting after layout preparation, chosen endpoint stopping, safe order changes and unchanged file bytes/dirty state. The selected read-aloud/editor regression run passed 139 tests on 2026-10-11. After final raster-bound checks and reader copy changes, all 23 extraction/controller tests passed again; the iOS Simulator app build and the same seven extraction tests hosted inside the iPad app passed. The fixture was independently rendered with Poppler and visually checked; arbitrary scientific layout, recognition latency on physical devices, hyphenation and mathematical recognition remain open. Scanned text has since been added in the increment above.

Latest functional increment: word highlights now reach Markdown Reading view as well as native Source/Live Preview text, rendered Live Preview tables, equations, and nested callouts. A complete ordered comparison of source/rendered words and equation attachments is required before a block receives exact source ranges. This handles repeated words without searching by spelling; unsupported transformations disable that block's source highlights while retaining the transcript. Formula highlights cover the whole expression. Follow Reading uses actual word bounds, opens folded sections/callouts, and realizes distant lazy blocks with bounded retries. Manual source scrolling suspends following for that pane; event delivery on a device remains unverified. The Mac editor now explicitly creates TextKit 2: the inherited scrollable-text-view factory created TextKit 1 on this Mac, leaving native source geometry unavailable. Lazy PDF page progression also stops when the original document revision changes, even without a workspace notification.

Structured mathematics increment: the production Markdown reader and fixed voice sample now prepare mathematical speech through an actor-owned, bundled offline MathJax 4.1.3 / Speech Rule Engine 4.1.4 in JavaScriptCore. Concise ClearSpeak and detailed MathSpeak are selectable and remembered. Six native engine tests cover sums/products, limits, definite integrals, derivative notation, vectors, matrices, piecewise and aligned rows, indexed roots, named operators, colored expressions, grouping/sign distinctions, source-to-whole-equation mapping, rejected unknown/malformed/file/URL/macro commands, bounded inputs/depth, cancellation and subsequent recovery. Mode changes preserve the requested source range, wait for Play and reject old speech callbacks; invalidated note snapshots cannot be revived by a mode change. An additional preference/sample test passed. The complete selected regression run passed 188 tests before the final color fixture. All 57 read-aloud tests passed after the final color, preference and preview-lifecycle changes; the iOS Simulator app build also passed. The final iPad simulator hosted run of the same six engine tests passed, including the color fixture, proving packaged resource loading and JavaScriptCore execution inside the app. Preview preparation now cancels when the window reader stops or the scene becomes inactive; actual background/recording sheet interaction remains device work. Native pronunciation listening, PDF equation recognition, custom macros, standalone TeX and term-level highlighting remain unverified or unimplemented as specified in the TODO.

Checks for this increment: all 165 selected read-aloud, Reading-view/task, numbered-equation, native Mac editor/handoff, PDF-view/session, and Markdown-session tests passed on 2026-10-11. Six new native Mac rendering tests verify original ranges across Markdown transforms, safe rejection of missing/changed tokens, separate occurrences of the same word, actual layout bounds and visible highlight pixels, clearing on Stop, distant-word scrolling only when following is enabled, native editor following without changing selection or text, and rendered Live Preview table/math/collapsed-callout highlights. The new PDF revision test passed. This run also includes the production speech adapter's short audible completion check; it does not approve voice quality. The iOS Simulator app build passed after these changes with signing disabled. UIKit narration gestures/widget placement, Pencil use, physical-device timing and sustained performance remain open.

Replacement-engine preparation: `ReadAloudAudioTiming` validates original-text character timings and queries word ranges using a media playback clock. Four tests passed for Unicode/combining-mark offsets, source mapping, silence and backwards seeking, text/number changes, canonical spelling changes, malformed arrays, and invalid timestamps. It is not yet connected to a generated-audio player or online/offline replacement engine. A combined run of 88 read-aloud/PDF-view/PDF-session/Markdown-session tests passed on 2026-10-11, including actual native speech completion.

Additional implemented controls: Markdown heading-section selection (including subsections and Setext headings), native PDF selection endpoints for Read From Here and separately marked start/end ranges, previous/next sentence, repeat passage, and optional PDF following. A later run of 37 focused Core/Apple/UI tests passed, including Unicode/CRLF section boundaries, endpoint stopping across pages, stale/reversed ranges, sentence seeking and stale callbacks, and native PDF geometry when following is suspended/resumed. A separate real `AppleReadAloudSpeech` integration test passed: the actual adapter spoke “Read this sentence.” and delivered native word ranges plus completion. It does not establish subjective quality, long-duration drift, or physical-device behavior. The PDF following test exercises real PDFView geometry and controller policy; native manual-scroll event delivery and a visible full SwiftUI pane were not visually verified. The latest iOS Simulator app build passed after these additions and timestamp validation, with signing disabled. Physical-device validation remains open.

[Read-aloud TODO](Read-aloud-todo.md) retains the full request and deferred natural-voice acceptance work. More › Read Aloud is available in Markdown and PDF panes. Notes support the document, an editor selection, from the cursor, a heading section, and a chosen start through the cursor/selection. PDFs support the document, current page, from the current page, a page-position range, and native selected text, including discontiguous pieces and cross-page selections. Selected pieces retain their exact page/range anchors, so repeated text is not relocated by searching. In the app, reading starts once the passage is prepared; the toolbar's Pause/Resume and Reading Controls popover offer previous/next sentence, speed, Follow Reading and Stop, and Reading Options holds the voice, math/PDF order and transcript (see the 2026-10-12 entry below). The current-passage voice preview was removed: Play reads the current passage, and Play Sample plays a fixed prose/math sample through the same native speech path without changing the chosen voice.

Read Aloud polish, 2026-10-12 (branch `alpha-readaloud`, simulator "Graphite Read Aloud iPad", iPad Pro 11-inch (M5), iOS 27.0; assessment and screenshots in `~/.graphite-readaloud-build/assessment.md` and `shots/`):
- Voice: the iOS 27 simulator offers only compact voices plus the MacinTalk voices; before, the automatic choice there was Fred. After the change Samantha (Compact) is chosen and the download pointer shows (simulator screenshots `b02`, `c03`, `e09`). Unit tests cover the ranking with stand-in Premium, Enhanced and Personal voices, the automatic choice following a newly installed Premium voice, an explicit choice persisting, the passage's language overriding the device's (French and English passages), and the exclusion of this Mac's real MacinTalk and Eloquence voices. **Not verified:** how any Premium or Enhanced voice sounds, the Personal Voice permission prompt and reading with a Personal Voice (both need a real iPad; the simulator reports Personal Voice as unsupported).
- Notes that failed before ("could not safely match"): Lecture_L01_Shannon, 9_Static logic, 4_Logic design, 06 Math, 05 Callouts, 07 Tables from the test vault now prepare; Core tests cover smart punctuation, quoted display math, escaped table pipes, tables with headers, callout titles, web addresses and abbreviations (including the Reading view's written-word tokens).
- PDF pages: Core tests cover bullets, dot leaders, line joins, hyphenated line ends, a running footer with the page number, and its pieces on a later page. The narration of 9_Static logic pages 1, 2, 6, 13 and 21 and of the VLSI textbook's contents page was compared before and after. Not verified: two-column papers from the owner's vaults, and footers whose text never appears with a page number.
- Controls: in the simulator, Read Document started reading at once on a note and on the slides, Pause/Resume and the popover worked in light and dark appearance (`c01`, `c02`, `c06`), the Reading Options form and transcript highlight (`e06`), Settings › Read Aloud (`e09`). Contrast measured in the dark popover over a white PDF page: secondary text 5.9:1, accent link 4.5:1. Not verified: VoiceOver order and labels, the largest Dynamic Type sizes, iPhone widths, hardware keyboard.
- Background and lock screen: in the simulator, reading continued after locking the screen; the system log showed the Now Playing entry advancing passage by passage. The simulator's lock screen does not draw Now Playing, so the lock-screen controls, headphone buttons, unplugging headphones, a phone call interruption and the Ring/Silent switch are **not verified** and need a real iPad.
- Pauses between passages (0.6 s around headings, 0.35 s after paragraphs, 0.25 s after list items, table rows and slide lines) are covered by a session test of the values handed to the speech engine; they were not listened to.
- Follow-up the same day (branch `alpha-readaloud-2`): Graphite follows the system's Read & Speak voice by default and sets `prefersAssistiveTechnologySettings`; a voice chosen in Graphite overrides it; the picker lists every offered voice grouped by quality. Session tests cover following the system voice even when it is one Graphite would not offer (a stand-in "Fred"), the system-settings flag on and off, hiding Graphite's speed while an assistive feature applies the system rate, and the fallback to the best installed voice. In the simulator, Settings › Read Aloud shows "Same as Read & Speak (Samantha, Default)" and the grouped list (`shots/f02`, `f03`). `swift test` 1,780 tests, 0 failures (1 skipped); hosted Read Aloud tests (PDFReadAloudExtraction, PDFReadAloudScan, ReadAloudMathematics, ReadAloudScanHighlight) 18 passed. **Only the iPad can confirm:** that `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice(language:)` is the voice chosen in Read & Speak (Aaron, Enhanced, on the owner's iPad); whether the system applies Read & Speak's rate, pitch, Timbre, Sentence Pause and Equalizer with Speak Selection on (Apple's header only promises it for assistive technologies "like VoiceOver"); and that Graphite's pauses still sound right then.
- Third round (branch `alpha-readaloud-3`), after the owner reported a robotic voice, no highlight at all and freezing scrolls on the iPad (build 2973891): the iPad's preferences held `GraphiteReadAloudVoice` = Eloquence Eddy from an earlier build; that key is now removed at launch (unit test with Eddy stored). In the simulator, the reading view showed no highlight in a paragraph containing an apostrophe, quote or dash (`shots/h02-seq.png`); after the fix it follows word by word (`h04-reading-view-fixed.png`); `ReadAloudReadingViewTests.testTypographicMarksAndRespelledTermsKeepTheirParagraphMapped` fails without the fix. Hosted `ReadAloudLectureHighlightTests` read a lecture-style note (properties, headings, math, a missing embed, apostrophes, quotes, dashes, acronyms in every paragraph) with the simulator's real voice and assert a highlight appears and moves on in Live Preview, the reading view and a PDF; a fourth test scrolls Live Preview by 45 points per frame for 3 s while reading and asserts no frame gap above 100 ms and no pull back. On macOS, Eddy, Fred, Samantha and Nora all report word ranges, so a voice without ranges is unlikely to be the device cause, but the passage fallback covers it. UI pass in the simulator: light, dark and the largest accessibility text size (the popover broke words apart there; fixed, `i06`/`i07`), start from the cursor, Pause/Resume, popover, Voice and Transcript, Settings (Highlight While Reading, Pronunciation), switching notes while reading and coming back, Stop. **Not verified:** rotating while reading, the iPad itself (whether the owner's voice reports words, how IPA sounds, real scrolling hitches on the device), Instruments traces.
- Fourth round (branch `alpha-readaloud-4`, from alpha 98f58c5): pronunciations (unit tests of the mined top terms, plurals, possessives, the rule for unknown capitals, units, subscripts, symbols, highlight mapping, opt-in IPA); no machine listening check was possible (speech recognition needs a permission a command-line tool cannot get, and the phoneme API returned nothing); an audio file of the top terms said by Samantha is in `~/.graphite-readaloud-build/listen/top60-samantha.m4a` for the owner. PDF word highlight: reproduced with the owner's ISCA paper (no highlight at all) and fixed (16 and 19 words lit on two pages in 12 s); a unit test of the alignment and a hosted test reading a two-page PDF (word by word, the respelled CMOS lit, into the second page). Hyphenation: 320 hyphenated line breaks from two of the owner's papers, 21 right before (hyphen always kept), 302 after; the other 18 are cross-column or figure lines; unit tests of soft hyphen, Unicode hyphen, en dash, compounds and the highlight spanning both halves. Folding: unit tests (collapsed callout read as title, open one read, the person's expansion either way, folded heading) and a controller test (unfolding while reading); seen in the simulator on Lecture_L01_Shannon. Player and navigation: hosted tests (section skip, tap jump, "Read from Here" after scrolling away, player never covering the highlighted line); by hand in light, dark and the largest text size (two rows); Pronunciations editor writing the vault file. **Not verified:** landscape, the iPad itself (word highlight with the owner's PDFs on device, how respellings sound with Aaron, lock screen queue buttons), a sleep timer and range end markers (not built), tap-to-read in the reading view and on PDF pages (not built).
- Fifth round (branch `alpha-readaloud-5`, from alpha a13b034 with 90fed8c merged): the owner's screenshot of pale blue pills on clear glass over a white PDF page was reproduced (light and dark) and the player redesigned. Contrast measured from simulator screenshots: white labels 11.0:1 against the panel over a white page and 17.6:1 over a photo and a dark slide, secondary text 6.0:1, close glyph 17.3:1; Increase Contrast (edge, darker panel) and Reduce Transparency (opaque #1C1C1E, measured) seen in the simulator; the largest text size seen (text grows, page label drops, six controls stay). Unit tests: placement (stays put, moves off the word, neighbors) and drag positions; the pen's start, range, reversed range and reset. Hosted tests: the pen on a PDF page (reads from the tapped word, then exactly to the second tap, two marks, no stroke, no annotation, no change to the PDF, marks gone on stop), the pen's range in High Quality (only those words prepared), the player clear of the word on a PDF and in a landscape-wide window, and the earlier player test on a note. By hand on the owner's ISCA paper: pen with the floating palette (player moved above it), Reading Selection. **Not verified:** a real rotation (this Xcode has no Simulator app; landscape is a wide hosted window), Apple Pencil itself (taps were fingers with finger drawing on), the pen on the iPad, VoiceOver by ear, and the floating palette moved to another edge (the bottom band is assumed while it floats). Not built: the pen in drawings, the reading view's tap to read, and a sleep timer.
- Reading while writing (branch `alpha-readaloud-6`, from alpha 5a36dfa): hosted tests read a two-page PDF range while a pen stroke, a highlighter stroke, an erase and a save are made (no interruption, the same passages as without ink, the highlight moving on after the save); a scanned page with "BAI" written across it in ink (recognition of the page as shown reads BAI; the text Read Aloud recognizes does not); a note edited above the sentence read and inside it (reading goes on in the same passage, the highlight in that sentence) and with the sentence removed (paused with a message). Unit tests of `ReadAloudReanchoring`. **Not verified:** on the iPad with the owner's `2402.18736v2.pdf` and Apple Pencil; pages inserted or removed while a PDF is read (re-anchoring by page identity is untested); edits typed continuously while reading (each pause of 400 ms restarts the sentence).
- Tests on the alpha-readaloud commit: `swift test` 1,779 tests (GraphiteUITests 551 with 1 skipped, GraphiteIndexTests 137, GraphiteCoreTests 851, GraphiteAppleTests 240), 0 failures. Hosted iPad suite (`GraphiteIntegrationTests` on "Graphite Read Aloud iPad Tests"): 287 tests, 1 skipped, 1 failure, `EvenHighlighterTests.testTheTipTurnsAcrossTheStrokeInEveryDirection` (26 against 24 ± 1 points), which also fails alone and comes from alpha's highlighter work (bfd29de), not from Read Aloud.

Markdown narration uses the existing parser and extension scanners, reads headings and link labels without their markers, omits properties/comments/block identifiers/footnotes, skips fenced code/HTML/images/embedded files with notices, and reads inline code literally. Source mappings handle UTF-16, CRLF, Unicode, Markdown escapes, and decoded entities. Prose endpoints expand to complete words; partial equations and unsafe mappings are rejected. Production English math speech uses bounded MathJax/Speech Rule Engine interpretation described above; Core's pure Swift fallback is still a smaller conservative subset. Unsupported equations are announced. PDF text is extracted or recognized one page at a time on worker-owned PDFKit/Vision objects, using the current page export. Scans have reviewable/correctable recognized text and word-region highlights; PDF mathematical recognition remains unimplemented.

Actual native spoken-range callbacks drive transcript highlights, temporary Markdown editor/Reading-view overlays, and temporary selections over original PDF words. PDF highlighting validates character counts, UTF-16/composed-character ranges, exact source text, and PDFKit selection round trips. It preserves user selection and existing search highlights, makes no annotations, and adds no save or undo operation. Unsupported source coordinates remain undrawn; when a page export cannot be matched, the reader explains that only transcript highlighting is available. Show in PDF reveals the current word, or starting page before playback, in an open source pane without changing text selection. Optional source following is implemented; native manual-scroll event delivery and physical-device interaction remain unverified. Callback routing and session transitions are covered using a substitute speech adapter; this does not establish real audio/highlight drift. Individual formula-term highlighting is incomplete. Tab changes retain the window-owned session, vault changes clear it, and edits invalidate its offsets. Narration pauses on backgrounding and is unavailable during an active lecture recording. No simultaneous recording/playback, lock-screen controls, durable resume positions, or background-audio entitlement is claimed.

Verification: 31 focused read-aloud tests passed across Core, Apple, and UI. Tests cover semantic output, decoded-entity/source mappings, Unicode and CRLF, bounded chunks, ranges and partial equations, malformed/unsupported math, real-event-shaped timing validation, stale callbacks, preferred-voice removal, rate/interruption recovery, asynchronous request replacement, edit invalidation, PDF range advancement and endpoint stopping, and image-only/blank-page failures. Added PDF tests cover the exact repeated occurrence, cross-page/discontiguous selections, Unicode (combining marks, a ligature, and an emoji), rotated-page selection bounds, invalid coordinates/text, preserving native and search selections, explicit source reveal, and unchanged source bytes/annotation counts/dirty state. A hosted native Mac PDF pane test verifies speech events actually reach its highlights and Stop removes them; it caught and fixed missing observation of the source PDF. A combined run of 77 read-aloud/PDF-view/PDF-session/Markdown-session tests passed. The earlier first-increment run of 870 Core/read-aloud/Markdown-session/reading-view tests also passed; it was not repeated for this PDF increment. Native file-coordination tests require macOS access outside the shell sandbox. The iOS Simulator app build passed with signing disabled.
