The default Swift build engine encountered an existing resource-bundle signing/xattr error; the native SwiftPM build engine completed the test runs.

Initial voice inventory, before the later audible audition, on this development Mac: the production adapter's Apple/non-novelty/non-personal filters found one installed English Enhanced voice, named “Voice 4”, and no English Premium voices. Other English voices reported Standard quality. This did not play or evaluate audio and says nothing about the owner's iPad inventory; native voice availability is device-specific.

Not verified: human-voice quality or the ten-minute owner listening gate, native audible playback and timing on a physical device, voice download/availability across supported OS versions, representative scientific PDF reading order/scan accuracy beyond synthetic fixtures, VoiceOver/Dynamic Type/contrast and narrow-width usability, Pencil interaction while reading, sustained performance/battery use, or headset/call interruption behavior. Premium/Enhanced labels are Apple's metadata; they are not a naturalness score. Cloud narration, PDF/scanned mathematical recognition, standalone TeX, pronunciation overrides, and PDF outline section controls remain open. Encrypted PDFs require selected-text reading; page reading reports its current limitation. Input and speech-size budgets are documented in Architecture.md; whole-note preparation currently rejects oversized notes even for a small selection.

## Apple design review fixes: 2026-10-10

A review against Apple's Human Interface Guidelines (the apple-design skill, run on the iPad and iPhone simulators in light, dark and the largest text size) found three accessibility failures and several smaller problems; this change fixes them. The app's own appearance setting stays, for Obsidian parity, at the owner's choice. A new app icon is a separate change.

- **Text size.** A note's text follows the system text size (`GraphitePreferences.noteTextSize`): the Appearance slider (12 to 28 points) sets the size at the default text size, and Larger Text scales it as body text, in Live Preview, Source mode, reading view and on canvases. Footnote numbers are at least 11 points. At the accessibility sizes a property's name goes above its value, and its type symbol grows with it. Checked on the iPhone simulator at the largest accessibility size: the inline title is larger than the Properties heading again, and names no longer read "tit…".
- **Callout titles.** Light appearance draws a callout's title, icon and chevron in its color darkened to 4.5:1 on the callout's tint (`CalloutAppearance.titleColor`); measured from the iPad simulator, a success callout's title went from 2.0:1 to about 4.5:1. Unit test: `AccentLegibilityTests.testCalloutTitleReadsOnItsOwnTint`.
- **Accent.** `AccentLegibility` darkens an accent too light for light appearance (Amber, Orange, Teal, Green, Purple and others below 4.5:1 on `#F2F2F7`), as it lightened dark ones for dark appearance, and aims at 7:1 with Increase Contrast; the dark background's luminance is now computed, so lightened accents reach 4.5:1 rather than 4.49. Unit tests: `AccentLegibilityTests`. Increase Contrast also strengthens the selected fill and hairlines. With Increase Contrast on in the iPad simulator, callout titles were drawn darker again (aiming at 7:1).
- **Touch targets.** A tag's remove mark, a tab's close button (28 points), a heading's fold chevron (28 points, in reading view and Live Preview) and Edit Source take touches over at least 28 points; tab-bar buttons and the Write toggle are 36 points and take touches over the bar's 44-point height (`GraphiteChrome.barHeight`, 40 before). Seen in the simulators; hit areas were not measured with Accessibility Inspector.
- **Toolbar.** In regular widths the window's toolbar holds Back, Forward, the document's controls and More; Quick Switcher, Command Palette and Focus end each document's More menu (`WorkspaceMoreMenuItems`), and Exit Focus stays in the toolbar while in Focus. A PDF's page and a note's word count are the title's subtitle (iPadOS 26 and later) rather than a toolbar item and a floating capsule. One More symbol (`ellipsis`) everywhere; Back and Forward are chevrons on iPad and iPhone. Checked on the iPad simulator: a PDF's title reads "9_Static logic.pdf" in full with "Page 1 of 44" under it, where it had read "9_Static l…" beside 11 controls.
- **The fixed bar's tools.** The inks, the eraser and the lasso are SF Symbols, each ink with a bar of its color under it, the tool in use on the bar's faint fill (`PencilToolGlyph`), in place of tools drawn standing upright. Seen on the iPad simulator in the tab bar with a PDF written on.
- **Undo and Redo while writing on a PDF.** Hidden in the toolbar and the tab bar while the floating palette, which has its own, is over the page (`PDFSession.showsFloatingPencilPalette`).
- **Apple Pencil while reading a PDF.** A Pencil touching a page in Read switches to Write; that touch draws nothing. Not verified: the simulator sends finger touches only, so this needs an iPad and a Pencil.
- **Reduce Motion.** Folding, the pages sidebar, animated scrolling and zooming go without animation, and slides become fades (`GraphiteMotion`). Not checked with Reduce Motion on in a simulator.
- **Wording.** Labels, titles, toggles, pickers and menu items are title case throughout (Settings had Obsidian's sentence case beside title-case buttons); the Settings footer now says the vault switcher is at the top of the sidebar, where it is; the inline title follows Show File Extensions like the tab and title bar. Alerts take the first sentence of their message as the title instead of "Graphite" (`MessageAlert`; checked with a link to a missing note), and the two messages that only inform (a file moved to the vault's trash folder, several files matching a link) show in a banner over the document instead of an alert (`NoticeBanner`, not yet seen on screen). Search results' folder paths, group counts and file extensions in the list use the secondary text color instead of the tertiary one (about 1.7:1 on white).
- **Rendered blocks after a jump or a change of appearance.** A jump to a heading no longer leaves the callout above it drawn over the heading, and changing to dark appearance at the largest text size no longer draws the embedded PDF over the properties; both stayed wrong until the note was scrolled. A freeze found on the way (the views removed and rebuilt without end while TextKit had no viewport range) is gone too. Checked before and after on the iPad and iPhone simulators; hosted tests `LivePreviewBlockPlacementTests` (2: the callout above a heading jumped to does not cover it; appearance changes leave no views overlapping and the editor responsive), which failed or timed out without the fix and passed with it on the iPad and iPhone simulators.
- **App icon.** The graphite crystal replaces the glass note and stylus in all five presets (blue by default, red, steel for Black, a darker Charcoal, blue graphite), as nine vector facets per Icon Composer document, generated with the Settings previews by `Design/GraphiteIcon/GraphiteCrystal/make_graphite_crystal_icon.py`; Settings › Appearance › App Icon describes it. The compiled blue icon was seen in the iPad simulator's Dock beside Apple's icons. `AppIconBundleTests` checks the five registrations and previews.
- **Inline code shortcut.** ⌃⌘C, beside the headings' ⌃⌘1 to ⌃⌘6, rather than ⌘`, which cycles through an app's windows. Not tried with a hardware keyboard.
- Checks run: the iOS simulator build; the macOS package tests (`swift test`, 1,618 passed; `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading` failed once while simulator builds ran beside it and passed three times on its own); the hosted integration tests on an iPad simulator (196 tests). That run caught a fault of this change: the word count's subtitle was first applied only once there was a count, and switching between the pane with and without a subtitle made SwiftUI build the note's editor again, losing its cursor and revealed markup (9 of 10 `LivePreviewRevealTests` failed, all 10 passing on the commit before); the subtitle is now always applied, empty when there is nothing to show, and the PDF's page subtitle likewise. `PicturesAndPaperTests` now expects the tools' 40-point row centered in the 44-point tab bar (`PencilToolbarMetrics.heightInTabBar`). After both, all 196 passed. A Release build was installed on the owner's iPad.

## PDF ink undo after the page's ink is read back: 2026-10-10

Reported by the owner on their iPad: Undo on a PDF page answered "The ink on this page changed in a way this step does not know, so it was not undone." Cause, found by experiment in the iPad simulator: PencilKit stores a stroke's color and transform as 32-bit numbers. A stroke in a color made from a hex value, as the fixed bar's colors are, or a stroke the lasso moved, reads back from the page's stored drawing a little different from the stroke drawn (a red component of 0.8313725490 reads back as 0.8313725590), and `PDFStrokeFingerprint` compared those numbers at full precision. Undo then refused every step drawn before the page's ink was read back, which happens when a tab switch rebuilds the PDF view and when a page's canvas, released far from the page shown, is made again. Black ink and strokes the eraser cut read back exactly. Locations and creation dates read back exactly too.

Fix: the fingerprint keeps a stroke's color and transform at the precision PencilKit stores them with (`PDFStrokeFingerprint`, GraphiteApple). A recolored or moved stroke still counts as changed.

Checked in iOS 27.0 simulators (iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) and iPhone 17 Pro), Xcode 27.0:

- `PDFInkHistoryTests.testColoredAndMovedStrokesAreUndoneAfterThePageInkIsReadBackFromTheFile` draws a colored stroke and a moved one on a page, switches to another tab and back, undoes and redoes, then scrolls far enough to release the page's canvas and undoes both. `testColoredAndMovedStrokesKeepTheirPlaceInTheHistoryThroughTheStoredDrawing` undoes a change against the drawing read back from its stored form, and checks that reading it back is no change while a recolored stroke still is. Without the fix both failed on the iPad simulator with the owner's message; the other seven tests of the class, which draw black ink at whole-number points, passed.
- With the fix, the whole `GraphiteIntegrationTests` target passed: 194 tests on the iPad simulator, and on the iPhone simulator 191 with 3 Canvas tests skipped, as they are at a phone's width. The changed file is built only for UIKit, so the macOS suite does not run it and was not run again.

Not verified: Undo with real Pencil strokes on the owner's iPad after this change.

## Apple Pencil double-tap in the drawing editor: 2026-10-10

Reported by the owner on their iPad: a double-tap of Apple Pencil in the drawing editor closed the editor and opened it again. Cause, found by reading the code: a Pencil gesture reaches every `UIPencilInteraction` in the window, and the note's text view stays in the window under the full-screen drawing editor. The note's handler checked only that its text view had a window, so it answered too and asked for the drawing at the note's cursor. The editor is presented with `fullScreenCover(item:)`, so the new request closed it and presented another, without the strokes not yet saved. Neither tool layout nor the kind of drawing changes this: the note's handler runs the same with the floating palette and the fixed bar, for a new drawing and for a drawing opened from its embed.

Fix: the note answers a double-tap or squeeze only when no screen is presented over it (`NativeMarkdownEditor.Coordinator`), and `WorkspaceModel.beginDrawingAtCursor` does nothing while a drawing is open. The editor's own answer is unchanged: Apple's palette, or the fixed bar's `PencilGestureReceiver`, following Settings › Apple Pencil.

Checked: the change was written in a Linux container without Xcode, then built and run on a Mac the same day (Xcode 27.0, macOS 27.0.1) in iOS 27.0 simulators, an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) and an iPhone 17 Pro. With only the two new hosted tests on top of the code before the fix, both failed on the iPad simulator as the report describes: the note stayed in the window under the editor, its double-tap replaced the open drawing's request, and the editor closed and was presented again; the other 14 tests of `PencilToolsTests` passed. With the fix, all 16 passed on both simulators. `swift test` on macOS ran 1,616 tests (792 core, 137 index, 191 Apple, 496 UI) with the measurement test skipped as before and one failure, `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading`, in the Mac editor, which this change does not touch, while a simulator build ran at the same time; on its own it passed three times out of three. The two new tests:

- `testPencilDoubleTapInTheDrawingEditorKeepsItOpenWithItsStrokes` presents the drawing editor over the note as `GraphiteRootView` does, opens it with the note's double-tap handler, draws a stroke, then gives the note the double-tap and squeeze again, as UIKit does. The request, the presented editor and the stroke stay. With the fixed bar (regular width only), the bar's receiver switches to the eraser and the editor stays. A drawing embedded in the note opens as itself and keeps a stroke added to it. The test first checks that the note is still in the window under the editor, which is what the cause depends on.
- `testANoteLeavesPencilGesturesToAScreenOverItAndNeverReplacesAnOpenDrawing`: with a screen presented over the note, the handlers start nothing; while a drawing is requested but its editor is not on screen yet, a double-tap does not replace it; with neither, the double-tap starts a drawing.

Checked by the owner on their iPad Pro 11-inch (3rd generation), iPadOS 27.0, with a Release build of this change and the fixed bar: a double-tap of Apple Pencil while drawing in a note's drawing no longer closes it and opens it again; a Debug build made the same day before the change still did. Not verified on the device: the floating palette, and that the double-tap does what Settings › Apple Pencil asks with each layout.

## Obsidian community plugins: 2026-10-10

At the owner's request, ahead of the order `OBJECTIVE.md` had set: Graphite runs Obsidian community plugins from `.obsidian/plugins`, installs them from Obsidian's community directory or a GitHub repository, and shares `community-plugins.json` and each plugin's `data.json` with Obsidian. `Community-plugins.md` describes the runtime, the sixteen real plugins it was run with, and every gap; `Architecture.md` adds the storage contract and the risks.

- The runtime (`Sources/GraphiteUI/Resources/CommunityPluginRuntime`): Graphite's implementation of the `obsidian` module for a web view, with CodeMirror 6, Lezer, moment, js-yaml and Lucide vendored at the versions Obsidian 1.14.4 declares. All 103 classes of `obsidian.d.ts` 1.14.4, 422 of its 442 class methods and 51 of its 56 functions and constants exist (`Community-plugin-API-coverage.md`); some report themselves as unsupported when called.
- GraphiteCore: `CommunityPluginManifest`, `CommunityPluginList`, `installedCommunityPlugins` with `CommunityPluginCompatibility`, `CommunityPluginVaultBridge`, `CommunityPluginEditorChange`, `CommunityPluginMarkdownRendering`, `CommunityPluginNetworkRequest`, `CommunityPluginInstaller`. GraphiteApple: `CommunityPluginSecretStore` (keychain). GraphiteUI: `CommunityPluginHost` (the web view and its messages), the scheme handler, the plugin panel, notices and menus, the palette's plugin commands, and Settings › Community plugins with the directory browser. `VaultStore`'s two settings-file helpers became internal so `community-plugins.json` is read and merged like Obsidian's other settings files.

Checked on a Mac and in the iPad simulator (2026-10-10, afternoon; macOS 27, Xcode 27, Swift 6.4, iOS 27, Node 26.8.2; details in `Community-plugins.md`, How it was verified):

- Builds: the package for macOS, the app for the iPad simulator, and the Mac app (as a test host). The Swift written in the container compiled without a change.
- `swift test`: Core 809 (`CommunityPluginTests`, 17), Index 137, Apple 192 (`CommunityPluginSecretStoreTests` on the login keychain), UI 496 with one skipped; all pass. The first run crashed the UI tests: removing a vault from the list called `WKWebsiteDataStore.remove(forIdentifier:)` before anything had started WebKit, which crashes inside WebKit; fixed by asking for the default data store first.
- iPad integration tests on the simulator (iPad Pro 13-inch (M5)): 196, all passing (the 190 that ran before this work, and six new). They include the new `CommunityPluginHostTests` (a plugin running in WebKit inside the app: an editor command and its Undo, a command while an insertion is queued, `data.json` and `community-plugins.json` as Obsidian writes them, the keychain, the vault's own web storage surviving a restart, `isInspectable` in Debug, full-rate timers while no panel shows the plugin, unloading when another vault opens) and `CommunityPluginSecretStoreTests` on the data protection keychain.
- `npm test`: 54 tests, all passing, with sixteen real plugins built from their sources with their own lockfiles: the nine below, and Recent Files, Style Settings, Homepage, Calendar, Advanced Tables, Commander and Iconize, which the container could not build.
- By hand in the iPad simulator with a copy of `Tests/test_vault`: turning plugins on, Browse, installing Natural Language Dates 0.6.4, Templater 2.25.1 and Tasks 8.5.0 from GitHub, their options inline in Settings, their commands from the palette changing the note (date at the cursor, a task ticked with today's date, a Templater template inserted from its modal on the plugin panel), Undo taking each change back byte for byte, `data.json` and `community-plugins.json` in two-space JSON, a plugin's notice and menu. Timers off screen measured 6 ticks of a 100 ms interval in 5.2 seconds; after the fix, 48 in 5 seconds. A 5,000-note vault's metadata was read in 8.5 seconds with the main thread waiting for work in 97% of its samples.
- Fixed: the crash above; CodeMirror modules the runtime provides listed as missing; unranked directory search; concurrent `vault.process` calls conflicting or overwriting each other (Tasks' 22 property types at every launch, with a dozen identical notices); Graphite's own notices neither capped nor merged; plugins stalled off screen; Commander's look-ups of `app.statusBar` and the settings header; commands refused while an insertion was queued; secrets refused on the Mac (-34018), now kept in the login keychain there; the plugin build script ignoring lockfiles, and Iconize's `env.js`.
- Not verified: a physical iPad or iPhone, the iPhone simulator, the Mac app running; Safari's Web Inspector showing the page; a content process ended by iOS; memory, start-up time on a device, and 100,000-note vaults. Once, the first command after the plugins started inserted the date at the start of the note instead of at the cursor; not reproduced in three later runs.

First checked in a Linux container without Xcode or a Swift compiler:

- `npm test` in `Tests/CommunityPluginRuntimeTests`: 45 tests, all passing, in jsdom 26 against a vault folder on disk, among them nine real plugins built from their sources at fixed commits: Sample Plugin, Natural Language Dates, Templater, QuickAdd, Tag Wrangler, Dataview, Tasks and Outliner load, Kanban is refused with its reason. Templater replaced templates in a note through the vault; Natural Language Dates inserted the date, Tasks toggled a task done and Sample Plugin replaced the selection, each through the editor change. Outliner's commands need CodeMirror's editor view and fail saying so. jsdom is not WebKit: it lacks CSS nesting, Web Workers and IndexedDB (Dataview ran with a worker stand-in and fake-indexeddb).
- The Swift was not compiled. Every new and changed Swift file parses with tree-sitter-swift 0.6.0. A separate review against the project's declarations and the SDKs found two certain and two likely compile errors and nine logic problems, all fixed (`Community-plugins.md`, How it was verified). `CommunityPluginTests` (Core) was written and not run.

Not implemented then: CodeMirror editor extensions in Graphite's native editor, plugins' post-processors and code blocks in the reading view, typing suggestions and live editor events, plugins' items in context menus, hover previews, plugin views as tabs or panels (`Community-plugins.md`, What does not work yet). All but the editor extensions and hover previews came on 2026-10-12 (above).

## A quieter interface and a phone layout: 2026-10-06

At the owner's request, after Obsidian's Minimal theme: the interface and the Pencil bar felt cluttered, and the iPhone layout unconsidered. The eighth increment of `Premium-experience-plan.md` lists the changes; `Architecture.md` describes them under the shared document controls and the fixed tool bar.

- One Write toggle (`DocumentModeToggle`) replaces the segmented Read/Write control in notes, PDFs and canvases, in the toolbar, the tab bar and the control row. Show/Hide Tools left the PDF's toolbar and tab bar for a Show Pencil Tools switch in its More menu.
- Bars take the page's color with a hairline; tabs are muted names with a faint fill for the tab in use and a close button on that tab and under the pointer; the empty tab is a column of links that fits a phone; folder icons, the vault bar and the keyboard toolbar's icons are muted; sidebar rows are denser.
- Headings have one scale and weight in Live Preview, Source mode and Reading view (`HeadingTypography`); the inline title is semibold.
- Compact widths: the document comes first and the file list is a step back; no tab bar; a bottom bar with back, forward, the quick switcher, a new note, the tab switcher (`TabSwitcher`) and the command palette; the navigation bar holds only the document's controls; a PDF hides the bottom bar while the palette is docked; the quick switcher keeps its results above the keyboard.
- The fixed bar and the squeeze palette draw each tool standing upright with its tip in the ink's color, the tool in use raised (`PencilToolGlyph`).

The two commits that made these changes were written in a Linux container without Xcode or a Swift compiler: they were parsed with tree-sitter and reviewed by reading, nothing more. The same day they were built, run and corrected on a Mac (Xcode 27.0, macOS 27.0) and in iOS 27.0 simulators, an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) and an iPhone 17 Pro, with a copy of `Tests/test_vault` (its lecture notes and PDFs, without the videos). No iOS 18 simulator runtime is installed, and no physical device was used.

- Builds: the package and the app built as committed, with no errors and no new warnings. SwiftPM's build folder has to be outside the iCloud-synced Desktop (`swift build --scratch-path …`): in the repository's `.build`, codesign refuses the resource bundles for their extended attributes.
- `swift test`: 1,613 tests in four bundles as the two commits left them, and 1,616 at the end of the work below, none failing and one skipped, the three updated tests among them. `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading` failed once on this branch and once on `main` (e29dcc6), and passed on the next run: it depends on timing.
- `GraphiteIntegrationTests`: on the iPad simulator, 189 of 189 as the two commits left them, and 190 of 190 after the fixes below (one test added). On the iPhone simulator, three canvas tests and the equation-number test failed, on `main` as well: they were written for an iPad's width. The canvas tests now skip on a phone, saying why, and the equation test runs only its narrow case there. The last full runs, at the end of the work below: 190 of 190 on the iPad simulator, and 190 on the iPhone simulator with those 3 skipped and none failing.
- Seen in the simulators, light and dark: the Write toggle reads as on (accent pencil on a faint fill) and off (muted pencil) in the iPad tab bar, and as a filled accent circle in the iPhone's navigation bar; headings measure the same width in Read and Write; the iPhone opens on the document, not the file list, and choosing the file already open, after going back to the list, shows it again; the bottom bar hides while a PDF's palette is shown and comes back when Show Pencil Tools is turned off; Go to Page in the PDF's More menu opens its sheet on the iPhone and goes to the page; the quick switcher's results end above the software keyboard; the tab switcher lists, switches and adds tabs.
- Measured in the iPhone simulator, on a Mac with an Apple M5 Max (not device figures; a phone is several times slower): scrolling a 54 KB lecture note in Live Preview with four flicks over 12 seconds kept the app's main thread busy 74 ms in all, and Time Profiler reported no hang.

Fixed after running it:

- The iPhone's navigation bar had no room for the document's controls: beside the editable title and the always-present Record button, iOS put Write and More, and for a PDF Undo and Redo, into an overflow menu, where Write was a checked item and Undo a submenu. The title is now a plain one that truncates, Rename is in More, and the Record button shows only while there is a recording to follow; a recording starts from the command palette.
- The iPhone's file list was the iPad sidebar drawn as inset cards with separators, a blurred large title under the panel picker, a segmented picker cutting off "Bookmarks", and the vault bar above the search field. The file list on both is now drawn as Obsidian's file explorer (chevrons before folder names, indents with guide lines, no icons or separators, extensions muted after names), the panel picker shows symbols, and on a phone the list is plain, its inline title switches vaults, and the vault bar is gone.
- A note with an embedded PDF raised the note's keyboard toolbar as it opened, with nothing being typed: PDFKit makes the embed's document view first responder, which inherits its ancestor's input accessory. The PDF view now has an empty one.
- On a phone the keyboard toolbar stood over the bottom bar while typing with a hardware keyboard; the bottom bar now gives way while the keyboard is up or a note takes typing.
- The inline title showed `.md`; it is the name without it, as Obsidian's is.
- On a phone, choosing a note in the file list slid in the note that was open before and swapped it for the new one after the slide, about 0.4 seconds after the touch, with the title changing 0.2 seconds later still. The document now slides in once the chosen note is loaded: recorded in the iPhone simulator with a 54 KB lecture note, the slide began 35 ms after the touch, showing the new note and its title from its first frame.
- On a phone a note's properties took its whole first screen: a 124-point name column left values about 210 points, so every tag took a line of its own, and Live Preview narrowed them again for its Edit Source column. Names are a step smaller and their column narrower on a phone, tags are slimmer, and the properties' Edit Source button sits in their heading row; in the iPhone simulator two tags now share a line in both views.
- The text moved about 15 points sideways when a note switched between reading and writing: the reading view's column was 760 points with 28 beside it, Live Preview's text 730 with 33. The reading view now uses Live Preview's numbers; the same heading starts at the same place in both.
- Switching between reading and writing showed each view where it had last been itself, so a note read halfway down opened for writing at its top. Each view now starts where the other was: seen in the iPad simulator in both directions on a lecture note, to within the part of a callout scrolled away; `testEveryBlockKnowsWhereItStartsInTheBody`, `testTheReadingViewKnowsWhichCharacterOfTheNoteIsAtItsTop` and `testSwitchingBetweenReadingAndWritingKeepsThePlace` cover where blocks start, the character at the top and the hand-over.
- PDF ink was drawn at the page's size and magnified by the zoom: soft at every zoom above 1, which includes a page fitted to an iPad's width in portrait, with PencilKit's tile edges showing when zoomed in. Page canvases now draw at the zoom they are seen at, up to three times the page's size, and a stroke stays as wide on the page as at any other zoom (`Architecture.md`, PDF Pencil). Checked in the simulator by drawing, zooming and comparing pixels, and by `PencilWorkspaceTests.testPageInkIsDrawnAtTheZoomItIsSeenAtAndStaysInPagePoints`; `InkSelectionTests` and `PicturesAndPaperTests` were adjusted to the canvas's zoom and the guides' new place.

Not established: how the sharper PDF ink, and writing while zoomed, look and feel with a real Apple Pencil on the iPad, and the memory a page full of ink takes at a rendering scale of 3 (the simulator does not report PencilKit's tiles); the toggle and the toolbars on iOS 18; large Dynamic Type sizes and VoiceOver for the changed views; landscape and Split View widths of the iPad. Read mode draws PDF ink 16 to 29% heavier than Write mode (measured in the simulator; older than this change). Hold-to-shape (0.45 seconds at rest after 24 points) is unchanged and still has to be judged with a Pencil.

## SOLID compliance review: 2026-09-30

The [SOLID and clean-code standard](solid/README.md) assesses the implementation against `RULES.md` from a line-by-line audit of `b13dec3` (2026-10-18): Core value types, file formats and storage contracts are sound; the coordinating layer (`WorkspaceModel`, no composition root, rules implemented more than once) is not. Its [rule status](solid/rule-status.md) and [finding status](solid/status.md) are the current assessment; architecture intent alone does not establish compliance.

This was a documentation and source-review update. Existing test assertions were inspected, but no tests, builds, simulator interactions, or device checks ran for it. It adds no implemented behavior or fresh passing-test claim. Earlier verification entries below retain their original scope and dates.

## Bug hunt: 2026-09-30

This audit added reproductions before fixing confirmed failures. The fixes cover:

- Markdown edits lost during asynchronous Reload or Save a Copy, queued insertions omitted by saving before the editor updates, stale formatting commands consuming queued text, and command selections moving past their closing markers. Copies preserve their earlier snapshot while newer work remains open. Native queued-save Undo is exercised on macOS and in the iPad app (`MarkdownSessionConcurrencyTests`, `UiEditorMacEditorTests`, `EditingContinuityTests`).
- An older recorder's encoding error abandoning a newer recording, and Stop or an encoding error racing a delayed Resume. Synthetic delayed recorder tests verify a single resume, deferred stop, retained recovery audio, and recovery actions remaining unavailable during the ownership transfer (`AppleRecordingAccessRecordingTests`). Microphone permission cancellation now checks its start attempt after awaiting permission; the actual permission prompt and audio hardware remain device validation.
- Unreadable global or view-local Bases filters broadening the results. These now yield no rows, no vault-record loading, and an actionable explanation. Missing named views report which fallback is shown (`BaseQueryTests`, `UiBasesDocumentModelTests`).
- Bases YAML writes stripping custom tags or expanding anchors and aliases into detached copies. Rewritten entries now keep their tags, anchors, aliases and flow style (see "Bases: column widths, map tiles, html(), Kanban"), and an output that still cannot preserve them, such as a tag whose name cannot be written back, is refused (`BaseDefinitionEditorTests`, `CoreBasesEdgeCaseFixTests`, `CoreBasesDefinitionQueryFixTests`, `UiBasesDocumentModelTests`).
- Task search losing indented and lazy continuation text, reading task-shaped source in code/frontmatter, an empty task consuming the next completed task, and a quoted thematic break hiding its tasks as if they were frontmatter. Null-property searches no longer treat empty lists as null (`CoreSearchQueryFixTests`, `SearchSyntaxTests`). Compatibility reference: [Obsidian Search](https://obsidian.md/help/plugins/search).
- Template offsets using a different calendar time zone from formatting, producing a one-hour shift across daylight-saving changes. Extreme offsets also crashed through integer overflow. Offsets now use the requested zone, checked arithmetic, and a 10,000-year bound; an unrepresentable placeholder is left as written (`TemplateTests`, `MomentDateFormatTests`).
- Updating a property-type assignment overwriting an unreadable non-object `types` entry in `.obsidian/types.json`. Such updates now fail with the original bytes intact (`CoreFilesSettingsFixTests`). Bases `inFolder` now respects path case and boundaries, and `duration("")` returns null while invalid nonempty durations still report an error (`BaseEvaluatorTests`).
- The iPhone app run caught the new ink-selection resize frame exceeding the requested width. Resizing now corrects against rebuilt stroke render bounds before committing one history step. Hosted ink-selection tests pass on the iPhone simulator, including resizing, saved ink, and Undo (`InkSelectionTests`). The resize regression asserts the requested frame width and stroke breadth; an arbitrary fixed upper limit on the scale factor was removed because the canvas-to-screen scale changes that factor.

Final validation: the complete macOS suite executed 1,236 tests, with zero failures and one optional measurement skipped (`UiBasesReloadMeasurementTests`). The iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator app suite passed 105 tests, including queued-save Undo and the resize correction. That iPad run preceded the last two task-search corrections and the resize test's scale-limit correction. The final iPhone 17 simulator suite passed 106 tests after those changes. Both simulators use iOS 27.0. The final two search reproductions also passed in the 26-test search run and the full macOS run. A fresh iPhone simulator initially failed to launch the test host before boot and data migration completed; the successful final run used that simulator after explicitly waiting for readiness. Its temporary device was removed afterwards. Test logs and result bundles for this audit are under `/tmp/graphite-bug-hunt-*`.

This audit does not establish that the application has no bugs. Physical Pencil and microphone behavior, real file-provider conflicts, and the outstanding behaviors below remain unverified or incomplete.

## Second bug-hunt pass: recovery and deep links

This follow-up reproduced and fixed five additional defects:

- Different recovery snapshots taken within one millisecond overwrote the earlier version. Snapshot publication now checks absence under file coordination and uses sequence suffixes, retaining the original date and newest-first order.
- Renaming a note into a path with existing recovery history discarded source snapshots with matching filenames. History merges now publish all source versions before removing the source folder, preserving both histories and the order of same-date source versions.
- Overlapping background snapshot calls bypassed the minimum interval. Eight concurrent calls reproduced eight copies despite a five-minute interval. Copies of the recovery store now share a mutation lock covering the interval check and mutation; the same test produces one copy. Forced concurrent copies with interval zero retain all eight versions (`FileRecoveryTests`).
- Deep-link appends inserted an extra blank line after CRLF and introduced LF into notes using CRLF or CR. Appends now inspect Unicode scalars and preserve the existing separator (`VaultURITests`).
- Creating a note through a deep link when its name was already taken saved the uniquely named file but left it out of the sidebar. This branch now refreshes the directory after publication (`UiWorkspaceModelFixTests`).

Verification: 60 targeted macOS tests passed across recovery, deep links, workspace operations and index scan batches. The final complete macOS run executed 1,396 tests with zero failures and one optional measurement skipped. The generic iOS Simulator app build also succeeded for arm64 and x86_64. These new regressions were exercised on macOS; this pass did not run hosted iPad/iPhone interaction tests. The initial full run recorded one canvas timing failure during a simultaneous iOS build (11.498 ms per frame against an 8 ms limit); the complete rerun passed that check at 1.565 ms per frame. Other work in the shared checkout added tests between those full runs. Logs for the failing reproductions and successful targeted checks are under `/tmp/graphite-bug-hunt-recovery-*`; the complete checks are under `/tmp/graphite-bug-hunt-second-pass-*`.

## Display math: colored rank formula regression

The reported `$$ \color{#7852ee} … \operatorname{rank}(A^{T}A)=r. $$` fell back to raw source because the native typesetter rejected `\operatorname`. Rendering preparation now converts unstarred operator names to upright text and scoped color declarations to explicit color groups. Formula renderers honor explicit colors. Markdown files are not rewritten.

Verified on macOS on 2026-09-30: 29 targeted tests passed across `AppProjectTextualPatchTests`, `CorePreviewTypesetterCompatibilityTests`, `CorePreviewLaTeXTests`, and `CorePreviewDisplayMathTests`, using `swift test --build-system native` after the default build system hit an iCloud resource-signing failure. The exact reported formula was tested as both one-line and multiline display math, through preparation and Markdown parsing into a display attachment, then rendered to an image. Pixel checks confirmed purple ink across both halves; the rendered image was visually inspected. Nested color scopes and escaped braces are covered.

The Graphite app also built successfully for the generic iOS Simulator destination with signing disabled and derived data outside the repository.

Remaining limits: this is not full MathJax compatibility. Upright operator names do not reproduce all operator spacing rules; starred operators and their limit placement remain unsupported. This regression has not yet been exercised interactively in an iPad note.

## How to run the checks

The repository sits in an iCloud-synced Desktop folder. iCloud adds extended attributes that make code signing of test bundles fail, so build outside it:

```bash
swift test --scratch-path /tmp/graphite-build
```

The community plugin runtime's tests run in Node (22 or later), outside Xcode; `Docs/Community-plugins.md` lists the commands that build the real plugins and regenerate the API coverage:

```bash
cd Tests/CommunityPluginRuntimeTests && npm ci && npm test
```

iPad-only tests (PencilKit) run on a simulator through the app project. The Graphite scheme's `GraphiteIntegrationTests` target compiles and runs six files of `Tests/GraphiteAppleTests` (`InteroperabilityTests`, `PencilDrawingTests`, `PDFAnnotationTests`, `PDFPencilInkTests`, `VaultLocatorTests`, `VectorDrawingTests`) plus `AppIconBundleTests`, `PencilWorkspaceTests`, `EditingContinuityTests` and `PDFInkHistoryTests` from `App/Tests` (and, since 2026-10-10, `CommunityPluginSecretStoreTests` and `App/Tests/CommunityPluginHostTests.swift`, which runs community plugins in WebKit inside the app), so copying the package outside the project (the `Graphite-Package` scheme) is no longer needed for them:

```bash
xcodebuild -project Graphite.xcodeproj -scheme Graphite -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPad Pro 13-inch (M5)' test
```

The other files of `Tests/GraphiteAppleTests` are not in that target, so their UIKit-only tests (`testSecondEraserCutInsideAStrokeIsConvertedAgain` and `testDeferredRecordIsWrittenOnceByTheTracker` in `ApplePdfFixTests`) run nowhere yet.

Full-suite results (2026-09-25, after the bug-fix merge): `swift test` on macOS ran 1,117 tests: 1,116 passed and one was skipped (`UiBasesReloadMeasurementTests.testMeasureRepeatedReloadOfLargeBase`, which measures only when `GRAPHITE_MEASURE_BASES` is set). By target: 568 core, 127 index, 123 Apple, 299 UI. The app also builds for iOS (`xcodebuild -project Graphite.xcodeproj -scheme Graphite -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' build`). At that point, no test had run on an iPad simulator or a device after the merge: the iPad-only tests above were last run before the search and editing changes of 2026-09-23 (evening) and need a fresh run, and the iOS-only code the merge changed is verified only by that build (see "Implemented but not yet verified").

## Reading and writing controls: 2026-09-29

The first increment of `Premium-experience-plan.md` adds explicit Read/Write controls to the iPad/iPhone PDF pane, separate from palette visibility. Compact layouts place these controls in a row below the navigation bar so they do not disappear into overflow. The drawing editor adds Show/Hide Tools, an explicit remembered Draw with Finger option (Pencil-only by default), and Fit to Width. Export a Copy now sits in Drawing Options, and Insert/Done uses the dedicated confirmation placement. In compact layouts, Show Tools is in Drawing Options. Newly created PDF canvases inherit the native picker's ruler state.

Verification:

- `PencilWorkspaceTests` is included in the app's `GraphiteIntegrationTests` target. Its three hosted-view tests pass on the iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) and iPhone 17 simulators, both running iOS 27.0. They check that Read releases page touch routing and displays PDF ink, returning to Write keeps the stroke, saving retains one ink annotation, mode changes without edits leave the file byte-for-byte unchanged, changing finger policy preserves the drawing canvas, and Fit to Width returns from a zoomed view.
- The iPad run of `PencilWorkspaceTests`, `PencilDrawingTests`, `PDFAnnotationTests`, and `PDFPencilInkTests` passed all 30 tests. The final adaptive-toolbar changes were then checked with the three workspace tests on both simulators.
- The macOS package run filtered to `UiPdfViewsTests|UiRootSettingsTests` passed all 30 tests. This is targeted validation, not a rerun of the full suite recorded above.
- Hosted-view screenshots were inspected for regular iPad and compact iPhone layouts. The initial compact layout hid Read/Write and Insert in overflow; the revised layout keeps them visible. Screenshots cover the hosted editors, not the complete vault workspace or the separate system Pencil palette window.

Still unverified: real Pencil input and latency, palm rejection, double-tap/squeeze/hover behavior, ruler continuity during physical writing, palette hiding/showing with a Pencil, Dynamic Type extremes, and iPad multitasking layouts. The Read/Write choice lasts for the open PDF session; reopening an evicted document returns to Write. Undo across tab switches and page-canvas eviction was addressed later the same week (see "Undo continuity and workspace controls").

## Shared workspace controls and continuity: 2026-09-30

Markdown and PDF now share the Read/Write control, including compact placement below navigation. Focus mode hides the side panels and tab bars, restores their previous visibility when closed, and is available from the navigation toolbar and command palette. Opening a side panel exits focus mode. Reading view keeps a vertical offset in its open note session, restores it after rebuilding, and gives new heading requests priority over that checkpoint.

- The five `PencilWorkspaceTests` pass on iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) and iPhone 17 simulators, running iOS 27.0. The two added tests verify reading position across view destruction/recreation and heading-request precedence, and verify that hiding/restoring the tab bar retains the native Markdown editor, selection, and working undo history. These tests also retain the three drawing/PDF checks from the previous increment.
- `WorkspaceFocusModeTests`, `UiRootSettingsTests`, and `UiEditorAreaHandoffTests` pass on macOS (32 tests). The focus tests cover restoring all supported prior column arrangements with the inspector open or closed, repeated entry, and leaving an inactive focus state.
- Hosted Markdown and PDF layouts were reviewed in simulator screenshots. The full root window's sidebar interactions, hardware keyboard navigation in focus mode, accessibility-size extremes, and iPad multitasking need interactive validation. These checks do not establish a completed visual redesign or physical Pencil quality.

Reading position is retained only while the note session remains open, as a pixel offset rather than a semantic passage anchor. Editing the note or changing its width, folding, or late-loading embeds can change the passage at that offset. Undo across actual tab changes and PDF page-canvas eviction is covered in "Undo continuity and workspace controls" below.

## Undo continuity and workspace controls: 2026-09-30

Notes keep their undo history when their tab is hidden, moved to the other side, or switched to reading view and back: the workspace keeps the native editors of the three most recently hidden notes (`MarkdownEditorRetention`, at most 2,000,000 UTF-16 units together, none after a memory warning). Each PDF has its own undo history, owned by its session, covering Pencil ink, markup, and page changes (rotate, move, duplicate, insert paper, import, delete). It survives page canvases being released and the view being rebuilt, and never mixes two PDFs. Undo and Redo sit just before Read/Write in every workspace; when the documents area is narrower than 1,100 points they move with Read/Write to the row below the tab bar, because the toolbar's overflow menu drops a segmented control.

Fixed later on 2026-09-30: closing the other side of the split left the note's pane empty until Read and then Write were tapped. With the cursor in the note, the same steps left both sides about half as tall as the window when the split opened, and quit the app when it closed. SwiftUI makes a note's new view before it dismantles the old one, and when the split closes it also makes a second view that it dismantles at once. The text view used to move to each new view as it was made, so it ended in the view that was dismantled; moving it then also took the keyboard from it in the middle of SwiftUI's update. It now stays in the view that shows it until that view is dismantled, and moves to the newest view of the note that is left. After such a move each typed character was also recorded twice, because the editor began following the text again without having stopped; that no longer happens.

Verification:

- `EditingContinuityTests` (9 hosted-workspace tests in the app's integration target): undo and redo after switching tabs, with each note's history separate from the other's; after moving a tab to the other side (the same text view is reused); after reading view and back; text inserted while the note was hidden adopted as an undoable edit; the bound (the note hidden longest is recreated with its text and without history, the three most recent keep theirs, a memory warning releases every hidden editor while the one on screen stays); closing a tab releases its kept editor; a view of a note made and then dismantled while an earlier view stays leaves the text view, attached and with its history, in the view that stays (it failed before the fix); the split opening and closing in the hosted workspace without the cursor in the note (it passed before the fix too: there SwiftUI updates the split's content before the panes, the order that always worked) and with it (before the fix it never finished).
- `PDFInkHistoryTests` (7): ink undone and redone after the page's canvas was released by scrolling through 20 pages (the page is shown again, a new canvas restores the redone stroke, and the saved file has it); ink undo after the PDF view was destroyed and rebuilt; two PDFs side by side keep separate histories, their views return their own history, and page canvases keep PencilKit's steps out of the window's history; the stroke-level change type (a new stroke stored alone, an erased middle stroke put back in place, reordered strokes, and refusal of a drawing it did not produce).
- `PDFEditHistoryTests` (6, macOS): rotate, move, duplicate and insert undone and redone in order, and the saved file matches the document; deleted pages come back with unsaved markup and rotation, and an earlier step reaches the restored copy; undoing an insertion and then editing clears redo and saves consistently; a step whose page is gone reports it and leaves the history; large kept pages go to a private temporary file removed with the step; consecutive-run grouping.
- `MarkdownEditorRetentionTests` (7, macOS): the retention policy (count and text budget, stale tabs, takeover by a new view of the same note, one history per note, releasing every hidden editor), and the list of a note's views in use (the newest one left, without views that were released).
- `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testEachNoteHasItsOwnHistoryThatSurvivesBeingHiddenAndShownAgain` (macOS): per-note undo managers instead of the window's, and a suspended editor shown in a new container undoes its edit.
- `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testEditorEndsInTheViewThatStaysWhicheverViewIsDismantledFirst` (macOS): with a view that stays and a passing view made while the split's view still shows the editor, the editor stays put until that view is dismantled and ends in the view that stays in both orders, attached, brought up to date once, not counted as hidden, and with its history; with no view left it is kept hidden. On the Mac this is the only check of the fix: the split was not opened and closed by hand there.
- The split fix in the running app, a Debug build on the "Graphite Sidebar iPad" simulator (landscape, iOS 27.0, Scratch vault): before the change the three failures were reproduced and logged (the view that stayed was made first, a second view took the text view and was dismantled 40 ms later; the crash report shows the keyboard leaving inside `makeUIView`). After it, "Plain note" stayed on screen through opening and closing the split, without and with the keyboard up, and so did the long "2_Nanometer_MOSFETs" note with its Properties view and embedded PDF (opened with the keyboard up, closed and reopened without it); both sides kept their full height; nothing crashed; a character typed after two moves was recorded once and Undo was still offered after the split closed; a frame-by-frame recording of opening and closing showed no empty pane in between. Not checked: a physical iPad, a hardware keyboard, portrait.
- Runs for the split fix on 2026-09-30: `swift test` on macOS, 1,135 tests (568 core, 127 index, 123 Apple, 317 UI), all passed except the measurement test skipped as before (run on a copy of the tree outside the Desktop: in a `.claude` worktree every file carries the hidden flag, which the index skips, so `BaseSampleVaultTests` cannot run there); the app's `GraphiteIntegrationTests` target, all 70 tests passed on the "Graphite Sidebar iPad" simulator (iOS 27.0). With the table and Pencil work of the same day underneath (the four changes rehearsed as commits in a copy of the repository): `swift test` passed 1,178 tests (590 core, 127 index, 127 Apple, 334 UI, the measurement test skipped) and the integration target passed all 94 on the same simulator.
- Existing markup-undo tests (`UiWorkspaceAreaHandoffTests`, `UiPdfSessionFixTests`) now read the session's history. One test moved a page with `movePage`, which now records a step and clears redo, so it moves the page with a plain edit to keep checking that redo follows the page.
- Full runs on 2026-09-30: `swift test` on macOS ran 1,132 tests (568 core, 127 index, 123 Apple, 314 UI): 1,131 passed and `UiBasesReloadMeasurementTests.testMeasureRepeatedReloadOfLargeBase` was skipped as before; `WorkspaceFocusModeTests` then gained one test for the control-row width rule, run on its own (3 tests passed). The app's `GraphiteIntegrationTests` target passed all 67 tests on the iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27.0, and the 18 tests of `EditingContinuityTests`, `PDFInkHistoryTests` and `PencilWorkspaceTests` passed on the iPhone 17 simulator, iOS 27.0, whose screenshots show Read/Write, Undo and Redo in the row below navigation for notes and PDFs.
- In the running app on the "Graphite Sidebar iPad" simulator (iPad Pro 13-inch, portrait, test vault): typing in a note, opening another note in a new tab, returning and pressing Undo removed the typed line, and a second Undo left the file byte-identical to the vault's copy; a finger stroke on a PDF (Draw with Finger on) enabled both the row's and the Pencil palette's Undo, and one Undo removed it, so PencilKit's own steps did not also reach the PDF's history; Read/Write, Undo and Redo stayed visible in portrait with and without the sidebar, where before the toolbar overflow had dropped Read/Write; light and dark appearance of the note, PDF and drawing editor toolbars.

Not established: physical Pencil strokes (the simulator strokes were finger drags through PencilKit), the lasso, pixel eraser and ruler through the new history on hardware, the Pencil palette's Undo with a real Pencil double-tap or squeeze, landscape and Split View widths in the running app (the 1,100-point threshold is estimated from portrait layouts), Dynamic Type extremes, hardware-keyboard ⌘Z routing for PDFs, memory use of kept editors on a device, and deletion undo on large PDFs (the copy replays the whole file). Reading position remains a pixel offset kept only while the note session is open.

## Tables fit the note: 2026-09-30

Reported by the user from a lecture note: a table with a figure in each of three columns showed every image at its full size, so the table was about three notes wide and scrolled sideways, where Obsidian shows the three figures side by side. An image in a table cell was sized to the whole note, not to its column, and a table never adapted to the note's width.

A table now fits the note as it does in Obsidian (see "Tables fit the note" in `Architecture.md` and patch 7 in `Vendor/textual/GRAPHITE-PATCHES.md`). This also changes tables of text alone: a table wider than the note used to keep every cell on one line and scroll sideways; it now wraps its text, and scrolls only when its widest words and formulas do not fit side by side.

- `AppProjectTextualPatchTests` (14, macOS; 8 added): how columns share a width between their limits, in whole points; one word per line for measuring, keeping non-breaking spaces, formulas and text styles; only attachments that shrink make a cell follow its column; in a hosted table, three figures wider than the table each shrink to a third of it, figures that fit keep their size and the table does not stretch, and text wraps to the offered width while a table offered almost nothing keeps its widest words whole; empty cells and an empty row keep their places, and a long heading after an empty first cell stays over the second column.
- `UiReadingViewTests` (23, macOS; 2 added): through `ObsidianMarkdownText` with image files in a vault, three 500-pixel images in a table end inside a 600-point note with equal columns, and 80-pixel images keep their size.
- Full run: `swift test` on macOS ran 1,160 tests (585 core, 127 index, 123 Apple, 325 UI): 1,159 passed and `UiBasesReloadMeasurementTests.testMeasureRepeatedReloadOfLargeBase` was skipped as before. The app's `GraphiteIntegrationTests` target was not run again for this change; it has no table tests.
- In the running app on the "Graphite Sidebar iPad" simulator (iPad, landscape, iOS 27.0, notes written for this check in the Scratch vault), in Live Preview and reading view: three 564-pixel figures under formula headings and above captions fit the note side by side, with the captions wrapped, as in the user's Obsidian screenshot, where before the first figure filled the note and the table scrolled; five figures in a row; a label column beside a figure, where the figure takes the rest of the width and a 1,600-pixel image shrinks to it; two wide images in one cell, one above the other; text before and after an image in one cell; an 80-pixel image and one sized `|40` keep their sizes in centered and right-aligned columns; a table in a callout and one under a list item fit their narrower places; a missing image shows its source; a three-column table of sentences wraps to the note; a sixteen-column table keeps its words whole and scrolls sideways to its last column; a two-by-two table keeps its small size. In Live Preview the tables fit again in a split pane about half as wide and look right in dark appearance, and a long press on a figure in a table selects it with handles at its fitted size.
- The user's own lecture note (`2_Nanometer_MOSFETs.md`, 27,942 words, with its 84 attachments) was copied into a new simulator, "Graphite Tables iPad" (iPad Pro 11-inch (M5), portrait, iOS 27.0), and opened in the final build. In Live Preview and reading view the table from the report shows its three figures side by side with wrapped captions; two of its two-figure tables (one with 880 and 1,155 pixel images) sit side by side. A scratch test laid out each of the note's 13 tables through `ObsidianMarkdownText` in a 690-point note: none was wider than the note.
- Found and fixed in that note: the table headed `| | Cutoff | Linear | Saturation |` showed "Cutoff" over the row labels, each heading one column too far left, because an empty cell was not laid out (patch 8). The headings now sit over their columns.
- A recording of a note opening showed the image tables first with their alternative text and then with the figures at their fitted size, with no frame at the full size in between.
- Found and fixed during this check: an 80-row table whose cells now wrap became taller than a texture can be (about 8,000 points on the simulator), so the one `Canvas` that drew all its cell borders failed ("unable to create texture" in the log) and the table had no lines. Borders and header shading are now separate rectangle views, and the same table shows them in both views with nothing in the log. Before this change the same table was about half as tall, because its cells never wrapped, and kept its lines; a table of several hundred rows would presumably have met the same limit, which was not tested.
- Measured on macOS (debug build, 80 rows by 5 columns with 160 formulas, hosted in a 760-point note): laying the table out took about 0.43 s, against about 0.26 s before the change. The hidden copies that measure the columns account for the difference; a table of the usual size (a few dozen cells) pays a few milliseconds.

Not established: a physical iPad, Dynamic Type extremes, right-to-left text, the macOS app's windows, and VoiceOver's reading order in a table. Known limits: a column is never narrower than its widest word, and only spaces and tabs count as places to wrap, so text without spaces (Chinese, Japanese, a long URL) keeps its cell on one line and the table scrolls, as every wide table did before. As in a browser, a column of short entries beside a column of long text can be given slightly less than its widest entry, so "row 4" may wrap where "row 3" does not.

## Handwriting tools, drawing on images, and colors: 2026-09-30

The fourth increment of `Premium-experience-plan.md`. PDF pages, embedded PDFs and the drawing editor share one Pencil palette with every tool Apple Notes has; PencilKit keeps each tool's color and width and the tool in use across canvases and launches. A button at the end of the palette opens Favorite Colors, the colors of Settings › Colors, for the pen in use. A shape tool, switched from the same button or the More menu, turns a stroke into the line, ellipse or polygon it was meant to be as one undo step. The drawing editor has its own undo history. An image in a note, or an image file, can be drawn on: the result is a new PNG ("Name annotated.png") with the picture under editable ink, the original is only read, and the note's embeds switch to the new file as one undoable edit. An Apple Pencil double-tap in a note starts a drawing at the cursor or where the Pencil hovers. The default accent is an ink blue, lightened in dark appearance for contrast.

Verification:

- `ShapeRecognizerTests` (11, Core): a nearly level line made level, a diagonal kept, a round stroke made a circle, a tilted oval kept tilted and a nearly level one straightened, a box made an upright rectangle, a tilted box kept tilted with right angles, a triangle; a cursive wave, a wavy underline, half a circle, a dot and a closed scribble left as drawn; outlines follow the shape with sharp corners; a 50,000-point stroke is simplified without recursion.
- `DrawingGeometryTests` (3 added, Core): a drawing record with a picture is version 2, round-trips the picture and its frame and keeps them when its digest is rewritten, while a record without one stays version 1; a picture with no data or no usable frame is refused; the saved region is the picture, extended only where ink leaves it.
- `AccentLegibilityTests` (3, Core): the default ink blue is lightened just to 4.5:1 on the dark background and stays the same blue, a color that already reads there is left as chosen, black becomes a readable gray, and the luminance of white and black match the standard.
- `PencilToolsTests` (13 hosted tests in the app's integration target): a round stroke becomes a circle in the same ink and width while handwriting stays as drawn; the shape tool on a PDF page leaves one ink annotation and one step in the PDF's history (Undo removes the shape and the wobbly stroke does not come back as a second step), and with the tool off a stroke stays as drawn; the drawing editor uses its own history, not PencilKit's or the window's, and the shape tool; the palette has each kind of pen once (pen, fine liner, marker, pencil, crayon, fountain pen, watercolor), the eraser, lasso and ruler, saved state, and a button whose menu has the favorite colors and the Draw Shapes switch and which announces whether shapes are on; a favorite color goes to the tool in use and keeps its kind, width and opacity, the menu marks the color in use, other tools keep their colors, and the eraser takes none; a stand-in for PencilKit's lasso selection inside a canvas gets the canvas's empty input view instead of the keyboard, and the palette's first responder takes first responder back from it but not from a text field elsewhere; undoing and redoing a stroke that was moved keeps the lasso tool and puts the stroke back and forth; a picture is prepared upright at the canvas width; drawing on an image writes a PNG that any reader shows as picture plus ink and that Graphite reads back with its strokes and picture, refuses the vector formats, and keeps the picture in the background draft; drawing on a note's image leaves the original byte-identical, saves the new PNG and rewrites both a Wikilink embed with a size and a Markdown embed, and the result opens again with its stroke and picture; drawing on a standalone image saves beside it and opens the result; the editor shows the picture under a clear canvas and opens at its top; the double-tap handler starts a new drawing at the cursor and draws on the image there, and does nothing when iPadOS is set to ignore double-tap or when the preference is off.
- Full runs on 2026-09-30, after the last change: `swift test` on macOS passed 1,160 tests (585 core, 127 index, 123 Apple, 325 UI, among them another session's table tests), with `UiBasesReloadMeasurementTests.testMeasureRepeatedReloadOfLargeBase` skipped as before. The app's `GraphiteIntegrationTests` target passed all 80 tests on the iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator and on the iPhone 17 simulator, both iOS 27.0. One iPad run made while the macOS suite ran beside it failed `PencilWorkspaceTests.testReadingPositionSurvivesViewRecreationAndNewHeadingTakesPriority` on its ten-second wait; the suite alone, and the full run repeated alone, passed.
- In the running app on a new "Graphite Pencil iPad" simulator (iPad Pro 13-inch (M5), iOS 27.0, portrait, and earlier the same day in landscape on "Graphite Sidebar iPad"), with finger drags through PencilKit (Draw with Finger on) in a small vault made for the check:
  - The first palette (three preset pens, a highlighter, a pencil) showed its pens in black, blue and red over a PDF and in the drawing editor, with the selected tool and the Shapes state the same in both and after relaunching. On the user's iPad the same build showed three black pens (see below), and the palette was replaced by the system's full tool set. With that palette: pen, fine liner, marker, eraser, lasso, ruler and pencil visible, the rest reached by scrolling; the button's menu showed Draw Shapes with its checkmark and the eight default favorite colors as a row of swatches; choosing red and then yellow recolored the pen, its swatch ring and the next stroke; the color in use was marked in the menu; switching Draw Shapes off in the menu changed the button from the inverted shape symbol to the neutral colors symbol.
  - With Shapes on, a rough circle, a rough box and a rough line on a PDF page became a circle, a rectangle and a straight line; Undo in the control row removed them one step each and Redo brought one back, with the palette's Undo and Redo enabled alongside, and the shapes were still in the file after relaunching. In the drawing editor a wavy stroke drawn with Shapes on stayed as drawn.
  - The lasso on a PDF page: selecting a stroke, dragging it, Undo and Redo (the stroke moved back and forth and the selection ended), the selection's menu, and Duplicate followed by Undo. No keyboard or typing suggestions appeared, also with the tools hidden. The lasso in the drawing editor selected and moved a stroke the same way.
  - Drawing on a note's image: press and hold › Draw on Image opened the editor with the whole picture at the top; an oval drawn with the red pen and Shapes on was saved with Done; the note then embedded `Screenshot … annotated.png`, the new file was in the vault, the original's SHA-1 was unchanged, the note's Undo was enabled, and the drawing's Edit button reopened it with the picture and both strokes editable. An image file opened in a tab showed the Draw on Image toolbar button.
  - Light and dark appearance of the note and sidebar, and dark appearance of Settings (General, Pencil drawings): neutral surfaces with the ink-blue accent, lighter in dark appearance. The palette was seen in light appearance only.
- Found and fixed during this check:
  - Selecting strokes with the lasso by finger raised the software keyboard over half the page: PencilKit's selection is a text input that makes itself first responder. This was already so before this increment; the lasso had never been checked.
  - After Undo of a lasso move the page still showed the stroke in its new place (the saved file was right), and Redo likewise: PencilKit keeps showing the version of a stroke it already shows. A selection also stayed on screen over a drawing that Undo had changed.
  - The picture being drawn on was invisible (an opaque canvas paints its paper over it), and then opened with its top under the navigation bar.
  - The palette filled the Shapes button with the system's blue when on; it is now inverted in neutral colors.
  - A gently wavy stroke became a straight line; lines now have to be nearly as short as the distance between their ends.
  - The discard question of the drawing editor appeared in the middle of the title instead of at Cancel, where it now opens.
  - `AppIconBundleTests` could not pass on an iPhone (the running app's manifest leaves out the iPad's keys); it now reads the file.
- Found by the user on the iPad (iPadOS 27.0) the same day: the three preset pens were all black, the highlighter and pencil had the standard colors, and the eraser was the pixel eraser. The app's saved palette state, read from the device, held the standard picker's tools in place of the presets; the simulators used for the checks had no earlier saved state, so the presets' colors had held there. The palette now uses the standard picker, where each kind of pen appears once, and favorite colors replace preset pens. Favorite colors are not available on the iPhone, where the palette has no accessory button.

Not established: everything with a physical Apple Pencil, above all the double-tap and hover (only the handler is tested), how the shape tool feels while writing, the Favorite Colors menu with a Pencil and with the palette docked at a side or minimized, and whether a Pencil selection shows the Scribble controls; the lasso's resize handles, Cut, Copy, Delete and Insert Space Above, and its hardware-keyboard shortcuts, which no longer reach the selection because the palette's first responder keeps the focus; the pixel eraser and the ruler through the history; the toolbar's Draw on Image with the cursor on an embed and the full-screen viewer's button in the running app (their workspace paths are tested); the shape tool in a PDF embedded in a note; drawing on very large or transparent images, and on HEIC or GIF files; VoiceOver on the palette's Shapes button; the iPhone layout of the image drawing editor beyond the hosted tests; custom accent colors in dark appearance beyond the unit tests. Two sessions shared the "Graphite Sidebar iPad" simulator for part of the day, so the landscape checks there were interrupted and only the portrait ones on the separate simulator are complete.

## Images, paper, and the fixed tool bar: 2026-09-30

The fifth increment of `Premium-experience-plan.md`, a first implementation of its immediate priorities; the plan's status section lists what each priority still lacks. Pictures from Photos, Files or the clipboard can be placed on PDF pages (also in embedded PDFs) and on drawings, under the ink, and then moved, resized in proportion, deleted, and undone. On a PDF page a picture is a standard stamp annotation; on a drawing it is part of the saved PNG. Drawings have paper (plain, squared, ruled, dotted) that is either only a guide while drawing or part of the saved drawing, and a white or transparent background, per drawing and as defaults in Settings. Settings › Pencil drawings chooses between the system's floating palette and a fixed tool bar. A squeeze of Apple Pencil Pro starts a drawing in a note, and a stroke that snaps to a shape gives the Pencil Pro's tap.

Verification:

- `DrawingGeometryTests` (5 added, Core): where each paper's lines are; a saved drawing starts on a line of its paper; the saved region covers ink and placed pictures; a record with pictures or shown paper is version 3 and round-trips both, while a guide leaves it version 1 and readable by an older build; too many or too large pictures are refused, and all pictures of a drawing share one budget.
- `PDFPictureTests` (4, macOS, and on the iPad and iPhone simulators through the app's integration target): a two-colored picture is in place and upright on pages turned 0°, 90°, 180° and 270°, the same in the open document and in the written file; a saved picture is read back exactly, also as a JPEG, as a standard stamp, while another application's stamp is not taken for one; moving keeps the name and bytes, the picture moved last is over the others, removing one leaves the others, and a picture read from a file moves and is removed the same way; data that is no image, or a picture without size, is refused and leaves the page unchanged.
- `PencilToolboxTests` (5, macOS): the fixed bar's state starts with a pen and medium widths; each tool keeps its own color and width; choosing a color with the eraser in use takes up the pen; the state is kept between launches and damaged state falls back to defaults; the selection changes exactly when the tool does.
- `PicturesAndPaperTests` (7 hosted tests in the app's integration target): a picture on a PDF page is placed at 60% of the page in proportion and selected, moved and resized as one step each with undo and redo, saved so that a reader shows it and Graphite reads the same name and bytes back, and removed and restored exactly; a picture read from a saved file is found under a tap, ink drawn after it stays after it in the page's annotations, a second picture goes under the ink too and leaves the ink editable, and the upper of two overlapping pictures is taken; a drawing with shown paper and a picture is saved with the picture under the ink and the paper's lines in the PNG, reads back as version 3 with the picture placed in the saved region, keeps guide paper out of the PNG (version 1), carries shown paper as a shape in SVG and PDF, refuses pictures in vector formats, and keeps both in the background draft; pictures on a drawing are added at 60% of the canvas and selected, moved, deselected by taps beside them, deleted and undone, up to the limit of 32, while paper and background are changes without undo steps; the editor shows the paper, then the pictures, under everything PencilKit draws, through a clear canvas; the selection frame moves within its limits and resizes in proportion about the opposite corner, never below the minimum side; with the fixed bar chosen a PDF canvas draws with the bar's tool, follows it to highlighter, eraser and lasso, and follows the system's picker again when the palette is chosen (on iPhone, the palette stays in use whatever the setting).
- `PencilToolsTests.testPencilDoubleTapStartsADrawingAtTheCursorOrOnTheImageThere` also covers the squeeze: it starts a drawing when iPadOS leaves the squeeze to the app, and does nothing when it is set to another action.
- Full runs on 2026-09-30, after the last change: `swift test` on macOS passed 1,176 tests (590 core, 127 index, 127 Apple, 332 UI, among them another session's table tests), with `UiBasesReloadMeasurementTests.testMeasureRepeatedReloadOfLargeBase` skipped as before. The app's `GraphiteIntegrationTests` target passed all 91 tests on the iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator and on the iPhone 17 simulator, both iOS 27.0.
- In the running app on the "Graphite Pencil iPad" simulator (iPad Pro 13-inch (M5), iOS 27.0, portrait), with finger touches through PencilKit (Draw with Finger on):
  - Drawing editor: the Paper menu (Plain, Squared, Ruled, Dotted; "Show Paper in the Note" off for Plain); squared and dotted paper under the canvas; Add Image › Photo Library placed a photo with its selection frame and the bar with Delete and Done; dragging moved it and dragging a corner resized it; Insert put the drawing in the note showing the photo and the ink without the dotted guide, as chosen; Edit reopened it with the photo, the ink and the dotted paper.
  - PDF: More › Add Image › Photo Library, and the fixed bar's Add Image button, placed a photo at 60% of the page with its frame and bar; a stroke drawn afterwards was over the photo; after relaunching, the photos and the strokes over them were shown from the saved file, a finger tap selected a saved photo, dragging a corner resized it and dragging it moved it, Delete removed it, and Undo in the control row brought it back.
  - Fixed tool bar, chosen through the preference: shown below Read/Write in the PDF pane, above the canvas in the drawing editor, and inside an embedded PDF while annotating, with the same tool in each; pen, pencil and highlighter took the colors tapped and drew with them; the choice of tools and colors was kept over a relaunch; an embedded PDF's toolbar showed Add Image while annotating.
  - Settings › Pencil drawings showed the Paper, Pencil tools and Apple Pencil sections with their explanations (light appearance).
- Found and fixed during this work:
  - A JPEG picture on a PDF page could be placed but never selected again after saving: PDFKit wrote the picture's base64 text, which starts with a slash, as a PDF name, and it came back without its first characters. The text now has a prefix. PNG pictures were not affected, which is why the first tests passed.
  - Dragging a corner of a picture also moved it, because the frame's move gesture took the same touch.
  - On a PDF page the selection frame did not get its touches: the view sends every touch on a page to the page's canvas.
  - With the fixed bar, the first stroke after choosing a color could come out in the previous color, because the canvas got its tool with SwiftUI's next update. Canvases now take the tool when it is chosen.
  - Raising the size budget of a drawing's record for pictures let a crafted PDF use more memory than its test allows; pictures share the existing budget instead.
  - With the fixed bar, selecting a picture removed the bar and moved the page up under the finger, so the next drag missed the picture's corner and drew or moved instead. The bar now stays while a picture is arranged, and choosing a tool ends arranging.

Not established: everything with a physical Apple Pencil (squeeze, the shape tap, hover, barrel roll, Scribble in notes, the palette on squeeze); Choose File and Paste Image; arranging a picture in an embedded PDF by touch, and in the PDF pane with the floating palette after the last changes; pictures on rotated PDF pages in the running app (the rendering is tested), and on pages of imported PDFs with a crop box; pictures and paper in another application (Preview, Obsidian) beyond PDFKit and ImageIO reading them in tests; ruled paper and "Show Paper in the Note" on in the running app, and the transparent background with paper; very large photos and HEIC files; the fixed bar's eraser modes, lasso, ruler and color picker, its layout in landscape, Split View and focus mode, and with a left hand; VoiceOver on the bar and on the picture frame; the Settings page in dark appearance and its pickers in use; the iPhone layouts beyond the hosted tests.

## Less chrome, every ink, and hold to make a shape: 2026-09-30

The sixth increment of `Premium-experience-plan.md`, after the fixed tool bar was first used on an iPad in portrait: four rows stood above the page (navigation bar, tabs, Read/Write, tools), and the tool row was mostly empty and offered three of PencilKit's inks. Read/Write, Undo and Redo now sit at the end of the tab bar where the window's toolbar has no room for them; the fixed bar is one centered row whose drawing tools can each be any ink, in any width and opacity; and holding the Pencil still at the end of a stroke makes the stroke's shape.

Verification:

- `PencilToolboxTests` (11, macOS; replaces the 5 of the section above): the bar starts with a pen, a pencil and a highlighter at medium widths; each of the eight inks offers three rising widths inside the range it can draw; each tool keeps its own color, width and opacity, takes any width in its ink's range, and ignores widths, opacities and colors that are none; a tool becomes any ink at that ink's medium width, and its color follows only while it is still the old ink's default; tools are added as a copy after the one in use, up to six, and removed down to one; choosing a color while erasing takes up the ink last in use; the state survives a relaunch, and state with values another build would not offer, no tools, two tools with one identifier, an unknown ink, or no sense at all falls back to something the bar can show; canvases are told exactly when the selection changes.
