Also there: a stroke is at rest while it stays within the resting radius, and the rest moves along once it leaves; holding is on until turned off in Settings; the controls go to the tab bar from 480 points, and to a row of their own when narrower, in focus, or not yet measured.
- `PencilToolsTests.testHoldingAtTheEndOfAStrokeShowsItsShapeWhichTakesTheStrokesPlaceOnLift` (hosted): the canvas of a PDF page carries the watching recognizer, which neither cancels nor delays touches and neither prevents PencilKit's recognizer nor is prevented by it; a hold at the end of handwriting shows nothing and leaves the writing as drawn; a hold at the end of a round stroke shows its circle; drawing on withdraws it; lifting while it shows replaces the arriving stroke with the circle as one undo step; the next stroke, not held, stays as drawn; with the setting off no touch is watched. The test gives the canvas the stroke's points and the lift directly: a touch cannot be made in a unit test.
- `PicturesAndPaperTests.testEveryInkOfTheBarIsAPencilKitInkAtAWidthItDraws` (hosted): the bar's inks are eight different PencilKit inks, their width ranges lie inside PencilKit's, a tool is made with the ink, width and opacity of its preset, each ink's sample image is drawn in the preset's color, and the eraser is PencilKit's stroke eraser or its fixed-width pixel eraser at each of three sizes. `testFixedToolBarGivesCanvasesItsToolAndThePaletteTakesOverAgain` was rewritten for presets.
- `EditingContinuityTests`: another session's two tests of the note staying on screen when the split opens and closes failed in this work's first full run on the iPhone simulator. They waited for the note to narrow beside the new side, which a compact width never shows, since it shows one side at a time; the failure does not depend on this work, though the commit itself was not run again to show it. The step now expects the note's side to be hidden in a compact width; the rest of each test is unchanged and passes on both simulators.
- Full runs on 2026-09-30, after the last change: `swift test` on macOS passed 1,184 tests (590 core, 127 index, 127 Apple, 340 UI), with `UiBasesReloadMeasurementTests.testMeasureRepeatedReloadOfLargeBase` skipped as before. The app's `GraphiteIntegrationTests` target passed all 96 tests on the iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator and on the iPhone 17 simulator, both iOS 27.0.
- In the running app on the "Graphite Pencil iPad" simulator (iPad Pro 13-inch (M5), iOS 27.0, portrait), with finger touches through PencilKit (Draw with Finger on):
  - A PDF being written on with the fixed bar: navigation bar, tab bar ending in the tools button, Undo, Redo and Read/Write, and the tool row, centered. A note: navigation bar and tab bar. A note beside a PDF: each side's tab bar ends in its own document's controls, and the PDF side's tools scroll sideways.
  - Focus: one row under the navigation bar with Read/Write, the tools, the tools button, Undo and Redo; with nine colors the tools do not quite fit, so they scroll a little and fade at the ends.
  - The ink options opened on a second tap of the tool in use, with a sample of each of the eight inks; choosing Watercolor changed the tool's symbol and the next stroke was a watercolor wash; choosing Pen changed it back. The drawing editor showed the same bar under its navigation bar.
  - Hold to make a shape, with the shape tool off: on a PDF page, a rough triangle drawn with a finger and held showed the clean outline over the stroke while the finger was down and became a triangle in the pencil's ink on lifting; a circle and a square did the same before the outline's width was corrected; in the drawing editor a held circle became a circle.
- Found and fixed during this work:
  - With a note beside a PDF, touching the PDF's side left the app computing without end. The PDF view took first responder inside SwiftUI's update of it; SwiftUI, asked about focus in the middle of its update, reported cycles, and with the controls in the tab bar it never finished. The build before this work logged the same cycles when focus moved to the PDF and carried on. The first responder now changes after the update.
  - An ink preview drawn as broad as the tool's width setting was several times broader than a pencil's line. It is now as broad as the newest stroke in that ink.
  - Monoline's sample was too fine to see at its medium width; samples are drawn at least 2.5 points broad.

Not established: everything with a physical Apple Pencil, including whether a resting Pencil stays within the resting radius, whether 0.45 seconds feels right, and the Pencil Pro's tap when the shape appears; holding in an embedded PDF, at other zoom levels, and with the highlighter, crayon or watercolor (the outline is drawn as a plain line in the ink's color); the tab bar's controls and the tool row in landscape, with the sidebar open, in Slide Over, with many tabs, at large text sizes, and in dark appearance; the width and opacity sliders, adding and removing tools, the pixel eraser's sizes, the lasso, the ruler and the color picker on the new bar in the running app; VoiceOver on the bar, the ink options and the tab bar's controls; the reed pen on iOS 18, where it is left out by an availability check that no test runs on; how textured inks (crayon, watercolor) look in a saved PDF in other applications.

## The backlog build-out: from 2026-09-30

The seventh increment of `Premium-experience-plan.md`: the items that were listed as not implemented, worked through one by one. Each entry says what was verified and what was not; the full runs are at the end of the section.

### Shapes: arcs, arrows, and stretching a held shape

The shape tool and hold-to-shape also recognize arcs, straight arrows and curved arrows. After a hold, moving the Pencil on stretches the shape until the lift.

- `ShapeRecognizerTests` (17, Core; 6 added): part of a circle becomes an arc with its ends where the stroke's were and turning the way it was drawn, at 180°, 120° the other way and 60°, while a barely bent stroke stays a line; a line ending in a head becomes an arrow, level when nearly so, whichever barb is drawn first, with an outline that returns to the tip and has a barb on each side; an arc ending in a head becomes a curved arrow; a check mark, a zigzag with both legs on one side, and a head as long as its shaft are not arrows; a curve that bends both ways, a bracket-sized curve and a spiral stay as drawn; dragging an open shape keeps its start and puts its end under the hand, keeping an arc's sweep; dragging a closed shape keeps its middle and scales it, never to nothing.
- `PencilToolsTests.testHoldingAtTheEndOfAStrokeShowsItsShapeWhichTakesTheStrokesPlaceOnLift` (hosted, extended): with a tool that has not drawn yet, moving on after a hold withdraws the shape and the stroke stays as drawn; a stroke drawn with a tool teaches the canvas that tool's footprint (3.3 points for a tool set to 9.9); after that, moving on stretches the shape (a square dragged to twice its size about its middle), no stroke arrives from PencilKit, the canvas adds the shape at the lift in the tool's ink and footprint as one undo step, and a cancelled touch leaves nothing.
- In the running app, on the "Graphite Pencil iPad" simulator by finger: a square held and then dragged outward showed the larger square following the finger and became that square on lifting; the discarded stroke stayed visible inside it until the lift (three ways of clearing it were tried: switching PencilKit's recognizer back on at once, keeping it off, and changing the tool and back). On the "Graphite Build iPad" simulator (iPad Pro 13-inch (M5), iOS 27.0, portrait), a line drawn with a head and held became a clean arrow, and on the other simulator a half circle became an arc.

Not established: all of it with a physical Pencil; curved arrows, and arrows and arcs through the shape tool rather than a hold, in the running app; dragging an open shape in the running app; whether 32 points is the right smallest arc for handwriting with the shape tool on.

### A lasso that moves, resizes and recolors

Graphite's own lasso, in the fixed bar and as Select Ink in the floating palette's menu, selects strokes by a loop or a tap; the selection moves, resizes in proportion, and is cut, copied, duplicated, deleted, recolored or pasted.

- `InkLassoTests` (4, macOS): points inside a loop by the even-odd rule, a loop that closes itself, a C-shaped loop that does not take its opening; a stroke half inside is not taken, one mostly inside is; a moved and doubled frame carries its content along in proportion; the palette's switch notifies only when it changes.
- `InkSelectionTests` (4 hosted): a loop takes the strokes mostly inside it and not those it only crosses, a tap takes the stroke under it, the frame surrounds the ink and is gone when nothing is selected, the menu has Cut, Copy, Duplicate, Delete and the colors, and choosing a tool again ends selecting; moving and doubling a selection changes its stroke's points and breadth, leaves the frame where it was put, keeps other strokes, saves, and is one undo step that also ends the selection; recoloring keeps each stroke's ink and opacity; copies go after everything else, selected, and stay copies with the shape tool on; copy puts PencilKit ink and a picture on the pasteboard and paste puts it where asked; deleting and undoing; the palette's switch puts a canvas into selecting, shows as on in the menu, and choosing another tool in the palette ends it. `PicturesAndPaperTests` checks that the fixed bar's lasso makes the canvas select and not draw.
- In the running app, on the "Graphite Build iPad" simulator by finger (Draw with Finger on): a loop around an arrow and a highlighter stroke selected both and showed the menu; dragging moved them, a corner resized them, a tap on the frame showed the menu again, and Color › red recolored them. Two defects were found and fixed this way: the recolored strokes stayed black on screen while the file had them red, and a resized stroke came back from the file at its old breadth after a relaunch (both described in `Architecture.md`). After the fixes, a resized arrow kept its breadth over a relaunch.

Not established: the lasso with a physical Pencil; cut, copy, duplicate, delete and paste through the menu in the running app (tested in code only); pasting ink copied in Notes, or pasting Graphite's ink into Notes; the lasso in the drawing editor and in an embedded PDF in the running app; the frame on a page zoomed far in or out; selecting across two PDF pages (each page has its own selection, and starting one on another page ends the first); VoiceOver on the selection.

### Turning, cropping and ordering pictures

Pictures on PDF pages and on drawings can be turned a quarter turn, cropped, and brought to the front or sent to the back, from the bar shown while a picture is arranged.

- `PDFPictureTests` (8, Apple, 4 added): a picture turns clockwise and a PNG stays a PNG and a JPEG a JPEG; four turns bring it back; a crop keeps the part inside and is refused when too small or outside; a crop on screen is the right part of a picture shown turned 0, 90, 180 and 270 degrees; a replaced picture keeps its name and place among the pictures, goes to the front, the back or a given place, leaves another application's note alone, and the saved file shows the turned picture.
- `PDFPictureEditingTests` (3, UI, macOS): through the session, turning swaps the sides about the middle and is one undoable step saved to the file; a crop keeps the half with the red side, on an upright page and on a page turned 90 degrees, and a crop of the whole picture is no step; bringing to the front and sending to the back reorder the pictures and undo puts them back.
- `PicturesAndPaperTests.testPicturesOnADrawingAreTurnedCroppedAndReorderedAsSteps` (hosted): the same on a drawing, with undo and redo, the kept part in the proportions of its frame, and the crop mode ending with Cancel or Done.
- In the running app, on the "Graphite Build iPad" simulator: a photo on a PDF page, selected by a finger tap, turned a quarter turn with the ink staying over it, then cropped by dragging a corner of the crop frame (the rest of the screen dimmed) and Crop.

Not established: turning and cropping on a drawing in the running app, and Arrange in either; pictures on a page of an imported PDF with a crop box offset from its media box; very large photos; VoiceOver on the crop frame.

### Paper: spacing, line color and strength, paper colors, guides on PDF pages, notebook paper

- `DrawingGeometryTests` (2 added, Core): paper colors and the style of a shown pattern are version 4 of the drawing record and round-trip; the style of a guide is kept without raising the version; the standard style of a shown pattern stays version 3; plain paper keeps no style; spacing moves the lines and where a saved drawing starts.
- `AppleDrawingFixTests.testNotebookPaperHasItsColorsAndTheNewTemplatesDrawTheirLines` (Apple): a yellow notebook page with strong blue lines, rendered by Core Graphics' own PDF renderer, is yellow between the lines and blue on them; the standard style keeps its grays; colored dots are drawn; isometric and music paper draw their lines, the music staff five lines six points apart from the top margin; a specification written before paper colors reads back as white paper with gray lines; a spacing out of range is refused. The dotted pattern first drew no dots at all in a color other than its gray, because Core Graphics' PDF renderer draws nothing from a pattern cell without a color; the cell now carries its color.
- `PicturesAndPaperTests` (hosted, 2 added): the editor shows ivory paper with wide, strong blue lines, and its preview hides a guide, keeps a shown pattern and shows no paper as a checkerboard; writing guides show on a PDF page's canvas while writing, in light gray, as large as the page, go away when turned off, and the saved page has the ink and nothing of the guides.
- In the running app, on the "Graphite Build iPad" simulator: ruled writing guides under the ink of both pages of a PDF, over the pages' own content.

Not yet: the saved PNG, SVG and PDF drawings still draw the pattern in the standard style and the paper white, whatever the editor shows; that part waits for the writers another change is rewriting (see "Pictures in SVG and PDF drawings"). Not established: the editor's paper menu and the new notebook sheet's pickers in the running app. Drawings without paper in a dark note: see below.

### Canvas

- Package tests (Core, Index, UI; 71): `CanvasFileTests` (12) read the specification's sample byte for byte and every attribute, Obsidian-written files with unknown keys and card types, CRLF, a byte-order mark, unusual whitespace, escapes and surrogate pairs, fourteen kinds of damaged JSON, invalid UTF-8, oversize input, deep nesting, too many cards or connections, empty files and a 20,000-card board (read in 0.31 s on this Mac). `CanvasEditingTests` (16) check every kind of edit against the exact expected file text in Obsidian's, compact, two-space/CRLF and missing-list layouts, that an edit changing nothing keeps every byte, that each undo patch gives the file back byte for byte and redo the edit again, and new ids. `CanvasGeometryTests` (15) cover anchors, side choice, arrowheads, hit testing order, selection rectangles, group contents, snapping with guides, resizing with a minimum size, the viewport, zoom to fit, reveal above the keyboard and reading order. `CanvasLinkTests` (9, Index) cover backlinks from file cards, group backgrounds and text-card links, a file card not matching a same-named note beside the canvas, canvases not searched by content, and renames of a note, a folder, the canvas itself, an unrelated rename, CRLF, a byte-order mark and a canvas damaged after indexing, each checked byte for byte. `CanvasSessionTests` (19, UI) drive the gestures in view coordinates (drag, resize handle, connection dot, taps, Shift, long press, rectangle, double taps) and check saving exact bytes, undo and redo, a conflict and "Save a Copy", adopting another app's change, level of detail, a damaged file, reading order and spoken descriptions.
- Hosted `CanvasTests` (10, with screenshots): a canvas in a tab with its cards where the file puts them (checked by pixel colour), light and dark appearance, twice magnification, a card moved and snapped and saved and undone, the selection bar and handles, the add bar, a text card edited in place in a real `UITextView` and saved, zoom to fit, a link to a canvas opening it, creating a canvas, turning the plugin off, a rename reaching an open canvas, the board's recognizers installed as intended, and a 5,000-card board (opened in 0.07 s; about 25 to 30 ms of the simulator's debug build drawing a full-window snapshot).

Not established: real touches, trackpad and Pencil input (the tests call the gesture handlers, not the recognizers), the software keyboard moving the edited card, taps on links and buttons inside cards with the board's recognizers present, VoiceOver and Switch Control navigation (only the order and descriptions were checked), and frame times on an iPad. Not implemented: web pages inside link cards (the address is shown and opens the browser), drag and drop onto the board, "Convert to file", moving an existing connection's end, Obsidian's desktop keyboard modifiers, setting group backgrounds, a canvas embedded in a note (shown as a link), snap settings in `.obsidian/canvas.json` (they are kept on the device, since the file's keys could not be confirmed), editing on the Mac, and undo after the tab's canvas is released.

### Lecture video and provider conflict versions

- Video recording, verified by tests without a camera: `VideoRecordingTests` (20, GraphiteApple, run on macOS and hosted in the iPad simulator) record frames and sound from a synthetic source through the app's writer and check the files with an independent reader: H.264 and AAC in an MP4 whose closed form has no fragments; pauses leave no gap; a change of camera stays in one file; turned frames are fitted inside the first frame's size; leaving the screen closes a complete part and the video goes on in a second part that Stop joins; a paused video stays paused; resuming by hand; a file cut off while recording plays up to its last fragment and is recovered as an ordinary MP4, alone or with a second part; refused access, a missing camera, a cancelled start, too little storage to start, storage running low, a part that cannot be written, and unevenly spaced frames (which made the fragment writer fail while H.264 frame reordering was on). `RecordingRecoveryTests` (3 added) cover the parts in the recovery folder. `RecordingControlsTests` (10, UI) cover the control's choices and wording, the preview's size and position, saving a video again and recovering one in the workspace. Hosted `VideoRecordingWorkspaceTests` (3) record a video with the toolbar control and the floating preview while moving between tabs, check that touches reach the note beside the preview, and play the saved MP4 with Graphite's player; the simulator's missing camera is explained.
- Provider conflict versions, verified by tests: `ConflictVersionTests` (14, Core) for copy names and the line comparison; `FileVersionConflictTests` (13, macOS) with real `NSFileVersion` objects on a local volume, where versions can be made but never become conflicts, so the store lists every other version there; `WorkspaceConflictVersionsTests` (20, UI, 17 of them also hosted) with a test store for detection bounds, cancellation, the three decisions and the reload of open notes, and with real versions on macOS end to end. Hosted `ConflictVersionsTests` (3) show the banner, the sidebar mark, the sheet and a comparison.
- After merging with the audio recorder's later fixes (a resume that owns the recorder, a recorder being abandoned), the recording, recovery and access suites pass together.

Not established: everything that needs a camera (capture, permission prompts, rotation, mirroring, real interruptions, Stage Manager and Split View, restart after an interruption, heat and battery over a lecture, dragging the preview by finger or Pencil, writing with Pencil while recording) and everything that needs iCloud or another provider (real conflict versions, the conflict flag on iPadOS, device names, versions not yet downloaded, the provider's notices). The macOS app's camera use is untried. Whether the root view's `onChange(of: lastCompletedURL)` embeds a finished recording in the running app is untried for audio and video alike (the hosted test calls `recordingDidFinish(at:)` itself).

### Bases: column widths, map tiles, html(), Kanban

Obsidian's behaviour was checked against primary sources: Obsidian 1.13.7's own code (installed on this Mac, read only) for `columnSize`, `rowHeight`, `inFolder`, the argument checks of `date()`, `duration()` and `html()`, the HTML rendering and the date Range summary; the Maps plugin's source for `mapTiles`; the Kanban help page, the 1.14 changelogs and a plugin issue showing the native view is `type: kanban`.

- Package tests (96 new): `CoreBasesColumnSizeTests` (14) and `UiBasesColumnResizeTests` (10): widths read as Obsidian reads them, written under the property's full name with only that line changing, reset, limits, right-to-left drags, a refused save after an external change. `CoreBasesMapTilesTests` (8) and `UiBasesMapTilesTests` (5): templates filled in as MapLibre fills them, unusable URLs refused, style URLs recognised, the tile fetcher against a stubbed server (the exact URL requested, 404 and oversized responses refused, no cookies or disk cache), the tiled MapKit view's layers, markers, camera and zoom limits. `CoreBasesHTMLTests` (20) and `UiBasesHTMLValueTests` (4): `html()` values as text, formatting, links only to web and mail addresses, scripts, styles, frames, media, forms and images left out, hostile and 2,000 random fragments. `CoreBasesKanbanTests` (3) and `UiBasesKanbanTests` (9): columns with empty values in None, moving a card writes the column's value into the note (only that line), refused for formula and file groupings, non-Markdown files and edits that would change another property. `CoreBasesEdgeCaseFixTests` (23): a date Range across a daylight-saving change counts wall-clock days, anchors, aliases, custom tags and flow style kept inside rewritten entries (and a tag that cannot be written back refuses the edit), `inFolder` exact, `date()` of a number an error, `duration("")` empty. Before the fixes those tests had 39 failures.
- Hosted `BasesViewsTests` (4, screenshots): a resized column before and after its width was saved; an `html()` cell with bold red, italic and link text and no script text; a Kanban board before and after a card moved; a map drawn entirely from stubbed raster tiles with its marker over them, checked by pixels.
- Merged with another session's safety rule for YAML edits (refuse what cannot be preserved): the other session's tests that expected a refusal where the new writer now keeps the tags and anchors were changed to expect the preserved text.

Not established: dragging a column edge or a card by touch, the context menus, VoiceOver actions, and a real tile server. Not implemented: Kanban's own settings (column width, hiding empty columns, cover images, column order, a new note from a column) and moving cards between folder columns, because Obsidian 1.14's keys for them are not documented; map styles (vector tiles) and the Maps plugin's own tile sets; images and frames in `html()`; resizing columns of a base code block in a note. Differences left for a decision: Obsidian shows durations humanized ("a month") where Graphite shows exact parts; Obsidian gives an empty value for `date("not a date")` and `duration("soon")` where Graphite shows an error.

### Reading view: ticking tasks and equation numbers

A task's checkbox in reading view is a button that ticks the task in the note, as in Obsidian (`[ ]` to `[x]`, any other status to `[ ]`), in the note itself or, for a task of an embedded note, in that note. Display formulas with `\tag` show their numbers at the right of the column, in reading view and Live Preview; nothing is numbered without `\tag`, as in Obsidian, whose MathJax configuration (read from Obsidian 1.13.7's app bundle) leaves `tags` at `none`.

- `ReadingTaskTests` (16, Core), `UiReadingTaskTests` (12, macOS): every kind of task (list markers, numbers, tabs, quotes, nested and folded callouts, custom and emoji statuses, lines with links, colors, comments and footnotes, identical lines) ticked through the note's session and saved, compared byte for byte with the original in LF, CRLF, and both with a byte order mark; taps on an older drawing refused; code, tables, headings and lazy lines not tasks; embedded notes (whole, section, block) ticked in their files byte for byte, or through their open session; the edit keeps the selection.
- `NumberedEquationTests` (17, Core), `UiNumberedEquationTests` (9, macOS, rendered and read pixel by pixel): which environments number which rows; unnumbered formulas prepared exactly as before; the numbered formula's ink in the same columns as the unnumbered one; numbers at the column's right edge on their rows' baselines (the same glyph as formula and number has the same rows of ink, within a pixel); narrow columns keep every glyph, with the number below.
- `ReadingTasksAndEquationsTests` (4, hosted on the "Graphite Agent Reading" iPad simulator): the checkbox's accessibility label, value, traits and touch target; the text selection overlay leaves it out while the task's text still goes to selection; ticking keeps the reading position and is saved by autosave; one undo step in the returned editor; an embedded note's task; equation numbers at 700 and 170 points wide.

Remaining limits: several ticks in one visit to reading view undo together (the kept editor adopts the changes as one edit); real finger and Pencil taps, VoiceOver speech and pointer use were not tried on a device; where a numbered formula does not fit, Graphite breaks it and puts the number below, where MathJax lets it overflow; a numbered formula cannot be selected as text; `\eqref` and `\ref` are not supported, as Obsidian does not process them.

- `PencilWorkspaceTests.testReadingPositionSurvivesViewRecreationAndNewHeadingTakesPriority` failed in several full hosted runs (the remembered position stayed 0) and passed alone. Reading view ignored scrolls made before its first build had decided whether to restore a saved position; with nothing saved, such a scroll is now recorded. It then passed 8 times of 8 in a row; the full hosted runs at the end of this build-out are the real check.

### Live Preview: one element at a time

Live Preview shows the markup of the elements the selection touches, as Obsidian does, instead of the cursor's whole line.

| Area | Verified | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| Live Preview reveal | One element at a time: markup of the elements the selection touches, either edge counting; nested, adjacent, at line start and end; several elements and several lines; colors over several lines; heading marks by line, list, task, quote markers and block ids by line as before; rendered blocks entered and left; taps on links with hidden or shown markup; emoji and right-to-left text; restyling limited to the markers that change (far jump in a 4,000-line note: 8 UTF-16 units), equal to restyling the whole note; cursor move timed in a 1.1-million-unit note | `UiEditorRevealedMarkupTests`, `UiEditorRevealedLinesTests`, `MarkdownInlineElementTests`, `UiEditorMacEditorTests`, `LivePreviewRevealTests` (hosted) |

- Hosted `LivePreviewRevealTests` (10) on an iPad simulator type, delete, undo and redo, compose with marked text, pair brackets and Markdown, continue lists, accept a link suggestion and run a toolbar command, checking after each step that exactly the markup the selection touches shows; the caret keeps its character and a full height at every position of four lines; the closest position to each rendered character is that character, however much hidden markup lies around it. Screenshots show bold, a link, a formula, a heading, a list, italic inside bold, Arabic bold, a selection, marked text, link suggestions and a table.
- Emphasis can now hold links, code and formulas: `**[[Note]]**` was not styled as bold before. Moving the cursor restyles only the markers that change (a far jump in a 4,000-line note changes 8 UTF-16 units), and typing in a long note without blank lines restyles a line in about 1.3 ms instead of 10 ms (package test measurements on this Mac).

Not established: real finger taps, dragged selection handles, a hardware keyboard's arrow keys, a real input-method keyboard, autocorrection, Scribble and VoiceOver. Choices made without evidence from Obsidian: a block's `^id` still shows by line; a tag's `#` is never hidden; a color over several lines is one element; a selection ending at a line's start reveals an element starting there but not that line's `#` or bullet. UIKit's own selection setter costs 15 to 28 ms per call far down a long note in the simulator, and the line restyle after each keystroke sets it; to be measured on a device.

### Apple Pencil's double tap and squeeze with the fixed bar, and moving through changes

- `PencilToolboxTests` (7 added, macOS): switching to the previous tool goes back and forth between two tools, including the lasso; a color chosen while erasing is a change of tool; the eraser switch goes to the eraser and back to the tool before it, and after a relaunch while erasing to the ink last in use; a previous tool whose preset was removed takes the preset in use; a gesture heard by two bars is answered once; stepping through a real `UndoManager`'s history goes as far as it goes both ways; the scrubber takes one step per step width of a drag, and a second drag starts where the first ended.
- `PicturesAndPaperTests.testApplePencilGesturesSwitchTheBarsToolsAndOpenItsPaletteAtTheTip` (hosted): the choices of Settings › Apple Pencil map to what the bar does; with a PDF and the fixed bar, the bar's receiver switches to the eraser and back, once per gesture, and to the previous tool; the palette opens at the hovering tip as a popover, closes when a tool is chosen, opens under the bar without hovering, and a second squeeze closes it (screenshot inspected: the bar's three inks, eraser and lasso, Undo and Redo).

Not established: real double taps and squeezes of an Apple Pencil (2nd generation or Pro), hover positions reported by a real Pencil, and whether the system delivers a gesture to every bar's interaction or to one (either way it is answered once). The scrubber's drag was not driven by touch.

### The tools in the tab bar

- `PencilToolboxTests.testTheToolsGoInTheTabBarWhereTheyGetAtLeastTheirNarrowestRow` (macOS): the widths at which a side's tab bar holds the tools, for 13-inch and 11-inch iPads in portrait, landscape beside the sidebar, half of a landscape split, beside the sidebar in portrait, half of a portrait split, focus, and an unmeasured side; the limit itself.
- `PicturesAndPaperTests.testTheToolsGoInTheTabBarWhereItHasRoomAndInARowOfTheirOwnWhereItHasNot` (hosted, 11-inch iPad simulator in portrait): with a notebook open in the workspace and the fixed bar chosen, the tools are one row inside the tab bar and nothing but its divider lies between them and the page; opening a split gives them their own row again (screenshots inspected).

Not established: how the scrolling tool row feels by finger and Pencil in the narrower tab bars.

### Straightening and smoothing handwriting

- `HandwritingTidyingTests` (3, Core): the slant of a line of zigzag "letters" at 10 and −18 degrees is found within about half a degree; level lines, lines at 45 and 90 degrees, a block of ink as tall as it is wide, two points, one point repeated and points that are not numbers have none; smoothing flattens a tremor to under half a point, keeps both ends, leaves a straight stroke point for point, and leaves a stroke too short to smooth as it is.
- `InkSelectionTests.testStraighteningALineOfWritingAndSmoothingItsStrokesAreUndoableSteps` (hosted): on a PDF page, six letters written 12 degrees downhill are found at that slant, offered Straighten, turned level about their middle (after which Straighten is no longer offered), then smoothed with their ends kept and their zigzag flattened, on the canvas and in the page's ink annotations; each is one undo step. Smoothing first went unrecorded and unsaved, because strokes are told apart by their creation time, point count and ends, which smoothing keeps; a smoothed stroke now takes a new creation time. The lasso's menu without a line of writing offers Smooth and not Straighten.

Not established: how either feels on real handwriting written with a Pencil (the tests use drawn zigzags); pencil-shaded inks keep their tilt (azimuth) as written when turned.

### Drawings without paper in dark notes

- `PicturesAndPaperTests.testADrawingWithoutPaperShowsOnWhiteInADarkNote` (hosted): in dark appearance, a transparent drawing with black ink shows white where it has no ink and keeps its ink black; another image with transparency shows the note's background there.

Not established: how the white drawings look among other embeds in a long dark note on a device.

### Pictures in SVG and PDF drawings

Drawings with pictures, and drawings made on an image, are saved as PDF and SVG as well as PNG; the record (`DrawingPayload`) did not change.

- `VectorPictureTests` (20 package tests): SVG and PDF drawings with a JPEG photo, a PNG with a transparent half, ruled paper and ink show paper, pictures and ink in that order at nine sampled points in Foundation's XML parser (order, attributes, data URIs equal to the stored bytes), Apple's SVG renderer, WebKit, Core Graphics, PDFKit and Graphite's preview; the record reads back with the same picture bytes and frames; a drawing made on an image alone, 33 pictures, and the whole 16 MB budget round-trip in both formats; a single SVG picture over 7 MiB is refused with a message; removing or moving a picture in the SVG text, other pixels in a PDF image object (JPEG and PNG), a byte patched inside the JPEG, a moved picture, and a patch to either of two same-length JPEGs each discard the record while the file still opens; a PDFKit rewrite stays editable; image streams found in the file decode as Core Graphics decodes them and stop at their budget; the SVG reader refuses paths, `file:` and web addresses, other data types, bad or oversized base64, too many pictures, pictures over the budget, another `preserveAspectRatio`, an unparseable attribute and a bad frame, without crashing; previews decode pictures no larger than shown; a drawing without pictures keeps its SVG layout; a stream length running into a later stream is not a candidate.
- `VectorDrawingPicturesTests` (5 hosted tests in the app's integration target): the same drawing with Pencil ink, paper and two pictures is the same at nine points in its PNG, PDF, Graphite's SVG preview and WebKit showing the SVG in an `<img>`, with the same record and region; Export a Copy in every format keeps the picture; drawing on a note's image with SVG chosen and on a standalone image with PDF chosen saves "… annotated.svg/.pdf", rewrites the embed or opens the file, is shown and editable, and reopens with the same picture; an existing SVG drawing takes a picture and stays the same file.
- In `PicturesAndPaperTests` and `PencilToolsTests`, "refuses pictures in vector formats" / "refuses the vector formats" become "SVG and PDF hold the same pictures, paper and record version as the PNG".
- Not verified: Obsidian's rendering of SVG and PDF drawings with pictures (desktop and iPad), Chrome, Firefox, Safari and Preview as applications, Quick Look on iPadOS, Inkscape and librsvg. Apple's CoreSVG and Quick Look thumbnails on macOS show nothing for an SVG whose attribute text totals more than about 10 MB (pictures and ink alike). The too-large-PNG alert's "Save as PDF/SVG" for a drawing on an image and the editor's Add Image menu for existing vector drawings were not exercised.
- Decision taken: a drawing made on an image follows the default drawing format, as other drawings do (it was always PNG). Someone with PDF as default gets "Diagram annotated.pdf", which Obsidian embeds as a PDF viewer.

### Opening a PDF from a note while it is open in another tab (2026-10-12)

An embedded PDF's Open in PDF View did nothing when the PDF was open in another tab of the same side, whether or not the embed was being annotated, and a page link in a note that embeds a PDF did nothing in the same case. The PDF's tab was shown, and then the note's text view, as SwiftUI took it off screen, received focus from the embedded PDF inside it (PDFKit makes its document view first responder); having been touched a moment before, it accepted, began editing, and made the note's tab active again. The text view now refuses focus while its view is dismantled. Open in PDF View in Live Preview also takes focus to the PDF now, as it already did in reading view; with the PDF on the other side, the PDF's side is focused. Page links still leave the note focused.

- `PDFLinkFollowingTests` (5 hosted tests in the app's integration target, new): Live Preview's Open in PDF View with the PDF in another tab of the same side, after a touch on the note and with the embedded PDF first responder, shows the PDF's tab at the embed's page and it stays in front; the same from reading view; with the PDF on the other side, the PDF's side takes focus at the embed's page and the note stays on its side; a page link followed as the note's tap follows it (after a touch on a note that embeds the PDF) shows the PDF's tab of the same side at the link's page; two page links in a row with the PDF on the other side move it to each page while the note keeps focus. Without the two changes the first, third and fourth fail; the other two passed before too. Reading view's links are followed through the workspace in the test, not by an accessibility activation (SwiftUI exposes a paragraph's links as one element).
- Checked by hand in the iPad simulator (11-inch, iOS 27) on a note with two page links and `![[Twelve pages with outline.pdf#page=3]]`, the PDF open in the tab beside it: before the fix, Open in PDF View and the Page 5 link left the note in front (the PDF tab was shown and the note's tab activated again 140 ms later, from `textViewDidBeginEditing` under the note's dismantling); after it, Open in PDF View showed the PDF at page 3, also while annotating the embed; the Page 5 link showed page 5; in reading view the Page 9 link and Open in PDF View worked; split with the PDF on the right, the Page 5 link moved it to page 5 with the note focused and Open in PDF View focused the PDF at page 3; with the PDF open nowhere, Open in PDF View opened it in the note's tab at page 3.
- Full runs with both changes: the app's `GraphiteIntegrationTests` target passed 206 of 206 on the iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator, iOS 27.0. `swift test` on macOS ran 1,662 tests (816 core, 137 index, 201 Apple, 508 UI): all passed except `UiEditorMacEditorTests.testHeadingJumpWaitsForAWindowThenSelectsAndScrollsToTheHeading`, which fails on `main` too, with the measurement test skipped as before. The iPhone simulator was not run.

Not established: on the iPad itself, with Apple Pencil, and on the Mac. In the simulator, a tap on the embed's toolbar within about a second of switching back to the note's tab twice did nothing (Annotate, and reading view's Open in PDF View), while the same tap a few seconds later worked; the cause was not found.

## The test vault

`Tests/test_vault/` (not committed; see `.gitignore`) is a copy of a real course vault plus edge cases for testing by hand. Every edge-case note starts with a "What to check" callout saying what correct behavior looks like, compared with Obsidian.

- `Restaurants/`: a real Obsidian base with cards over remote cover images, a map with Lucide pin icons, and views embedded by name in `Restaurants.md`. The Google-hosted cover URLs answer 403 outside a browser session, so they show as broken images in Graphite (and in any app that is not signed in).
- `Edge cases/Markdown/`: headings and text (RTL, CJK, emoji, long words), lists and tasks (custom statuses, deep nesting), links (aliases, headings, block references, ambiguous and unresolved, Unicode names), embeds (notes, sections, blocks, loops, images with sizes, media, PDFs, bases), every callout type, math (environments, matrices, invalid LaTeX, dollar amounts), tables, 31 property edge cases (invalid YAML, CRLF, byte order mark, duplicate keys), Colors, code, footnotes, comments, HTML, a mixed lecture note, and a 3,000-line note.
- `Edge cases/Bases/`: 93 notes (books, people, courses, assignments, places in six cities, odd records) and 18 bases, including broken ones. `00 Bases overview.md` gives the expected counts and summary values per base.
- `Edge cases/Media/`: images (EXIF rotation, transparency, GIF, HEIC, WebP, CMYK, 16-bit, 8000×6000, fake PNG), audio and video (including corrupt, portrait and HEVC), PDFs (outline, annotations from another app, rotated and mixed page sizes, password `graphite`, corrupt, empty, page labels, scans, 100 pages). `Media gallery.md` embeds them all.
- `Edge cases/Files/`: encodings (CRLF, CR, BOM, UTF-16, Latin-1, binary), 0-byte and 2 MB notes, odd names, hidden files, non-note files, a folder named like a note, twelve levels of folders, 250 linked notes, and symbolic links inside and outside the vault.

## Read Aloud as a core plugin, off by default: 2026-10-19

Owner decision of 2026-10-19; the design is in `Read-aloud-core-plugin.md`, and what it does is in `Architecture.md` (Read Aloud as a core plugin).

**Covered by package tests.** These are run on macOS with `swift test --filter` (85 tests, 0 failures, including the classes around them). Every Read Aloud reading in them goes through a silent fake voice.
- `CorePluginSwitchesTests` (9): a fresh device has Read Aloud off and every other plugin on, and the switches are stored and read back.
- `CorePluginSwitchMigrationTests` (17):
  - the pure decision;
  - preference, voice-folder and no-evidence devices;
  - Read Aloud turned off earlier;
  - the migration runs once;
  - a workspace of an existing user and of a new one.
- `WorkspaceReadAloudCorePluginTests` (9):
  - no controller is built while Read Aloud is off;
  - turning it on builds one, without a relaunch;
  - turning it off during playback stops speech, releases audio, closes the reader and drops the controller;
  - remembered places are kept;
  - the Settings plugin pages follow the switch;
  - the `graphite://read` link refuses while Read Aloud is off.
- `WorkspaceDeepLinksTests` and `GraphitePreferencesPersistedKeysTests`, which now include the `GraphiteEnabledCorePlugins` key.

**Not run yet.** The full package suite and the hosted iPad suite were held back on 2026-10-19 while Read Aloud tests that speak aloud are being made silent. The four hosted Read Aloud test files turn the plugin on first.

**Not verified.** Whether each menu, button and the floating player actually disappear and come back in the running app on iPad and Mac has been checked only through the model the views read, not by eye.

## Implemented and covered by automated tests

| Area | Behavior | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Vault paths | Relative paths, `..` escapes and symbolic links outside the vault rejected; always in Unicode's composed form, whether given decomposed (as file URLs give them) or decoded from a stored layout, so one file is one index row whichever form refreshed it; backslashes in existing names, a combining mark after `/`, and chained symbolic links through a dangling target | `VaultTests`, `IncrementalIndexTests`, `CoreFilesSettingsFixTests` |
| Vault list | Picking a listed folder again updates its entry; a moved vault is followed by its identifier; alphabetical order and the most recent restored at launch; the last open document stored; removal; names for new vaults; locations worded as in Files (On My iPad › Graphite for Graphite's own folder, iCloud Drive › Obsidian; file-provider storage as Files › folders, since On My iPad and third-party providers share one path layout) | `VaultListTests` |
| Vault folders | A new vault is an empty folder, found again through the folder it was created in; an existing item is never reused; a picked folder is followed when renamed and reported when deleted; a folder inside Graphite's own folder is remembered by its path there (iPad simulator); Graphite's own Documents folder, or a vault inside it, picked as a vault is remembered by its path; a vault folder renamed or deleted by another app is reported, a moved one is opened again at its new place, and a deleted one asks to be opened again | `VaultLocatorTests`, `AppleRecordingAccessFolderTests`, `UiWorkspaceAreaHandoffTests` |
| Safe writes | Revision-checked atomic replace; same-size external edits and deletions are conflicts; exact bytes preserved; staged outside the vault folder, leaving nothing behind; the system trash asks other presenters to let go first | `VaultTests`, `CoreFilesSettingsFixTests` |
| File management | Renaming a note rewrites every link to it in its own style (bare names, headings with aliases, embeds, Markdown links, vault paths, links in properties, links in tables with `\|`), leaving code alone; moving to another folder recomputes relative links, including the moved note's own; renaming a folder follows paths into it; a name that becomes ambiguous gets a path; a note changed after planning is left alone and reported; moves never replace another item or put a folder inside itself; copies are named "Name 1"; `.trash` and permanent deletion; names Obsidian refuses are refused; Markdown link destinations read with CommonMark's rules (balanced parentheses, titles, code spans and images in labels), a link inside another link rewritten as one edit, reference-style links updated through their definition, a quoted property link escaped for its quotes, letters beyond ASCII kept in the form they were written, bare names and aliases that still find their target kept, Markdown links in shortest and absolute format keeping that format; linking notes that cannot be read, are too large or not UTF-8, or whose links cannot be located are left untouched and listed with their reason; a leading byte order mark kept; a folder's files listed from the disk as well as the index; links spelled with other capitals rewritten on case-insensitive volumes; a move without link updates still reports every moved file; nothing moves until the index has read the vault | `FileOperationTests`, `CoreLinksRewriterTests`, `CoreLinksFileOperationTests`, `IndexFileOperationsFixTests`, `UiWorkspaceAreaHandoffTests` |
| Pasted web pages | HTML converted to Markdown: headings, emphasis, links, images (not inline data), nested lists, quotes, code blocks, inline code, tables, line breaks; scripts, styles and comments dropped; entities decoded; broken markup and a bare `<` tolerated; paragraphs and headings inside list items, blocks inside table cells, escaped pipes in cells and link destinations, code with backticks, markup inside `pre` kept as code, quote and list prefixes on code, tables and rules, the Google Docs wrapper, empty and nested emphasis, script and style text skipped, an unclosed `head`, `ol start` and `li value`, text after a nested list, script links in any case or spelling (including entity and backslash tricks), escaped alt text and destinations, Latin-1 and Greek entities, no-break spaces kept between words, a byte order mark, the first line break in `pre`, linear time on unclosed tags, bounded nesting | `HTMLToMarkdownTests`, `CoreHtmlColorsHTMLToMarkdownTests` |
| Block links and embeds | `![[Note#^id]]` shows only that block; a heading subpath shows its section; `^id` markers hidden when reading and concealed in Live Preview | `BlockEmbedTests`, `NotePreviewDocumentTests` |
| Link and tag suggestions | What is being typed at the cursor: `[[` with a note, `#heading` or `#^block` part, embeds, a closing `]]` already there, nothing inside code or past the alias; choosing inside an existing link replaces the rest of it and keeps an alias already written; `#tags` after a space, including digits so far, emoji tags and tags after punctuation; no suggestions in fences inside block quotes, code spans across lines, HTML comments, after escaped brackets, or on an empty last line; link text follows the vault's link format; blocks (paragraphs, list items with their continuation lines, whole lists, tables and quotes with their lazy continuation lines, and code; not setext headings, thematic breaks, code inside list items, or indented code), each with its `^id`, including Obsidian's own-line form after a blank line; a new `^id` goes after a blank line for tables, quotes and lists, in the note's line endings; tags counted per note, whatever their capitals | `LinkCompletionTests`, `TagSuggestionTests`, `CoreLinksCompletionTests`, `CoreFrontmatterBlockTests`, `IndexSearchFixTests`, `UiEditorAreaHandoffTests` |
| Footnotes | References, definitions with indented continuation lines, and inline footnotes, outside code; numbered by first reference with labels ignoring case, repeats keeping their number, unreferenced definitions last; undefined references left as written; the Footnotes view's list and locations | `FootnoteTests` |
| Folding | Headings fold to the next heading of the same or higher level, keeping blank lines before it visible; list items fold their indented lines; headings with nothing under them, in code and in frontmatter do not fold; keys survive edits elsewhere and count repeats; nested folds; which folds hide a location; Return at a folded line starts a sibling item after the section (tasks stay tasks, numbers continue) | `NoteFoldingTests` |
| Mentions | Unlinked mentions as whole words, ignoring case and accents, outside links, embeds, code, math, comments, tags, web addresses, HTML tags and frontmatter, preferring the longest of overlapping names; links that could name a note (by name or alias, embeds and web links left out); a mention linked as `[[Note]]` or `[[Note\|words]]` or a Markdown link; excerpts mark the range | `MentionTests` |
| Templates and daily notes | Moment.js formats (tokens, ordinals, bracketed text, ISO weeks, long formats, time zones) and reading dates back from names, rejecting impossible days; `{{title}}`, `{{date}}`, `{{time}}` with formats and offsets; `templates.json` and `daily-notes.json` read with Obsidian's defaults and written keeping unknown keys; daily note paths with folders from the format; a template's body goes in at the cursor, never inside frontmatter, and its properties are merged (lists gain items, existing values stay, empty ones are filled) | `MomentDateFormatTests`, `TemplateTests` |
| PDF links and quotes | Page links in Wikilink and Markdown form, labels and paths escaped; quotes join a PDF's broken lines and hyphenated words, keep hyphens before digits and capitals, and keep paragraphs; a quote inserted into a note is kept apart from the lines around it | `PDFCitationTests` |
| File recovery | A copy at most once per interval, including overlapping calls on a shared store, none for repeated or blank text, and one taken anyway after a copy dated in the future; same-millisecond and forced concurrent versions retained in ordinary Markdown files; newest first, including sequence ties; old copies pruned and notes left without any removed; at most 200 per note after snapshot publication; copies follow a renamed note or folder, retaining colliding histories; the editor reports its saved text before a save or a reload replaces it, and nothing when nothing changes | `FileRecoveryTests`, `MarkdownSessionRecoveryTests` |
| Tags and Properties views | Every level of nested tags counted once per note (tags in text and in properties together, capitals merged); tags nested into a tree, a missing level added, each level sorted by name or frequency with ties broken by name; property names counted ignoring capitals with a few values each; types from `types.json` (ignoring capitals), then the name (`tags`, `aliases`), then the first non-empty value; renaming a property changes only its name where it is written (quoted names, CRLF, comments and lists kept byte for byte), matches names ignoring capitals, quotes a new name YAML would misread, keeps the cursor on the same text, and refuses invalid YAML or a name the note already has; `types.json` assignments set and moved keeping other keys, left untouched when nothing changes, never overwritten when it is not a JSON object | `TagsAndPropertiesTests`, `VaultTagsAndPropertiesTests` |
| Bookmarks | Obsidian's `bookmarks.json` read with every kind (files with a heading or block, folders, searches, groups, graphs, web pages) and written keeping unknown kinds, keys and whole-number `ctime`s; titles as Obsidian shows them; add, rename (an empty name restores the default) and remove, inside groups too; renamed files and folders followed, headings kept; files not in Obsidian's format refused and never overwritten; changes applied to the file as it is now, keeping one made elsewhere; no file created for nothing, an unchanged list leaves it as it was | `BookmarkTests` |
| Index location | Named after the vault's identifier, so it survives the app's folder moving; an index named after the old path is moved with its journal; indexes unused for 30 days deleted; a removed vault's index deleted; a vault's identifier known before it is recorded; hidden files such as `.obsidian` settings never indexed, even when a change to one is reported | `IncrementalIndexTests`, `VaultListTests`, `VaultTests` |

| Deep links | `graphite://` and `obsidian://` read with Obsidian's actions and parameters, ignoring capitals, flags without a value on, `overwrite` before `append`, `obsidian:open` and `vault/Name/file` forms; other links refused; links written as Obsidian writes them (spaces as `%20`, notes without `.md`) read back; appended text on a line of its own preserving CRLF, CR and LF; unique note creation refreshes the sidebar after a name collision | `VaultURITests`, `UiWorkspaceModelFixTests` |

Saves are revision-checked in the code, but no test changes the file between reading and saving | `BaseDefinitionEditorTests`, `BasePropertyEditingTests`, `CoreAreaHandoffTests`, `CoreFrontmatterBaseEditingTests`, `CoreBasesDefinitionQueryFixTests`, `UiBasesDocumentModelTests`, `UiWorkspaceAreaHandoffTests` |

| Index | Full-text, tag, alias search; file-name matches listed first without duplicates, `%` and `_` literal; ambiguity; backlinks including relative Wikilinks, partial, vault-rooted and alias links, frontmatter links, Markdown links in Obsidian's shortest, absolute and relative formats (beside the note, then from the vault root, then by name; aliases name only Wikilinks) and case differences, including letters beyond ASCII, with the exact spelling preferred; lookups and the prune use indexes rather than table scans; existing rows migrated to folded keys; rename plans for partial-path links; links to dotted names, names stored in decomposed Unicode, and partial paths; notes saved during a scan are neither pruned nor overwritten; backslash names; unindexable notes not read again; byte-limited batches; a failed write costs only its file; per-file refresh, skipping hidden and symbolically linked paths, removing a deleted folder's files, and continuing past unreadable ones; scans across chunks and batches read only changed files; an unreadable note in a coordinated batch costs only itself; a cancelled link query does not run; pruning continues past unreadable notes; stored paths stay correct when the file enumerator reports the vault under another path prefix (every scan of a temporary folder meets this on macOS, which enumerates `/var/…` as `/private/var/…`; no test checks it by name) | `IndexTests`, `IncrementalIndexTests`, `BaseIndexTests`, `CoreAreaIndexHandoffTests`, `IndexVaultIndexLinkTests`, `IndexVaultIndexRenameTests`, `IndexVaultIndexScanTests`, `IndexVaultIndexScanBatchTests` |

| Drawing export | Crop to ink at full canvas width; 2× PNG; oversized canvases rejected before allocation with the original file kept | `DrawingGeometryTests`, `PencilDrawingTests` |
| PDF notebooks | Paper templates valid; page insert, duplicate, delete, move, rotate, export survive reopening; standard ink persists; erased gaps stay separate in `/InkList`; dotted paper shares one dot cell; inserted paper matches the page size, including scan-sized pages | `InteroperabilityTests`, `AppleDrawingFixTests`, `UiWorkspaceAreaHandoffTests` |
| PDF annotation | Ink saved as standard `/Ink` whose appearance stream is the variable-width outline (checked with CoreGraphics); per-stroke incremental updates and merged edits; ink changed by another app; highlight, underline and strikeout markup added, recolored and removed, including unnamed markup from other apps; bookmarks written; the saved file becomes the next baseline; conflicts; export; annotations and Graphite keys survive page changes; the warning when page changes would drop document settings; `#page=` and `#height=` embed options; outline entries and links follow moved pages and are removed with deleted or unexported pages (an entry keeps its children); another application's named markup found again after a page change and restored with its note, author and exact color; signed PDFs and a declared PDF version reported before page changes | `PDFAnnotationTests`, `ApplePdfFixTests`, `UiWorkspaceAreaHandoffTests` |
| PDF sessions and views | Later saves replay only newer edits; overlapping saves keep every edit; an external change under unsaved edits is a conflict, and Graphite's own saves are not; a failed save keeps the edits and is reported until a save succeeds, and a failed autosave is retried; autosave waits longer after slow saves and after failures; permission-restricted, encrypted and signed PDFs are shown without changes, a signed one until the user accepts invalidating the signature; baseline copies of ended processes removed; form fields on shown pages read-only and the file untouched; thumbnails drawn off the main thread from a mapped copy, cached per size, and released with their PDF; a second embed that starts annotating saves the first and reads the file again; a session whose closing save failed is kept for the next embed of its PDF; a tab's PDF reports its saves to the index | `UiPdfSessionFixTests`, `UiPdfViewsTests`, `TestsExistingAppleServiceTests`, `UiWorkspaceAreaHandoffTests` |
| Recording controller | A failed recording blocks a new one until it is saved again or discarded; saving again can target a new destination after the folder was renamed; a taken name is reported; a recording is saved even when its recovery copy cannot be deleted; Stop finishes even when the recorder never reports finishing; an abandoned recorder leaves the audio to save again | `AppleRecordingAccessRecordingTests`, `UiWorkspaceAreaHandoffTests` |
| Drawing editor drafts | New drawings take the note width limit; a draft reopens with its strokes until the editor closes; a draft removed right after writing leaves nothing; an existing drawing's draft keeps its revision for conflict checks; a draft that failed to encode is not offered | `UiRootSettingsTests` |
| PDF Pencil ink | Real PencilKit strokes, including width variation surviving a save | `PDFPencilInkTests` (iPad simulator) |

## App icon presets (2026-09-23)

- Implemented: five bundled Icon Composer documents (blue primary, red, black, charcoal, blue graphite) and an iPhone/iPad Appearance → App icon picker with previews, selected state, in-progress disabling, error reporting, and selection refreshed from `UIApplication`. This is separate from the existing custom accent-color picker. macOS uses the blue primary icon without the alternate-icon picker.
- Verified: Debug application builds for the iPad Pro 13-inch (M5), iOS 27 simulator, and macOS. `AppIconBundleTests` runs in the application host and checks primary/alternate declarations for both phone and iPad, all five settings previews, and system support for alternate icons. `assetutil` inspection confirms compiled icon images/stacks for every choice. The compiled default iPad icon was visually inspected at Home Screen size.
- Not verified: tapping the picker, actual Home Screen changes, selection persistence across relaunch, error presentation, older iOS versions, tinted/dark Home Screen appearances, and physical devices. Computer Use could not inspect Device Hub (repeated timeouts); an experimental runtime-switch test stalled and was stopped. It is not counted as a pass or retained as a hanging test. Full regression tests were not rerun for this change.
- Reproduce the packaging test with `xcodebuild -project Graphite.xcodeproj -scheme Graphite -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPad Pro 13-inch (M5)' -only-testing:GraphiteIntegrationTests/AppIconBundleTests test`.
- The target blue was RGB 48, 104, 232 (`#3068E8`). Generated foreground shading means this is a design color reference, not a claim of pixel-exact uniform RGB. Glass, ink, and pen are rendered in one foreground layer; arbitrary user-picked icon RGB is not implemented.

## Implemented and checked by hand in the iPad simulator

Checked on 2026-09-23 with a copy of a real course vault (653 files) on an iPad Pro 13-inch (M5) simulator:

- Opening a vault from On My iPad, restoring it on relaunch, folder tree, note names without `.md`.
- Settings: attachment location written to `.obsidian/app.json`; live "New files go to" preview.
- Drawing: full-screen canvas, canvas grows downward, SVG and PDF saved into `attachments/` with a shortest-path embed at the cursor, re-editing an SVG in place, drawings shown inline in the reading view.
- Saved SVG and PDF drawings rendered by macOS Quick Look (`qlmanage`), independently of Graphite.
- Write/Read toggle no longer inserts the last insertion a second time.

Checked on 2026-09-23 on the same simulator and vault copy (phase 3):

- Reading view, Live Preview and Source mode, switched from the toolbar's book button and the More menu.
- Live Preview: markup hidden except for the element under the cursor (bold, links, formulas, highlights, code; seen in hosted screenshots, in left-to-right and Arabic text); heading marks, bullets, checkboxes and quote markers shown on the cursor's line; properties panel with typed rows; tables, math blocks, callouts (as boxes, with bold, italics and math inside), images, the inline video player, PDFs and embedded bases drawn in place, each with a `</>` button that shows the source; bullets, task checkboxes (tapping one toggles `[ ]`/`[x]` in the file), quote bars, `Note > Heading` link text, and `$…$` inline math drawn over the hidden source.
- Reading view: tables with thin borders, math in table cells (including `$k_n$` and `$x_n$`), colors and nested colors, highlights, callouts, task checkboxes in place of bullets, accent-colored links, the inline video player with a poster frame.
- Embedded notes: `![[4_Logic design#Saturation Region]]` renders that heading's section (callouts, math, images) inline in Live Preview under a link to it, one level deep.
- Table-of-contents links: `[[#Heading]]` scrolls to the heading in both views, and so do GitHub-style slugs from tables of contents written outside Obsidian (`[2.1 Objectives](#21-objectives)`, repeated headings as `#notes-1`); Outline panel jumps put the heading at the top without raising the keyboard, and rendered images land in place after the jump.
- Search results are named by file; folders expand when tapped instead of opening.
- Bases: a `.base` file opens as a table (formula column, sort, display names) and as cards; tag lists stay readable in cells and cards; a `base` code block in a note renders as a live list in Live Preview.
- PDF view (checked by the PDF work on an iPad Pro 11-inch simulator, with Draw with Finger on): drawing, erasing, undo, zoom; highlight in two colors, underline, removing an underline; thumbnails with live ink; Go to Page; inserting a dotted page, adding and removing bookmarks, rotating a page and drawing on it; the delete confirmation and export dialogs; an embed starting at `#page=3` with its thumbnails and saved annotations; the conflict banner and Save a Copy when the file is replaced from outside. Saved files render correctly in `pdftoppm`, Ghostscript and `qlmanage`.
- PDF embeds in notes (checked here): the Obsidian-style toolbar (pages, zoom, "1 of 65", annotate, open) in Live Preview, and the tool picker appearing when annotating from the embed.
- Settings laid out like Obsidian's: Options (General, Editor, Files and links, Appearance, Core plugins) and plugin pages (Colors palette, Pencil drawings with the drawing format and background, Audio recorder).

Checked on 2026-09-24 on a new simulator, "Graphite Sidebar iPad", with a fresh copy of the test vault (Tags and Properties views):

Checked on 2026-09-24 on "Graphite Sidebar iPad" (find in a note, after a crash report):

- A crash report from the Mac on 2026-09-23 showed UIKit raising while the find bar's highlights laid out for a note no longer on screen. Closing the find bar is not enough: the system's find session survives it and can still raise (the other session reproduced this in 4 of 4 cases in a Mac Catalyst harness). The editor now turns its find interaction off when it leaves the window and on again when it returns. "Search current file" and "Search & replace in current file" in the command palette open the find bar without a keyboard (from reading view too). Searching "Dune" in Dune.md found 4 matches; opening a new tab with the find bar up closed it cleanly, and the app kept running; back in the note, find still worked (13 matches for "bookmark"). The original crash was on the Mac and was not reproduced before the fix, so this shows the new path works, not that the old one crashed here.
