- Found and fixed during this check: the Tags and Properties lists said "No tags in this vault yet" while the first scan was still running; a change reported for `.obsidian/bookmarks.json` put it in the index as a file, so the file count grew by one; indexes were named after the app's folder, which iOS changes on reinstall and update, so each one rebuilt from scratch and the old ones stayed behind (four 9 MB copies on this simulator); the graph row's "Opens in Obsidian" sat under its icon; a missing note's menu offered to open it.

Checked on 2026-09-24 on "Graphite Sidebar iPad" (graph view, the test vault):

- The right sidebar's graph panel ("Graph view: Open local graph") showed Dune with Frank Herbert and Children of Dune, names beside each; depth 2 added "Untitled book without properties"; tapping Children of Dune opened it and the graph moved its highlight there.
- "Graph view: Open graph view" drew the whole vault, 476 items and 1'690 links, with the benchmark cluster, the fan-shaped notes, and unlinked notes in an outer ring; the open note is blue. Filtering by "Library" left 16 books with their names; tapping Frankenstein closed the graph and opened it. Pinching zoomed and showed names; Show All fitted the graph again, kept from the last time; dragging a hub moved it with its notes; turning Tags on added them in green (699 items).
- On this Mac, a synthetic vault of 10,000 notes builds its graph in 0.29 s and steps the layout in 8 ms; 100,000 notes take 3.3 s and 120 ms a step, so a graph that size takes about half a minute to settle.
- Found and fixed during this check: building the graph ran several queries per link and took 27 s for 10,000 notes (now resolved in memory, checked against the index); the view fitted the graph again while a node was dragged, moving it under the finger; names at the edges were cut off; a filtered graph kept the zoom of the previous one; the settings panel cut off the Forces; "1 links".

Checked on 2026-09-24 on "Graphite Sidebar iPad" (deep links, with `xcrun simctl openurl`):

- With Graphite closed, `graphite://open?vault=Scratch&file=Bookmarks%20guide%23Second%20section` asked "Open in Graphite?", then started it and showed the note at Second section.
- `new` with `file=Inbox/From a link` created and opened the note; the same with `append&silent` added a line through the open editor; without `append` it made "From a link 2.md", not opened because of `silent`.
- A link naming a vault not in the list said so; `search` with `tag:#book/fiction` showed its 9 notes.
- Found and fixed during this check: a search asked for while the left sidebar was hidden (the link, ⇧⌘F, the palette's "Search in all files", a tag in the graph) set the query without showing it.

Checked on 2026-09-24 (recordings that survive a crash):

- Which formats a crash leaves playable was measured on this Mac by copying each file while it was still being written, five seconds in: M4A (what the recorder wrote) and AAC in CAF, nothing; PCM in CAF, 4.94 s at 317 MB an hour; IMA4 in CAF, 4.92 s at 86 MB an hour. Recordings are now IMA4 in CAF until Stop converts them to M4A.
- In the simulator, the Mac has not allowed the Simulator app to use its microphone, so no audio arrives: each audio call there waits 30 seconds and fails. This showed that starting a recording blocked the main thread (the whole app froze for 90 seconds); starting and resuming now happen off it, and the app stayed usable while the start waited, showing "Starting…" with a Cancel in its menu (which worked). A start that fails or is cancelled removes its empty file. Recording itself, and recovery after the app is killed while recording, still need an iPad or a simulator with microphone access.
- Found and fixed during this check: ⌃⌘S was both iPadOS's Show Sidebar and Graphite's Show or Hide Files, which the menu bar reported as a conflict; the recording control's row of buttons was cut to one in the toolbar, so Pause and Stop could not be reached (it is now one menu with the time and every action).

Checked on 2026-09-25 on "Graphite Sidebar iPad", after the bug-fix merge:

- Yesterday's features still work after the merge: a `graphite://` link opened "Bookmarks guide" at Second section, and the Bookmarks panel, graph and recordings code were all still in place.
- Found and fixed: after a jump down a note (a link or bookmark to a heading), opening the left sidebar left a rendered callout blank, with its space kept, and the fold chevrons beside the wrong lines, until the next scroll. The pre-merge build did the same, so it was older than the merge. When the text column's width changes (the sidebar, a split, a rotation), the editor now waits for the width to settle, lays the visible text out again and places the rendered blocks and chevrons on it. It happened in both tries before the fix and neither after it.
- iCloud Drive, which syncs the Desktop this repository is on, kept 69 older copies of source files as "Name 2.swift" when the merge replaced them, and the build failed on the duplicate declarations. They were moved out of the repository (to `~/.graphite-fix-work/icloud-conflict-copies-2026-09-25/`), and the build and tests pass again.
- Found and fixed (reported by the user): in the file list, the contents of an expanded folder were drawn centered and squeezed together instead of as rows. The merge had wrapped each row in `.equatable()`, which makes a folder's row a single view, so the list drew the folder's contents inside it. Without the wrapper (the rows still compare by their entry), files and folders three levels deep line up with their folders, and tapping one opens it and highlights its whole row.
- The iPad's own crash logs (six copied of the seven there, 23–24 Sep, all on the build installed on 23 Sep) are one crash: indexing ran out of stack parsing a note with lists nested more than about 55 deep, and because indexing runs at every launch the app crashed again on each reopen. The current code no longer hands such a note to the Markdown parser (`MarkdownNesting`, tested on a thread with the same 512 KB stack); a Release build with that change was installed on the iPad the same evening, and 45 seconds after launch it was still running with no new crash log. Whether the note that caused the crash is in the vault the iPad opens is not known, so this does not show that the crash itself is gone.

## Implemented but not yet verified

- The Pencil palette giving way to screens presented over a PDF or the drawing editor (2026-10-10): checked in the simulators (see its section at the top), not on a device or with a physical Apple Pencil.
- Recording a lecture and recovering it after Graphite is closed mid-recording, with a real microphone (see the check above).

- The camera (the simulator has none).
- Pasting a web page as Markdown in the app (the conversion is tested; the simulator's pasteboard sync delivers only promises).
- Dropping images, files, and sidebar items into a note (synthetic touches do not start drags).
- Arrow keys and Escape in the suggestion list with a hardware keyboard.
- Suggestions with Markdown links chosen instead of Wikilinks (Settings › Files and links).
- Tab and Shift-Tab, ⌘B, ⌘I, ⌘K, ⌘L, ⌥⌘↑ and ⌥⌘↓, and ⌃⌘1–6 in the editor with a hardware keyboard.
- Keyboard shortcuts with a hardware keyboard and the iPad menu bar: the File, View and Go menus (`GraphiteCommands`) with ⌘N, ⌘T, ⌘O, ⌘W, ⇧⌘T, ⌘S, ⌘,, ⇧⌘F, ⌘E, ⌘P, ⌃⌘S, ⌘[ and ⌘], ⌃Tab and ⌘1–9, and their enabled states; in the editor ⌘/, ⌥⌘F, and ⌥Return, ⌘Return and ⌥⌘Return on a link. The simulator tool types text but cannot hold ⌘, and its synthetic swipe does not open the menu bar. Arrow keys in the palettes.
- Dragging a file or folder onto a folder in the sidebar to move it: the simulator's synthetic touches open the menu instead of starting a drag, so it needs a real device or a pointer.
- Folding: arrow keys and Return at a folded line with a hardware keyboard (Return is covered by tests); "Fold All" and "Unfold All" from the More menu and the command palette; notes over 2 MB, which do not fold.
- Templates: the Templates and Daily notes plugins switched off in Core plugins; a daily note format with week numbers or times, which Graphite cannot read back from names, so previous and next skip those notes.
- Tabs: dragging a tab to the other side and dropping a sidebar file on a tab bar (tab reordering by drag was checked); ⌘T, ⌘W, ⇧⌘T, ⌃Tab, ⌃⇧Tab, ⌘1–⌘9, ⌘-tap and ⌥⌘-tap on links, and ⌘Return and ⌥⌘Return in the quick switcher, with a hardware keyboard; the split in a narrow window (Slide Over, a narrow Stage Manager window), where only the focused side shows with a button for the other; Apple Pencil drawing on a PDF focusing its side.

- Bases interactions by touch: header sorting, the view editor and property editor sheets, map markers and their popups, editing a property from a cell, the map view in general on the course vault. The Bases agent verified rendering of every view type and the file writes, but not taps.
- Turning each core plugin off and back on in a running session (the Settings pages and toggles were checked by eye, not each feature's disappearance).
- Properties editing through the panel (the file format is covered by tests).
- Dark and light appearance of drawn replacements (bullets, checkboxes, math) after switching appearance while a note is open.
- The macOS editor on a real Mac, by hand: it renders blocks and draws replacements since 2026-10-16 (`UiEditorMacRenderedBlocksTests`, `UiEditorMacEditorTests`). Heading and search jumps, link clicks (⌘ and ⌥⌘), pasted and dropped attachments (several items keep their order), and undo for text changed elsewhere are covered by `UiEditorMacEditorTests`; dragging from Finder or the sidebar has not been tried.
- Apple Pencil on PDFs. UIKit cannot tell Pencil from finger when choosing which view receives a touch, so while annotating every touch on a page goes to its canvas, which draws only with Pencil unless Draw with Finger is on.
- PDF page duplicate, move, drag reordering, multiple selection, page import, recoloring markup, PDFs with an existing table of contents, the dialog that warns about dropped document settings, embed zoom, Open in PDF View, and releasing an embed after scrolling away.
- Export a Copy (share sheet) from the drawing editor.
- Double-tap to zoom images (the simulator tool cannot send a real double tap), and editing property values by typing (changes are saved when the field loses focus).
- PNG drawing insertion of a new drawing through the UI (the file format is covered by tests; a PNG drawn on an image was saved through the UI on 2026-09-30).
- Transparent drawing backgrounds in other applications.
- Attachment import through the Insert menu with the new placement rules.
- Recording after the audio-session fix (needs a microphone). Also compiled for iOS and not device-tested: a recording that could not be saved blocks a new recording until it is saved with Try Saving Again (which can target a newly computed destination) or discarded; finalization publishes anyway after 30 s if the recorder never reports finishing; an iOS media-services reset or an encoding error stops the recorder and keeps the audio for retry; interruption, route-change and media-reset handling hops to the main actor. The controller's state rules are unit-tested on macOS (`AppleRecordingAccessRecordingTests`).
- Drawing drafts after the system ends the app, and the PDF and SVG buttons of the alert for an oversized PNG: unit-tested only up to the draft store (`UiRootSettingsTests`); they need a device check. The drawing editor's undo history is its own since 2026-09-30 and is tested with hosted views (`PencilToolsTests`); in the running app its Undo was seen to follow strokes and a lasso move, but was not pressed.
- Other view and UIKit code changed by the 2026-09-25 bug-fix merge that no test exercises, verified only by building for iOS (and for macOS where it is not UIKit-only): in Live Preview, the pool of off-screen rendered blocks and its memory-warning release, the find interaction turned off when the editor leaves the window, jumps waiting for the window, inline math drawn once per formula and its image cache, quote bars on every line of a wrapped quote, pasted HTML converted and pasted images encoded off the main thread, suggestions not updated while an edit is being applied, the block checkpoints the styler resumes from, and `tabSize` in the iPad editor's Return handling; in PDFs, the eraser mask clipping the ink's appearance stream (`PDFPencilInk`), a second eraser cut converted again, the canvases deferring their ink re-editing records (the session writing them before saves is tested on macOS), stored ink decoded in the background, the save when the app goes to the background, scrolling held while an embed annotates, the markup tap, and the lock notice in protected embeds; drawings inserted after the selection, kept when their note is in conflict, and placed where the note's text moved; drops onto file rows (moving the items into that file's folder) and the sidebar's drop outline; the unplayable-media notice and the players' time updates in reading view.
- Search operators `task:` and `[property:null]` now have unit and index-integration regressions (`CoreSearchQueryFixTests`, `SearchSyntaxTests`). Physical interaction with search and compatibility outside those fixtures remain to be checked. Nested `tag:` matching has separate unit coverage.
- Obsidian's `smartIndentList`, `autoPairMarkdown`, `useTab` and `autoConvertHtml` read from `.obsidian/app.json`: implemented; no test reads or writes those keys (`autoPairBrackets` and `tabSize` are covered above).
- Everything that needs a physical iPad: Apple Pencil input and latency, `PKToolPicker` behavior with a Pencil, memory of large PNG exports, iCloud and third-party file providers (including safe writes staged in the volume's item-replacement folder).
- Vaults on a device: bookmarks surviving an app update, vaults in iCloud Drive (including Obsidian's folder), and Create New Vault inside a folder chosen with Choose Folder… (the simulator run used Graphite's own folder). On the Mac, creating a vault needs a chosen folder and was not tried.

## Not implemented yet

Obsidian community plugins run and install (see the 2026-10-10 section), but not yet everything plugins do: CodeMirror editor extensions, plugins' code blocks and post-processors in the reading view, typing suggestions, live editor events, context menu items, hover previews, and plugin views as tabs or panels are missing, and none of it has run in WebKit or on a device yet. `Docs/Community-plugins.md` keeps the list.

A full comparison with Obsidian mobile, ranked for studying on an iPad, is in `Docs/Obsidian-mobile-gaps.md`. The defects it found in existing features are fixed (see above). The main remaining items:

- Bases Kanban settings (column width, hiding empty columns, cover images, column order, a new note from a column) and moving cards between folder columns: Obsidian 1.14's keys for them are not documented. Map styles (vector tiles) in `mapTiles` show Apple's map with a notice. `html()` shows no images or frames.
- Choosing a different format for an existing drawing (use Export a Copy).
- Native text selection handles, magnifier, Look Up and Translate on PDFs while annotating: a long press selects a word and dragging extends it, Graphite's own implementation. Outside annotation, PDFKit's native selection is used.
- Undo for PDF bookmark changes (toggle the bookmark again). A PDF's undo history ends when its hidden session is released or the file reopens after an external change, and is not kept across launches.
- Embeds of one PDF now coordinate: before an embed annotates, the other embeds and a PDF view showing the file are saved. The PDF view does not do the same before its own edits, so editing a PDF in its view while an embed of it has unsaved annotations can still end in a conflict.
- Filling PDF forms: form fields are shown read-only.
- Video keeps no sound while Graphite is off screen, and pauses while Graphite shares the screen with another app, which needs Apple's multitasking-camera entitlement. One fixed video quality. A player already showing an audio or video file does not reload after a conflict version replaces it.
- Hidden tabs keep their note sessions open; only hidden PDFs beyond the most recently visible one are released. Note editors are bounded (three kept hidden), sessions are not.
- Rendering SVG files made by other applications inside the reading view (they open with the system preview instead).
- Tabs: undo history survives switching tabs, moving a tab to the other side, and reading view for the three most recently hidden notes; beyond them, after a memory warning, and when a tab opens another file, the editor is rebuilt without its history (the note's text, cursor and scroll position are kept); a file is open in at most one tab, so "Open in New Tab" on a file already open shows its tab; tab history is not saved between launches; tabs stack at most two sides, left and right; no saved named workspaces. Reading view now retains its vertical offset for the open session, with the limitations recorded above.
- Opening a second vault in its own window, as Obsidian does on the desktop; one vault is open at a time.
- Bases: a date Range and durations are shown as exact parts, where Obsidian humanizes them ("a month"); `date("not a date")` and `duration("soon")` are errors, where Obsidian gives an empty value.
- Renaming or deleting a vault's folder from Graphite (do it in Files, then open the folder again).
- Per-vault Graphite preferences: which core plugins are on, the editor defaults and the Colors palette apply to every vault on the device, where Obsidian keeps core plugin choices per vault.
- 10,000 and 100,000 file measurements on hardware.
